Hisaab — square vs absolute, chaar ghaltiyon par

r p_sqr = r^2 p_abs = |r| kaun bara? ------ --------------- -------------- --------------------- 0.1 0.1^2 = 0.01 |0.1| = 0.1 absolute 10 guna bara 0.5 0.5^2 = 0.25 |0.5| = 0.5 absolute 2 guna bara 1.0 1.0^2 = 1.00 |1.0| = 1.0 BARABAR (crossover) 3.0 3.0^2 = 9.00 |3.0| = 3.0 square 3 guna bara 10.0 10.0^2 = 100.0 |10.0| = 10.0 square 10 guna bara

Matlab kya hai: square loss chhoti ghaltiyon ko taqreeban maaf kar deta hai (0.01 kuch bhi nahi) magar badi ghaltiyon par toot padta hai (100!). Is liye square loss wala model har point ko thoda thoda theek karne ki koshish chhod deta hai aur apni poori taqat sab se bure point par laga deta hai — aur agar wo bura point ek outlier hai, to aapka model us ghalat point ke peeche bhaag raha hai.

E2Symmetric vs asymmetric penalty

Slides do saaf khaane banate hain:

  • Symmetric penalty: p(−r) = p(r). Yaani hamein sirf ghalti ki jasaamat (magnitude) ki fikr hai, uski taraf ki nahi. Square aur absolute — dono symmetric hain.
  • Asymmetric penalty: p(−r) ≠ p(r). Yaani ek taraf ki ghalti hamein zyada chubhti hai. Tilted absolute isi ki misaal hai.

Asymmetric penalty ki asal duniya ki misaalein (exam mein ek do likhein, ye "design capability" dikhata hai):

Soorat-e-haalKaun si ghalti buriChunaav
Food delivery ka waqtKam batana (late ho jaayega)τ > 1/2
Hospital mein bistaron ki zarooratKam batana (patient ke liye bistar nahi)τ > 1/2
Project ka budgetKam batana (paisa khatam ho jaayega)τ > 1/2
Dawa ki khuraakZyada batana (overdose — jaan ka khatra)τ < 1/2
Pul par wazan ki hadZyada batana (pul toot jaayega)τ < 1/2

E3Residual histogram — loss ka chunaav shakal badal deta hai

Slide 33 ek gehri baat kehti hai: "choice of penalty function shapes the histogram of prediction errors". Yaani aap loss chun kar sirf ye nahi keh rahe "ghalti kam karo" — aap ye keh rahe hain ke ghaltiyon ki poori taqseem (distribution) kis shakal ki honi chahiye.

Histogram kya hai: saare n residuals r1, …, rn nikaalein, unko khaano (bins) mein baant dein, aur bar graph bana dein. Isi se nazar aata hai ke ghaltiyan kahan jama hain.

Slides ka experiment (slide 34): n = 300, m = 1, d = 31, 50/50 train/test split, pehla feature constant. Nateeja:

  • Square loss → histogram 0 ke aas paas symmetric, narm ghanti jaisa, magar dono taraf lambi dumein (kuch bade residuals).
  • Absolute loss → 0 par zyada ghana jhund (bohat se residuals taqreeban sifar), magar kuch bade residuals bardasht kar liye jaate hain.
  • Tilted loss → slides ka khaas jumla: "tilted loss results in distribution with most residuals ri < 0, i.e. predictor prefers ŷi < yi". Yaani histogram ek taraf khisak jaata hai — jaan boojh kar.

Is aakhri nuqte ko samajh lein (ye τ < 1/2 ka case hai): jab over-estimate mehnga ho, to model apni saari predictions thodi neeche rakhta hai. Natija: zyadatar residuals negative. Model jaan boojh kar ek taraf jhuk gaya, kyunki hum ne usay kaha tha. Yehi quantile regression ka maqsad hai.

Diagnostic hunar — residual histogram padhna

Agar exam mein residual histogram ya pattern diya jaaye, to ye padhein: 0 ke around symmetric aur random = model theek hai. Ek taraf khisak gaya (biased) = ya asymmetric loss istemaal hua, ya offset (θ1) missing hai. Residuals mein saaf pattern (jaise bade x par hamesha bade residual) = underfitting / missing features, dekhein B7. Dono taraf bohat lambi dumein = outliers mojood hain, robust loss chahiye.

E4Outliers — kya hain, kahan se aate hain, nuqsan kya

Slides ki tareef: "in some applications, a few data points are 'way off', or just 'wrong'. These points are called outliers."

Kahan se aate hain (slides ki wajuhat + OHT ki):

  • Transcription errors — data likhte waqt ghalti.
  • Decimal point ki ghalat jagah — 12.5 ki jagah 125. Ye 10 guna ghalti hai, aur square loss ke liye 100 guna.
  • Sensor ki kharabi — OHT Q-6 mein GPS ka "sensor glitch", Q-9 mein "electromagnetic interference".

Ahem baat: outlier ka matlab "bari value" nahi hai. Outlier wo point hai jo us process se nahi aaya jisay hum model kar rahe hain. Ek 10 crore ka ghar outlier nahi hai agar wo waqai Clifton ka mahal hai — wo asli data hai. Magar ek 1200 sq ft ke ghar ki keemat 10 crore likhi ho (decimal ki ghalti se), to wo outlier hai.

Nuqsan: slides kehte hain "even a few outliers in a data set can result in ERM picking a poor predictor." Chalein naapein ke kitna nuqsan — ye exact hisaab exam mein likhne par poore marks deta hai:

Hisaab — ek outlier poori line ko kitna kheenchta hai

8 "sahi" points lein, jo taqreeban ek line par hain:

u : 0.08 0.20 0.32 0.44 0.56 0.68 0.80 0.92 y : 0.14 0.24 0.33 0.45 0.52 0.64 0.72 0.84 u ka mean = (0.08+0.20+0.32+0.44+0.56+0.68+0.80+0.92) / 8 = 4.00 / 8 = 0.50 y ka mean = (0.14+0.24+0.33+0.45+0.52+0.64+0.72+0.84) / 8 = 3.88 / 8 = 0.485

Ab ek outlier daalein u = 0.50, y = 0.97 par (sensor glitch). Is jagah line ko taqreeban 0.485 hona chahiye tha, to ghalti taqreeban 0.49 hai.

Khaas baat: outlier theek u ke mean (0.50) par hai. Least squares ki line HAMESHA centroid (u ka mean, y ka mean) se guzarti hai. Aur u = u ka mean par point daalne se SLOPE bilkul nahi badalta (kyunki slope ke formula mein (u_i - u ka mean) aata hai, jo yahan 0 hai). To sirf y ka mean khisakta hai — yaani poori line OOPAR uthti hai: naya y ka mean = ( 3.88 + 0.97 ) / 9 [ab 9 points hain] = 4.85 / 9 = 0.53889 Line kitni oopar uthi? 0.53889 - 0.485 = 0.05389 (naya u ka mean = (4.00 + 0.50)/9 = 4.50/9 = 0.50 — bilkul wohi, confirm ✓)

Nateeja: ek ghalat point ne saari 8 sahi predictions ko 0.0539 oopar khisaka diya. Aur ghaur karein ye kitna hai — outlier ki ghalti 0.49 thi, aur line uthi 0.49 / 9 = 0.0544 (taqreeban wohi). Yaani square loss outlier ki ghalti ka 1/n hissa seedha saare jawabon par taqseem kar deta hai. Aur agar outlier 10 guna bura hota (decimal ki ghalti), to line 10 guna zyada uthti.

Slides ek saada tareeqa bhi dete hain outliers hatane ka (ye bhi exam mein likh sakte hain):

  1. Poore data se predictor banayein.
  2. Jin points ki prediction error bohat badi hai, unko outlier ka nishaan laga dein.
  3. Unko data se nikaal dein, aur dobara shuru karein (repeat).

Aur slides ye bhi kehte hain: "it's also possible to use a penalty function that is less sensitive to outlier data points" — aur yehi agla topic hai, aur yehi behtar raasta hai (kyunki points phenkne mein khatra hai ke aap asli data phenk dein).

E5Robust penalty ki theek tareef

Slides ki tareef lafz ba lafz, kyunki exam mein yehi maangi jaati hai:

Definition — yaad kar lein

"We say a penalty function is robust if it has low sensitivity to outliers. Robust penalty functions grow more slowly for large prediction error values than the square penalty, and so allow the predictor to have a few large prediction errors (presumably for the outliers), so they handle outliers more gracefully."

Aur: "ERM with Huber loss is called a robust prediction method."

Lafz "allow" (ijaazat dena) is tareef ka dil hai. Robust loss outlier ko theek nahi karta — wo us par haath khada kar deta hai. Wo kehta hai: "theek hai, is ek point par meri prediction bohat ghalat hogi, magar is ki qeemat mehdood hai — main baqi 98% par dhyan de raha hoon." Ye model ki harkat nahi, hamara faisla hai, aur wo faisla hum penalty function chun kar sunate hain.

Aur asal mechanism derivative mein hai, value mein nahi. Ye nukta likhne par poore marks milte hain:

ERM gradient descent se chalta hai, aur gradient mein har point ka hissa uske penalty ke DERIVATIVE ke mutabiq hota hai. To: Square penalty : p(r) = r^2 -> p'(r) = 2r derivative r ke saath BARHTA JAATA HAI, bay-had => jitna door outlier, utna ZYADA zor se kheenchta hai Robust penalty : derivative BARA NAHI HOTA (ya ghatta hai) => outlier ka zor MEHDOOD (bounded) reh jaata hai

E6Huber loss — OHT Q-6 ka jawab

Sab se mash-hoor robust penalty. Ye do penalties ko jod kar banta hai: chhoti ghalti par square jaisa, badi ghalti par absolute jaisa.

/ r^2 agar |r| <= alpha (quadratic) p_hub(r) = < \ alpha * ( 2|r| - alpha ) agar |r| > alpha (affine / seedhi line) alpha > 0 ek parameter hai — yehi tay karta hai ke "normal ghalti" kahan khatam hoti hai aur "outlier" kahan shuru hota hai.

Pehle tasalli kar lein ke ye formula joda theek se laga hai (ye derivation exam mein poochi ja sakti hai):

Hisaab — Huber r = ±α par smooth kyun hai

(1) VALUE match karti hai? r = alpha par dono formule nikaalein: Quadratic hissa : r^2 = alpha^2 Affine hissa : alpha ( 2*alpha - alpha ) = alpha ( alpha ) = alpha^2 Dono alpha^2 => curve mein koi chhalaang nahi (continuous) ✓ (2) DHALAAN match karti hai? r = alpha par derivative nikaalein: Quadratic hissa : d/dr [ r^2 ] = 2r = 2*alpha Affine hissa : d/dr [ alpha(2r - alpha) ] = 2*alpha Dono 2*alpha => koi nukeela mod nahi (smooth, C1) ✓ Yehi Huber ka kamaal hai: alpha par dono tukde VALUE aur DHALAAN dono mein mil jaate hain. Is liye wo convex bhi hai aur smooth bhi.

Ab asal cheez — outlier ka zor kitna kam ho jaata hai:

Hisaab — square vs Huber (α = 1 ke saath)

r p_sqr = r^2 p_hub = 1*(2|r| - 1) square / Huber ----- ------------- ---------------------- ---------------- 0.5 0.25 0.25 (quadratic area) 1.0 x 1.0 1.00 1.00 (crossover) 1.0 x 3.0 9.00 2*3 - 1 = 5.0 1.8 x 10.0 100.00 2*10 - 1 = 19.0 5.3 x 50.0 2500.00 2*50 - 1 = 99.0 25.3 x Aur ab wo cheez jo asal mein ahem hai — DERIVATIVE (gradient ka zor): r p'_sqr = 2r p'_hub = 2*alpha = 2 zor ka farq ----- ------------- --------------------- -------------- 0.5 1.0 1.0 barabar 1.0 2.0 2.0 barabar 3.0 6.0 2.0 3 guna kam 10.0 20.0 2.0 10 guna kam 50.0 100.0 2.0 50 guna kam

Yehi poora jawab hai. Square loss mein outlier ka zor 2r hai — jitna door jaayega, utna zyada kheenchega, bay-had. Huber mein zor α ke baad 2α par ruk jaata hai (saturate). Chahe outlier 10 door ho ya 10,000 — wo utna hi zor lagayega jitna ek point jo bas α par hai. Outlier ka asar bounded ho gaya. Yehi "robustness" ka asal mechanism hai, aur yehi exam mein likhna hai.

α kaise chunein (ye poocha ja sakta hai): α ko asli shor (genuine noise) ke paimane ke barabar rakhein. Agar α bohat bara ho, to taqreeban saare points quadratic hisse mein aa jaayenge aur Huber square loss ban jaayega (robustness khatam). Agar α bohat chhota ho, to taqreeban saare points affine hisse mein aa jaayenge aur Huber absolute loss ban jaayega. Darmiyani α chahiye — aur wo validation se chunein. Ek aam amali tareeqa: α ko residuals ke robust phailaav (jaise MAD — median absolute deviation) ka 1–2 guna rakhein.

E7Log-Huber — aur zyada robust (OHT Q-9 ka jawab)

Huber badi ghalti par linearly barhta hai. Log-Huber aur aage jaata hai: badi ghalti par logarithmically barhta hai — jo linear se bhi bohat sust hai.

/ r^2 agar |r| <= alpha p_dh(r) = < \ alpha^2 ( 1 - 2 log(alpha) + log( r^2 ) ) agar |r| > alpha Slides: "quadratic for small r, logarithmic for large r — diminishing incremental penalty at large r" "even less sensitive to outliers than Huber"

Lafz "diminishing incremental penalty" ka matlab: jitni door ghalti jaayegi, utna kam extra jurmana lagega. Numbers se dekhein:

Hisaab — teeno penalties ek saath (α = 1)

α = 1 rakhne se formula aasaan ho jaata hai, kyunki log(1) = 0:

p_dh(r) = 1 * ( 1 - 2*log(1) + log(r^2) ) = 1 - 0 + 2 log|r| = 1 + 2 log|r| [|r| > 1 ke liye] Pehle tasdeeq: r = 1 par => 1 + 2 log(1) = 1 + 0 = 1 ✓ quadratic hissa bhi r=1 par 1^2 = 1 ✓ (continuous)
r SQUARE HUBER LOG-HUBER r^2 2|r| - 1 1 + 2 ln|r| ----- -------- ----------- ------------------ 1.0 1.00 1.00 1 + 2(0) = 1.000 3.0 9.00 5.00 1 + 2(1.0986) = 3.197 10.0 100.00 19.00 1 + 2(2.3026) = 5.605 100.0 10000.00 199.00 1 + 2(4.6052) = 10.210 Aur DERIVATIVE (asal mechanism): r p'_sqr p'_hub p'_dh = 2/r ----- --------- -------- ------------- 1.0 2.0 2.0 2/1 = 2.00 3.0 6.0 2.0 2/3 = 0.67 10.0 20.0 2.0 2/10 = 0.20 100.0 200.0 2.0 2/100 = 0.02

Teen alag rawaiye, ek nazar mein: square ka zor barhta jaata hai (2 → 200), Huber ka zor ruk jaata hai (hamesha 2), aur log-Huber ka zor ghatta jaata hai (2 → 0.02). Yaani log-Huber kehta hai: "ye point itna door hai ke ye yaqeenan kharab data hai — main isay taqreeban bilkul nazar-andaaz kar deta hoon." Sab se zyada robust.

Log-Huber ki qeemat — ye likhne par examiner khush hoga

Log-Huber zyada robust hai, magar wo convex nahi hai. Saboot: |r| > α par p′(r) = 2α²/r, jo r barhne par ghatta hai — yaani p″(r) < 0, jo D5 ki convexity ki shart (p″ ≥ 0) tordta hai. Nateeja: ERM mein kai local minima ho sakte hain, jawab shuruati guess par depend kar sakta hai, aur global optimum ki zamanat nahi rehti. Huber convex hai, is liye wo pehla chunaav hona chahiye. Log-Huber tab uthayein jab outliers bohat hi shadeed hon aur Huber kaam na kare. Amali tareeqa: pehle Huber se hal karein, phir usi jawab ko log-Huber ka starting point bana dein.

E8Saari penalties ka comparison — ek hi table

Ye table exam se pehle aakhri 10 minute mein dekhein. Sab kuch yahan hai.

PenaltyFormulaBadi ghalti par barhta haiZor (derivative)Convex?Constant predictorKab use karein
Squarer²Quadratically (sab se tez)2r — bay-had barhta haiHaanMeanSaaf data, outliers na hon; aasaan closed-form hal
Absolute|r|Linearly±1 — constantHaanMedianOutliers hon; robust aur saada
Tilted absolute−τr (r<0)
(1−τ)r (r≥0)		Linearly, magar asymmetric−τ ya 1−τHaanτ-quantileEk taraf ki ghalti mehngi (late delivery, budget)
Huberr² agar |r|≤α
α(2|r|−α) warna		Quadratic → phir linear2r → phir 2α par ruk jaata haiHaan—Robust fitting ka default chunaav. Outliers hon magar optimization bhi bharosay ke qabil chahiye
Log-Huberr² agar |r|≤α
α²(1−2logα+log r²) warna		Quadratic → phir logarithmic (sab se sust)2r → phir 2α²/r — ghatta haiNAHI—Bohat shadeed outliers; local minima ka khatra qubool ho
Fractionalmax(ŷ/y−1, y/ŷ−1)Nisbat (ratio) ke hisaab se—HaanFractional middlePercentage ghalti ahem ho (keematein, income, sales)

Aur Lecture-4 ka apna khulasa slide (slide 43) bhi yaad rakhein — wo poore lecture ko chaar line mein samet deta hai:

  • Loss function ko aksar residual r = ŷ − y ke penalty function ki shakal mein likha jaata hai.
  • Loss function ye bayaan karta hai ke hum mukhtalif residuals par kitna aitraaz karte hain.
  • Mukhtalif loss function chunne se mukhtalif ERM predictors nikalte hain.
  • Khaas istemaal: robust fitting (outliers wale data par fit karna) aur quantile regression (ek muqarrar fraction over-estimation ke saath fit karna).
Part F — Lecture 5

Regularization aur Non-Quadratic Regularizers

Lecture-4 ne kaha "data ko theek fit karo". Lecture-5 kehta hai "magar bohat zor na lagao". OHT Q-7 seedha isi se hai, aur ye poora lecture sirf ek sawal ka jawab hai: theta ko chhota rakhna kyun generalization behtar karta hai?

F1Sensitivity — insensitive predictor acha kyun hai

Lecture-5 ek nayi khwahish se shuru hota hai. Ab tak hum sirf ye chahte the ke ℒ(θ) chhota ho. Ab hum ek doosri cheez bhi chahte hain:

Lecture-5 ka buniyadi usool

"We want to choose θ to achieve low empirical risk ℒ(θ), but also, we'd like the predictor to not be too sensitive."

Roughly: "for x′ near x, gθ(x′) should be near gθ(x)." Aur phir faisla-kun do line:

  • sensitive predictors sometimes don't generalize well
  • insensitive predictors often generalize well

Sensitivity ka matlab Roman Urdu mein: "chidchida-pan". Ek sensitive predictor wo hai jiska input zara sa badlein aur uska jawab bohat badal jaaye. Ek insensitive (narm-mizaj) predictor wo hai jo thodi tabdeeli par thoda hi badalta hai.

Aur ye generalization se kaise jura hai? Yahan asal daleel hai (ye samajh lein, ratti na maarein):

  1. Asal duniya ka data shor (noise) ke saath aata hai. Jo x aap ko mila, wo bilkul theek x nahi hai — sensor mein ghalti hai, measurement mein ghalti hai, zamana badal gaya hai.
  2. Agar aap ka predictor sensitive hai, to x mein wo zara si ghalti prediction mein badi ghalti ban jaayegi.
  3. Agar aap ka predictor insensitive hai, to wohi zara si ghalti prediction mein zara si ghalti rahegi.
  4. Aur unseen data mein hamesha aisi zara si tabdeeliyan hoti hain. Is liye insensitive predictor naye data par behtar chalta hai.

Ab 1-NN ko is nazar se dekhein: uski prediction ek step function hai. x ko zara sa khisakayein aur agar aap boundary cross kar gaye, to jawab ek dam doosre point ka label ban jaata hai — poori chhalaang. Ye duniya ka sab se sensitive predictor hai. Aur isi liye wo generalize nahi karta. Dekha — Lecture 5 ne Quiz 1 ke jawab ko ek nayi, gehri zubaan de di.

Aur θ ka kya ta'alluq? Slides kehte hain: "often predictor sensitivity corresponds to the size of θ". Yaani bade θ = sensitive predictor. Seedha dekhein: ŷ = θTx, to agar θj = 1000 hai, to xj mein 0.01 ki tabdeeli prediction mein 10 ki tabdeeli banayegi. Agar θj = 0.5 hai, to wohi tabdeeli sirf 0.005 banayegi. Theta chhota rakho, predictor narm-mizaj ho jaayega. Yehi poore Lecture-5 ka khulasa hai.

F2Regularizer r(θ) — aur uski do tafseerein

Slides ki tareef: "a regularizer r : ℝd → ℝ is a function that measures the sensitivity of θ". Yaani ek function jo θ leta hai aur ek number deta hai: "ye θ kitna chidchida predictor banayega".

Slides do mukhtalif tafseerein dete hain (dono likhein, dono qubool hain):

  1. Sensitivity ka paimana. r(θ) naapta hai ke predictor kitna sensitive hai. Hum sensitivity nahi chahte, is liye hum r(θ) chhota chahte hain.
  2. Prior information. Slides: "the regularizer encodes prior information we have about θ — specifically, that θ is small." Yaani data dekhne se pehle hi hamein ek yaqeen tha ke θ chhota hona chahiye, aur regularizer wo yaqeen model mein daal deta hai.

Doosri tafseer gehri hai. Iska matlab hai ke hum model ko sirf data se nahi sikha rahe — hum apni aqal (domain knowledge) bhi daal rahe hain. Aur ye bohat kaam aata hai jab data kam ho: jab 50 points aur 200 features hain, to data akela faisla nahi kar sakta, aur hamara "theta chhota hona chahiye" wala yaqeen faisla kar deta hai.

Slides ka khulasa: "with either interpretation, we want both ℒ(θ) and r(θ) small."

F3RERM aur λ — do khwahishon ka sauda

Masla ye hai: hum do cheezein chahte hain, aur wo aam tor par ek doosre ke khilaaf hoti hain. ℒ(θ) bilkul chhota karne ke liye θ bara karna padta hai (data ko hubahu fit karne ke liye), aur r(θ) chhota karne ke liye θ chhota karna padta hai (jo data ko theek fit nahi karta).

Hal: dono ko jama kar dein, aur ek knob laga dein jo faisla kare ke kis ko kitni ahmiyat deni hai. Isi ko RERM — Regularized Empirical Risk Minimization kehte hain:

RERM: theta chuno jo ye minimize kare: L(theta) + lambda * r(theta) ^^^^^^^^ ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^ "data theek "theta chhota fit karo" rakho" lambda > 0 = REGULARIZATION HYPER-PARAMETER

Slides: "λ > 0 is the regularization hyper-parameter, used to trade off ℒ(θ) and r(θ)."

λ ko knob ki tarah samjhein — ye seedha A13 ke U-curve se jura hua hai:

λKis ki jeetKya hota haiHaalat
λ = 0Sirf ℒRegularization bilkul nahi — saada ERM. θ jitna bara chahe ho jaayeOverfit
Chhota λZyadatar ℒHalki si rokThoda overfit
Theek λBarabarData bhi fit, θ bhi qaabu meinBehtareen test error
Bara λZyadatar rθ bohat dab gaya, model kamzorThoda underfit
λ → ∞Sirf rθ → 0 — model kuch nahi bolta (sirf offset)Underfit

Aur λ kahan se aata hai? Ye bohat ahem hai: λ training se nahi seekha ja sakta! Agar aap training data par λ optimize karein, to jawab hamesha λ = 0 aayega (kyunki λ = 0 training error ko sab se kam karta hai). Is liye slides kehte hain: "we choose λ (and r) by validation on a test set" aur "we use a regularizer to achieve better test set performance."

Parameter vs hyper-parameter — farq pakka kar lein

Parameter (θ) — training data se ERM/RERM se seekha jaata hai. Hyper-parameter (λ, α, k, β, τ, polynomial degree) — validation data se chuna jaata hai, training se nahi. Agar exam mein poochein "how would you choose λ", to jawab hamesha "by validation / cross-validation on held-out data" hai — kabhi "training error minimize kar ke" nahi.

Penalty-based regularizers. Slides kehte hain ke aam regularizers ek penalty function φ se bante hain, jo har coefficient par alag se lagti hai aur phir jama ho jaati hai:

r(theta) = phi(theta_1) + phi(theta_2) + ... + phi(theta_d) Usool: phi(z) >= 0 har z ke liye phi(0) = 0 Matlab: "phi(z) bayaan karta hai ke hum theta ka coefficient z chunne par kitna na-khush hain." TEEN AAM MISAALEIN: 1) SUM SQUARE (dusre naam: quadratic, Tychonov, L2, RIDGE regularizer) phi_sqr(z) = z^2 to r(theta) = || theta ||^2 2 = theta_1^2 + ... + theta_d^2 2) SUM ABSOLUTE (dusre naam: L1, LASSO regularizer) phi_abs(z) = |z| to r(theta) = || theta || 1 = |theta_1| + ... + |theta_d| 3) NONNEGATIVE regularizer / 0 agar z >= 0 phi_nn(z) = < \ infinity agar z < 0 (yaani: coefficients ko MAJBOOR karta hai ke nonnegative hon)

Teesre ko dhyan se dekhein — ye "jurmana" nahi, pabandi (constraint) hai. ∞ ka matlab "ye bilkul mana hai". Ye ek chalaak tarkeeb hai: pabandi ko penalty ki shakal mein likh dena.

F4Feature perturbation aur worst-case sensitivity

Ab Lecture-5 ka sab se khoobsurat hissa. Ab tak hum ne kaha "sensitivity bura hai, theta chhota rakho". Magar ye dhundhli baat hai. Lecture-5 isay theek theek naapta hai — aur phir us naap se ridge aur lasso khud nikal aate hain. Ye ratti ki cheez nahi, samajhne ki cheez hai.

Kahani ye hai: hamara linear predictor gθ(x) = θTx hai. Ab farz karein feature vector thoda bigad gaya — sensor mein shor aa gaya, ya measurement badal gayi:

x_naya = x + delta delta = "perturbation" (bigaad / tabdeeli) Prediction kitni badli? g(x + delta) - g(x) = theta^T (x + delta) - theta^T x = theta^T x + theta^T delta - theta^T x = theta^T delta <== bas yehi tabdeeli hai Note: x bilkul GHAYAB ho gaya! Tabdeeli sirf theta aur delta par depend karti hai.

Ab sawal: ye tabdeeli kitni se kitni ho sakti hai? Yahan ek chalaki hai — hum ye nahi maante ke δ kya hoga. Hum sab se bure soorat (worst case) ka hisaab lagate hain:

Pehle tay karein ke delta kis had tak ja sakta hai. Us set ko D kehte hain — "feature perturbation set". Slides: "we'll assume that any delta in D is possible." Phir WORST CASE SENSITIVITY ye hai: worst-case sensitivity = max | theta^T delta | delta in D Yaani: "D ke andar se wo delta chuno jo prediction ko SAB SE ZYADA bigade, aur dekho wo kitna bigad sakti hai."

Ab asal nukta. Jawab is baat par depend karta hai ke aap ne D kis shakal ka chuna. Aur yehi se do mukhtalif regularizer nikalte hain. Ye course ka sab se sundar nateeja hai:

Aap ka yaqeen (perturbation set D)Worst-case sensitivityIs se nikalne wala regularizer
‖δ‖2 ≤ ρ
"kul milakar tabdeeli mehdood hai" (L2-ball)		ρ ‖θ‖2Ridge / sum-square (L2)
|δj| ≤ ρ har j ke liye
"har feature alag alag thoda badal sakta hai" (L-infinity ball)		ρ ‖θ‖1Lasso / sum-absolute (L1)

Yaani: ridge aur lasso aasman se nahi tapke. Dono ek hi sawal ka jawab hain, bas "feature kaise bigad sakta hai" ka farz (assumption) alag hai. Agle do topics mein dono derivation hain.

F5Ridge kahan se aaya — L2 perturbation aur Cauchy-Schwarz

Pehla farz: perturbation ek L2-ball mein hai (ek gol gaind):

D = { delta : || delta || <= rho } ise "L2-ball" kehte hain 2 Matlab: feature vector x kisi bhi aisi jagah ja sakta hai jo x se L2 (seedhi, Pythagoras wali) distance rho ke andar ho.

Ab Cauchy-Schwarz inequality lagayein. Ye ek buniyadi riyaazi haqeeqat hai, aur iska matlab bilkul saada hai:

CAUCHY-SCHWARZ: | theta^T delta | <= || theta || * || delta || 2 2 Lafzon mein: "do vectors ka dot product un ki lambaiyon ke zarb se zyada nahi ho sakta." (Barabar sirf tab hota hai jab dono vectors EK HI DIRECTION mein hon.)

Derivation — qadam ba qadam

QADAM 1. Cauchy-Schwarz lagayein: | theta^T delta | <= || theta ||_2 * || delta ||_2 QADAM 2. Aur hamein pata hai ke || delta ||_2 <= rho (D ki tareef se): | theta^T delta | <= || theta ||_2 * rho => tabdeeli KABHI BHI rho * ||theta||_2 se zyada nahi ho sakti. (ye "upper bound" hai) QADAM 3. Magar kya ye had WAQAI pohonchti hai? Haan — slides ye delta dete hain: theta delta = rho * -------------- || theta ||_2 Tasdeeq (a): kya ye delta D ke andar hai? || delta ||_2 = rho * || theta ||_2 / || theta ||_2 = rho ✓ (had par hai) Tasdeeq (b): is delta se tabdeeli kitni hoti hai? theta^T delta = theta^T * rho * theta / ||theta||_2 = rho * ( theta^T theta ) / ||theta||_2 = rho * || theta ||_2 ^2 / ||theta||_2 = rho * || theta ||_2 ✓ QADAM 4. To upper bound WAQAI haasil ho jaati hai. Yaani: WORST-CASE SENSITIVITY = rho * || theta ||_2

Nateeja: Agar aap worst-case sensitivity kam karna chahte hain, to aap ko ‖θ‖2 kam karna hoga. ρ aap ke haath mein nahi (wo duniya ka shor hai), ‖θ‖2 aap ke haath mein hai.

Slides ka jumla: "justifies sum square regularizer" — aur formula:

r(theta) = || theta ||^2 = theta_1^2 + theta_2^2 + ... + theta_d^2 2 (square liya jaata hai kyunki wo smooth hai aur derivative lena aasaan ho jaata hai — minimize karne ka asar wohi rehta hai)

F6Lasso kahan se aaya — L-infinity perturbation

Ab doosra farz, aur ye asal duniya mein aksar zyada munasib hota hai: har feature alag alag, apni had tak badal sakta hai.

D = { delta : | delta_j | <= rho har j = 1 ... d ke liye } Isay "L-infinity ball" kehte hain, kyunki: || delta || = max | delta_j | <-- L-infinity norm ki tareef inf j=1..d to shart || delta ||_inf <= rho bilkul wohi baat hai. Matlab: "feature vector ka KOI BHI component rho tak oopar ya neeche ja sakta hai — aur sab EK SAATH ja sakte hain."

Ye farz zyada haqeeqi kab hota hai? Jab har feature ka apna alag sensor ho. Ghar ke data mein: area tape se naapa gaya (apni ghalti), age record se aaya (apni ghalti), distance GPS se (apni ghalti). Ye ghaltiyan ek doosre se juri nahi hain, aur sab ek saath apni maximum had par ho sakti hain. L2-ball ye farz karta hai ke kul ghalti mehdood hai (yaani agar ek feature bohat bigda to doosre kam bigdenge) — jo aksar sach nahi hota.

Derivation — qadam ba qadam

QADAM 1. Tabdeeli ko kholein: theta^T delta = theta_1 delta_1 + theta_2 delta_2 + ... + theta_d delta_d QADAM 2. Hum isay SAB SE BARA karna chahte hain, aur har delta_j ko -rho se +rho tak chun sakte hain. Har term ko alag dekhein: Agar theta_j POSITIVE hai -> delta_j = +rho rakho -> term = +rho * theta_j (positive) Agar theta_j NEGATIVE hai -> delta_j = -rho rakho -> term = -rho * theta_j (bhi positive!) Dono soorton mein term = rho * | theta_j | Yaani behtareen chunaav: delta = rho * sign(theta) QADAM 3. Saare terms jama karein: theta^T delta = rho |theta_1| + rho |theta_2| + ... + rho |theta_d| = rho ( |theta_1| + |theta_2| + ... + |theta_d| ) = rho * || theta || 1 QADAM 4. To: WORST-CASE SENSITIVITY = rho * || theta ||_1

Slides ka jumla: "justifies sum absolute regularizer r(θ) = ‖θ‖1 = |θ1| + … + |θd|."

Ab dono ka moazna numbers se karein — ye exam mein aa sakta hai:

Hisaab — ek hi θ par dono sensitivities

Lein: theta = ( 3 , -4 ) aur rho = 0.1 Pehle norms nikaalein: || theta ||_2 = sqrt( 3^2 + (-4)^2 ) = sqrt( 9 + 16 ) = sqrt(25) = 5 || theta ||_1 = |3| + |-4| = 3 + 4 = 7 || theta ||_inf = max( |3| , |-4| ) = max( 3 , 4 ) = 4 ----- CASE A: L2 perturbation (gol gaind) ----- worst-case = rho * ||theta||_2 = 0.1 * 5 = 0.5 Tasdeeq — wo bura delta banayein: delta = rho * theta / ||theta||_2 = 0.1 * (3,-4) / 5 = (0.06, -0.08) Check ||delta||_2 = sqrt( 0.06^2 + (-0.08)^2 ) = sqrt( 0.0036 + 0.0064 ) = sqrt( 0.01 ) = 0.1 ✓ (had par, qanooni hai) theta^T delta = 3(0.06) + (-4)(-0.08) = 0.18 + 0.32 = 0.50 ✓ formula se mel khaya ----- CASE B: L-infinity perturbation (chaukor dabba) ----- worst-case = rho * ||theta||_1 = 0.1 * 7 = 0.7 Tasdeeq — wo bura delta banayein: delta = rho * sign(theta) = 0.1 * ( +1 , -1 ) = ( 0.1 , -0.1 ) Check ||delta||_inf = max( 0.1 , 0.1 ) = 0.1 ✓ (qanooni hai) theta^T delta = 3(0.1) + (-4)(-0.1) = 0.3 + 0.4 = 0.70 ✓ formula se mel khaya

Sabaq: wohi θ, wohi ρ, magar worst-case sensitivity 0.5 vs 0.7 — L-infinity wali soorat zyada khatarnaak hai (0.4 guna zyada). Wajah: L-infinity dabba L2 gaind se bada hai (gol gaind dabbe ke andar fit hoti hai), to usme se chunne ko zyada bure options hain. Aur yehi wajah hai ke ye farz zyada sakht regularizer (lasso) ki taraf le jaata hai.

F7Ridge regression aur Lasso regression

Ab naam rakh dein. Slides saaf tareef dete hain — dono mein square loss hai, farq sirf regularizer ka hai:

Dono mein SQUARE LOSS: ell( y-hat , y ) = ( y-hat - y )^2 RIDGE REGRESSION = minimize L(theta) + lambda || theta ||^2 2 LASSO REGRESSION = minimize L(theta) + lambda || theta || 1

Slides ki tareekhi aur amali baatein (ye chhote extra marks dete hain):

  • Lasso Rob Tibshirani (Stanford) ne 1994 mein eejaad kiya.
  • Lasso "widely used in advanced machine learning" hai.
  • Ahem farq: slides kehte hain "unlike ridge regression, there is no formula for the lasso parameter vector — but we can efficiently compute it anyway (since it's convex)."

Is aakhri nuqte ko samajh lein, ye exam mein poocha ja sakta hai:

RidgeLasso
Regularizer‖θ‖22‖θ‖1
Kis perturbation se niklaL2-ballL-infinity ball
Smooth (differentiable)?Haan har jagahNahi — 0 par kink hai
Closed-form formula?Haan — least squares ka formulaNahi — magar convex hai, to efficiently hal ho jaata hai
Convex?HaanHaan
Sparsity deta hai?NAHIHAAN
Feature selection?NahiHaan — khud ba khud

Aur dhyan dein ke kink hi sparsity ki wajah hai. Ridge smooth hai is liye uska hal aasaan hai magar wo sparsity nahi deta. Lasso mein kink hai is liye uska formula nahi banta, magar wohi kink coefficients ko bilkul zero tak dhakel deta hai. Ye ittefaq nahi — dekhein F10.

F8Offset ko regularize na karein

Lecture-5 ka ek chhota magar bohat amali topic, aur ye examiner ko bohat pasand aata hai.

Yaad karein (B5) ke hamara pehla feature constant hota hai: x1 = 1, aur uska coefficient θ1 offset hai. Ab Lecture-5 ki daleel:

QADAM 1. Constant feature kabhi badalta nahi — wo hamesha 1 hai. To uski perturbation hamesha zero hai: delta_1 = 0 hamesha QADAM 2. Yaad karein ke prediction ki tabdeeli ye thi: theta^T delta = theta_1 delta_1 + theta_2 delta_2 + ... Pehla term: theta_1 * 0 = 0 QADAM 3. Yaani theta_1 predictor ki SENSITIVITY mein koi hissa NAHI daalta, chahe wo kitna bhi bara ho! QADAM 4. To usay saza dena bay-maani hai. Slides: "for this reason it's common to NOT regularize the associated coefficient theta_1."

Is liye regularizers ko thoda badal dete hain — jama j = 2 se shuru hota hai, j = 1 se nahi:

Badla hua SUM SQUARE regularizer: r(theta) = theta_2^2 + theta_3^2 + ... + theta_d^2 (slides isay ||theta_(2:d)||_2^2 likhte hain — yaani theta ka doosre se d-wein tak ka hissa) Badla hua SUM ABSOLUTE regularizer: r(theta) = |theta_2| + |theta_3| + ... + |theta_d| (yaani ||theta_(2:d)||_1 )

Amali nateeja — ye zaroor likhein

Agar aap offset ko bhi regularize kar dein, to model ko θ1 chhota rakhne par majboor kiya ja raha hai — yaani predictions ko zabardasti zero ki taraf kheencha ja raha hai. Agar aap ka y 50 lakh ke aas paas hai, to ye tabah-kun hai. Isi liye amali tor par: (a) offset ko regularize na karein, aur (b) y aur features ko standardize kar lein (dekhein B4), taake regularization har feature ko insaaf se saza de.

F9Sparsity aur feature selection

"Sparse" ka matlab: chhidra, jis mein bohat khaali jagah ho. Ek sparse θ wo hai jis ke bohat se entries bilkul zero hain.

Misaal — 10 features ke saath: GHANA (dense) theta: ( 0.31, -0.08, 0.52, 0.04, -0.19, 0.11, 0.27, -0.02, 0.41, 0.06 ) saare 10 non-zero — model saare 10 features use kar raha hai SPARSE theta: ( 0.47, 0 , 0 , 0 , -0.62, 0 , 0 , 0 , 0.38, 0 ) sirf 3 non-zero (positions 1, 5, 9) => model ne khud faisla kiya ke sirf 3 features kaam ke hain

Ye kyun matlab rakhta hai? Slides ki apni daleel:

y-hat = theta^T x = theta_1 x_1 + theta_2 x_2 + ... + theta_d x_d Agar theta_j = 0 hai, to us feature ka term: theta_j * x_j = 0 * x_j = 0 => wo feature prediction mein KOI hissa nahi daal raha => wo feature aap ko chahiye hi NAHI

Slides ka jumla: "prediction does not depend on features for which θj = 0. This means we select some features to use (i.e. those with θj ≠ 0)."

Aur isi ka naam hai feature selection — slides: "choosing the sparsity pattern of θ (i.e. which entries are zero) is sometimes called feature selection. There are many ways to carry out feature selection."

Sparse θ ke faide (slides se, aur exam mein likhne layaq):

  1. Performance behtar ho jaati hai jab bohat se regressors waqai ghair-mutaalliq (irrelevant) hon. Wajah: ek ghair-mutaalliq feature ka θj jo bhi fit hoga wo poora shor hoga — us ne training data ke ittefaqi pattern pakde honge jo test data mein nahi honge. Usay zero kar dena us shor ko bilkul hata deta hai, yaani variance kam.
  2. Predictor samajhna aasaan ho jaata hai (slides: "makes predictor simpler to interpret"). 200 coefficients ki list dekh kar koi kuch nahi samajhta. 35 coefficients, baqi zero — ab aap bata sakte hain ke faisla kaun kar raha hai. Hospital, bank, insurance — jahan jawabdehi chahiye, wahan ye lazmi hai.

In ke ilawa do amali faide jo likhne par extra marks dete hain: sasta deployment (sirf 35 features ka data jama karna padega, 200 ka nahi — yaani 165 sensors/surveys/API calls bach gaye) aur tez prediction.

F10L1 sparsify kyun karta hai aur L2 kyun nahi — OHT Q-7 ka poora jawab

Ye OHT ka sab se zyada "technical" sawal hai. Do alag alag tareeqon se samjhaya ja sakta hai — dono seekh lein, aur exam mein dono likh dein (ek paragraph + ek tasveer). Poore marks pakke.

Daleel 1 — Marginal incentive (slides ki apni daleel)

Slides ka jumla: "for square penalty, once θj is small, θj2 is very small, so incentive for sum square regularizer to make a coefficient smaller decreases once it is small. For absolute penalty, incentive to make θj smaller keeps up all the way until it's zero."

Isay numbers se dekhein — sawal ye hai: "coefficient ko thoda aur chhota karne se penalty mein kitna faida hota hai?"

Hisaab — ek coefficient ko 0.01 chhota karne ka faida

----- RIDGE (penalty = theta^2) ----- Agar theta = 1.00 hai aur hum isay 0.99 karein: penalty pehle = 1.00^2 = 1.0000 penalty baad = 0.99^2 = 0.9801 FAIDA = 0.0199 <-- acha khasa faida Agar theta = 0.10 hai aur hum isay 0.09 karein: penalty pehle = 0.10^2 = 0.0100 penalty baad = 0.09^2 = 0.0081 FAIDA = 0.0019 <-- 10 guna kam faida Agar theta = 0.01 hai aur hum isay 0.00 karein: penalty pehle = 0.01^2 = 0.000100 penalty baad = 0.00^2 = 0.000000 FAIDA = 0.000100 <-- taqreeban kuch bhi nahi! ----- LASSO (penalty = |theta|) ----- Agar theta = 1.00 -> 0.99 : faida = 1.00 - 0.99 = 0.01 Agar theta = 0.10 -> 0.09 : faida = 0.10 - 0.09 = 0.01 Agar theta = 0.01 -> 0.00 : faida = 0.01 - 0.00 = 0.01 FAIDA HAMESHA WOHI = 0.01 !

Yehi poora jawab hai. Ridge mein, coefficient jitna chhota hota jaata hai, usay aur chhota karne ka faida utna kam hota jaata hai — aur zero tak le jaane ka faida taqreeban sifar ho jaata hai. Magar θj ko zero karne ki qeemat (yaani ℒ(θ) mein izafa) hamesha kuch hoti hai. To sauda ghaate ka hai, aur optimum par coefficient chhota magar non-zero reh jaata hai. Lasso mein faida constant rehta hai — aakhri qadam par bhi 0.01 — to ek waqt aata hai jab wo faida ℒ ke nuqsan se zyada ho jaata hai, aur coefficient exactly zero par chala jaata hai.

Isi baat ko derivative mein likhein (ye zyada riyaazi aur zyada mukhtasir hai):

RIDGE penalty ka derivative : d/dtheta_j [ theta_j^2 ] = 2 theta_j theta_j -> 0 hone par 2 theta_j -> 0 => zero ki taraf dhakelne ka ZOR KHUD KHATAM HO JAATA HAI LASSO penalty ka derivative : d/dtheta_j [ |theta_j| ] = sign(theta_j) = +1 ya -1 theta_j -> 0 hone par bhi |derivative| = 1 (constant!) => zero ki taraf dhakelne ka ZOR AAKHIR TAK QAAIM REHTA HAI (aur 0 par kink hai, jo coefficient ko wahan "pakad" leta hai)

Daleel 2 — Geometry (gol gaind vs hira)

Doosra tareeqa: RERM ko aise sochein ke hum ℒ(θ) minimize kar rahe hain is shart ke saath ke θ ek muqarrar ilaaqe ke andar rahe. Ridge ka ilaaqa gol (circle/ball) hai, lasso ka ilaaqa hira (diamond) hai — aur hire ke kone axes par hote hain, jahan ek coordinate zero hota hai.

Slides ka apna experiment — aur yehi OHT Q-7 ka scenario hai

Lecture-5 bilkul wohi tajurba karta hai jo OHT Q-7 mein poocha gaya hai:

Slides ke exact numbers — ye yaad kar lein

Tajurba: artificially generated data 50 data points, 200 features, "only a few of which are relevant" Nateeja (behtareen lambda par): RIDGE REGRESSION -> SAARE 200 coefficients NON-ZERO LASSO -> sirf 35 coefficients NON-ZERO (yaani 165 features khud ba khud nikal gaye)

Dhyan dein: n = 50 magar d = 200 — features data se 4 guna zyada. Ye bilkul wohi soorat hai jahan regularization lazmi hai, aur jahan sparsity sab se zyada faida deti hai.

OHT Q-7 ka mukammal jawab — 4 lines mein

  1. Ridge sparsity kyun nahi deta: ridge penalty Σθj2 ka derivative 2θj hai, jo θj → 0 hone par khud → 0 ho jaata hai. Yaani ek chhote coefficient ko bilkul zero karne ka penalty-faida taqreeban zero hai, magar empirical risk mein nuqsan hota hai — to optimum par saare coefficients chhote magar non-zero rehte hain. Geometrically: L2 ka ilaaqa gol hai, uski koi nukeeli kon axes par nahi, is liye contour usay aam point par chhuta hai jahan koi coordinate zero nahi.
  2. Kya use karein: lasso — yaani ℓ1 regularizer r(θ) = ‖θ‖1 = Σ|θj|.
  3. Kyun chalega: ℓ1 ka derivative ±1 hai — constant, zero tak. Dhakka aakhir tak qaaim rehta hai, aur 0 par kink coefficient ko wahan pakad leta hai. Is liye ℓ1 ek sparsifying regularizer hai aur khud ba khud feature selection kar deta hai. Lecture ka tajurba: 200 features par ridge ke 200 non-zero, lasso ke sirf 35.
  4. Amali baatein: λ ko validation se chunein; offset θ1 ko regularize na karein; pehle features standardize karein. Aur zikr kar dein ke lasso ka closed-form formula nahi hai magar wo convex hai, to efficiently hal ho jaata hai. (Chahein to elastic net — L1 + L2 — ka naam bhi le lein, jo mutaalliq features ke saath zyada stable hota hai.)

F11Aur strong sparsifier — L-0.5 regularizer

Agar |θj| (power 1) sparsity deta hai aur θj2 (power 2) nahi deta, to shayad power 1 se kam aur zyada sparsity degi? Slides yehi karte hain:

phi(z) = | z |^(1/2) = sqrt( |z| ) Isay "L_0.5 regularizer" kehte hain. MAGAR — slides ki do tanbeeh: 1) "you shouldn't use this term" kyunki ye NORM NAHI hai: ( |theta_1|^0.5 + ... + |theta_d|^0.5 )^2 ek norm nahi hai (triangle inequality tordta hai — tafseel VMLS mein) 2) ye L1 se "stronger sparsifier" hai MAGAR "not convex, so computing theta is heuristic"

Chalein dekhein ke ye L1 se zyada sakht kyun hai — wohi marginal-incentive wala hisaab:

Hisaab — teeno penalties ka "zero tak dhakelne ka zor"

Derivative nikaalein (theta > 0 maan kar): Ridge : d/dtheta [ theta^2 ] = 2 theta Lasso : d/dtheta [ theta ] = 1 L_0.5 : d/dtheta [ theta^0.5 ] = 0.5 / sqrt(theta) Ab theta chhota hone par zor dekhein: theta Ridge (2*theta) Lasso (1) L_0.5 (0.5/sqrt(theta)) ------- ---------------- ----------- ------------------------- 1.00 2.00 1 0.5/1.000 = 0.50 0.25 0.50 1 0.5/0.500 = 1.00 0.01 0.02 1 0.5/0.100 = 5.00 0.0001 0.0002 1 0.5/0.010 = 50.00

Teen bilkul alag rawaiye: ridge ka zor ghatta hai (2 → 0.0002), lasso ka constant rehta hai (hamesha 1), aur L-0.5 ka zor barhta jaata hai (0.5 → 50) — yaani jaise coefficient zero ke qareeb aata hai, penalty usay aur zor se zero ki taraf dhakelta hai. Isi liye ye sab se sakht sparsifier hai. Magar bilkul isi wajah se wo non-convex hai (zero par derivative ∞ ki taraf jaata hai), aur optimization sirf heuristic reh jaati hai — global optimum ki koi zamanat nahi.

Slides ka tajurba (slide 19): teen regularizers — ℓ1, ℓ2 aur square-root — ke train/test loss aur coefficient paths saath saath dikhaye gaye hain.

Convexity ka khulasa — ek table mein

RegularizerPowerConvex?SparsityAmali faisla
Ridge (L2)2HaanKoi nahiJab saare features thoda thoda kaam ke hon
Lasso (L1)1HaanAchhiSparsity ka default chunaav
L-0.50.5NAHIBohat zyadaSirf tajurbe ke tor par; heuristic hal

Yaad rakhein: power 1 ek sarhad (boundary) hai — wo sab se chhoti power hai jo ab bhi convex hai aur ab bhi sparsity deti hai. Isi liye lasso itna mash-hoor hua: wo donon jahanon ka behtareen milaap hai. Ye baat exam mein likhne par examiner ko pata chal jaata hai ke aap ne soch kar padha hai.

F12Nonnegative regularizer aur NNLS

Lecture-5 ka aakhri qism ka regularizer. Ab tak hum keh rahe the "θ chhota rakho". Yahan hum kuch aur keh rahe hain: "θ negative na ho".

/ 0 agar theta_j >= 0 phi_nn(z) = < \ infinity agar theta_j < 0 Yaani nonnegative theta par koi jurmana nahi, aur negative theta BILKUL MANA.

Iska matlab kya hai? Slides: "this means that when xj increases, so must our prediction." Yaani agar θj ≥ 0 hai, to us feature ka asar sirf ek taraf ho sakta hai — feature barhne se prediction barhegi (ya barabar rahegi), kabhi ghategi nahi. Ye monotonicity ki pabandi hai.

Ye kab chahiye? Slides ka example: "y is lifespan, x measures healthy behaviour". Yaani agar aap ki sehat ka score barhta hai, to zindagi ki tawaqqo barhni chahiye — ghatni bilkul nahi chahiye. Agar model ko azaadi dein, to shor ki wajah se wo kisi healthy-behaviour feature ka coefficient negative fit kar sakta hai, aur ye bakwaas hogi. Nonnegative pabandi domain knowledge ko model mein daal deti hai — yehi F2 wali "prior information" tafseer hai.

Square loss ke saath isay NNLS — Nonnegative Least Squares kehte hain.

Wo heuristic jo kaam nahi karta — slides ka khaas warning

Slides: "common heuristic for nonnegative least squares: use (θls)+ (works poorly)."

Yaani: pehle aam least squares hal karein, phir jo coefficients negative aaye unko zero kar dein ((·)+ ka matlab "negative ko zero kar do"). Ye bura kaam karta hai.

Wajah: jab aap ek coefficient zabardasti zero karte hain, to baqi coefficients usay muaawza (compensate) nahi kar paate — kyunki unko dobara fit nahi kiya gaya. Theek tareeqa ye hai ke pabandi ko optimization ke andar rakhein, taake saare coefficients ek saath, pabandi ko maante hue fit hon.

Aur slides is ka numerical saboot bhi dete hain (slide 22) — feature vector z = (1, u, (u−0.2)−, …, (u−0.8)−) par:

NNLS loss = 0.59 <-- pabandi ke saath theek se hal kiya LS loss = 0.30 <-- bagair pabandi (sab se kam, magar theta negative ho sakta hai) Heuristic loss = 15.05 <-- LS kar ke phir clip karna Heuristic ka loss NNLS se taqreeban 25 GUNA bura hai (15.05 / 0.59 = 25.5) aur LS se 50 GUNA bura (15.05 / 0.30 = 50.2)

Aur slides ek khoobsurat geometrical nateeja bhi batate hain us misaal ka: us khaas feature vector ke saath, "nonnegative θ means both predictor function is convex (curves up) and nondecreasing." Yaani pabandi ne na sirf monotonicity di, balki poora function convex aur nondecreasing ban gaya — theek wo shakal jo lifespan vs healthy-behaviour jaise masle ke liye munasib hai.

F13Regularizer aur λ kaise chunein

Lecture-5 ka aakhri slide, aur ye poore course ka amali khulasa hai. Slides ka jumla:

Slides ka tareeqa — lafz ba lafz

"Use out-of-sample or cross-validation to choose among regularizers. For each candidate regularizer, choose λ to minimize test error (and maybe a little larger …). Use the regularizer that gives the best test error."

Teen qadam, saaf saaf:

  1. Candidates ki list banayein — jaise ridge, lasso, elastic net, nonnegative.
  2. Har ek ke liye λ ki ek range aazmayein (aam tor par logarithmic grid: 0.001, 0.01, 0.1, 1, 10, 100… — isi liye slides ke plots mein x-axis log10(λ) hai) aur wo λ chunein jo held-out error sab se kam kare.
  3. Jis regularizer ka behtareen test error nikla, wohi istemaal karein.

Ab wo chhota sa jumla jo slides mein bracket mein hai magar bohat gehra hai: "(and maybe a little larger …)". Iska matlab: jo λ test error ko sab se kam karta hai, us se thoda bara λ lena behtar hota hai. Kyun?

  • Aap ka test error ka andaza khud shor ke saath aaya hai. Wo validation set bhi mehdood hai; uska minimum ittefaqan thoda idhar udhar ho sakta hai. Us minimum par bilkul bethna usi validation set par overfit karna hai.
  • Bara λ = zyada insensitive predictor = asal duniya ke badlav ke khilaaf zyada mehfooz.
  • Bara λ = zyada sparse aur saada model (lasso ke saath) = samajhna aasaan, deploy karna sasta.

(Statistics mein isi ko "one standard error rule" kehte hain: sab se bara λ chunein jiska error minimum se ek standard error ke andar ho.)

Kaunsa regularizer kab — faisle ka khulasa

Soorat-e-haalChunaavWajah
Bohat se features, sirf kuch mutaalliq (OHT Q-7)Lasso (L1)Sparsity + khud-kaar feature selection
Saare features thoda thoda kaam ke, ya ek doosre se mutaalliq (correlated)Ridge (L2)Sab ko mila kar dabaata hai, kisi ek ko bay-wajah nahi nikalta
Dono chahiye — sparsity aur correlated features ke saath stabilityElastic net (L1 + L2)Dono ke faide
Domain knowledge kehta hai asar sirf ek taraf ho sakta haiNonnegativeMonotonicity ki pabandi; prior information
Predictor bilkul insensitive hai (constant predictor)Koi nahiLec-4: "we don't need regularization since the predictor is completely insensitive"

Aakhir mein poore course ko ek saath dekhein — aap ne ab ye poora safar kar liya hai:

RAW DATA u | | phi (feature map — Part B: embedding, one-hot, standardize, | polynomial, interactions, hinge features) v FEATURE VECTOR x | | g_theta(x) = theta^T x (Part B6: theta mein linear, u mein non-linear) v PREDICTION y-hat | | r = y-hat - y (Part D2: residual) | p(r) (Part D, E: square / absolute / tilted / | Huber / log-Huber / fractional) v EMPIRICAL RISK L(theta) = average loss (Part D4) | | + lambda * r(theta) (Part F: ridge / lasso / L-0.5 / nonnegative) v RERM -> theta -> aur sab hyper-parameters VALIDATION se (Part F13)

Is page par kya hai. Saare 4 quiz sawal aur OHT-1 sample paper ke saare 10 sawal — chhote jawab: sirf concept, wajah, aur ilaaj. Bayein column English (jo paper par likhenge), dayein Roman Urdu (samajhne ke liye).

Jawab itne chhote kyun: asli paper mein har sawal ke neeche sirf 4 line ki jagah hai aur 6 minute milte hain. Lamba jawab likhna waqt bhi khata hai aur examiner ko point dhoondna padta hai. Agar kisi topic ki tafseel chahiye to Concepts tab par link diya hua hai.

Hint pakadne ka tareeqa — 3 qadam

  1. Pehle aakhri jumla padhein. Scenario (grocery chain, Mars rover) sirf kahani hai; asal sawal aakhri 1–2 line mein hota hai.
  2. Trigger word dhoondein — neeche ki table se. Ek lafz mil gaya, jawab tay ho gaya.
  3. 4-line formula bharein (neeche box mein).
