4-line formula — har sawal par yehi

LINE 1 NAAM LO concept ka theek naam, foran. LINE 2 MECHANISM formula ya derivative likho (p'(r) = 2r, 2*theta_j -> 0). LINE 3 SCENARIO SE JODO sawal ke apne lafz/numbers use karo. LINE 4 SIFARISH kya karein + "choose alpha/lambda/k by validation".

Rubric paper par likha hai: problem analysis, design capability, technical justification — ye chaar lines teeno cover kar deti hain. Waqt: 6 minute per sawal; kisi ek par 8 se zyada nahi.

Hissa 1

Quiz Sawal — saare 4

Quiz-1 ke teen mukhtalif versions (MS AI, MS DS, solution file) aur Quiz-2. Har ek 3 marks, 20 minute.

Quiz-1 MS AI3 marks1-NN, overfitting

1-NN — zero training error aur generalization

A student fits a 1-Nearest Neighbor (k = 1) predictor to a training dataset (x1, y1), …, (xn, yn), where all feature vectors xi are distinct.
(a) What is the predicted value when the model is evaluated on one of its own training points xi, and what is the resulting training error?
(b) Explain conceptually why achieving zero training error with a 1-NN predictor does not guarantee good performance on unseen data.

Hint

  • "all xi are distinct" — ye examiner ka tohfa hai: iska matlab xi ka nearest neighbour sirf khud ho sakta hai (koi doosra 0 faasle par nahi).
  • "conceptually" — part (b) mein formula nahi, wajah chahiye.

English — likhein

(a) ŷ(xi) = yi, and training error = 0.

Why: ‖xi − xi‖ = 0, and since all x are distinct no other point reaches distance 0 — so the model simply recalls the stored label. This holds for any loss, since ℓ(y,y) = 0.

(b) Zero training error is vacuous — it follows from the predictor's definition, not from learning. A lookup table is always perfect on its own entries, so it carries no information about generalization. Four reasons it fails on unseen data:

  • Biased estimate — the predictor was selected using the same data it is scored on, so training error underestimates true risk.
  • Noise is memorised — a mislabelled point is interpolated exactly and corrupts its whole region; no averaging smooths it.
  • Maximum variance / sensitivity — each prediction rests on one point; the predictor is a step function, so a tiny change in x jumps the output.
  • Test points fall between training points, where the fit was never constrained.

Fix: raise k (or soft-NN with β), standardize features, choose k by validation — not training error, which always picks k = 1.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

(a) ŷ(xi) = yi, aur training error = 0.

Wajah: apne se faasla 0 hota hai, aur saare x alag hain to koi doosra bhi 0 par nahi — model ne bas yaad kiya hua label wapas de diya. Ye kisi bhi loss ke saath 0 hi aayega.

(b) Ye zero khaali hai — model ki tareef se nikla, seekhne se nahi. Lookup table apne hi khaano par hamesha 100% hoti hai, to is se generalization ka kuch pata nahi chalta. Chaar wajuhat:

  • Apna paper khud check kiya — jis data se model bana usi par naapa, to andaza jhoota (umeed se acha) hai.
  • Noise bhi yaad kar liya — ek ghalat label apne poore ilaaqe ko kharab kar deta hai; average lene ka zariya nahi.
  • Variance/sensitivity maximum — har jawab ek point par tika hai; prediction step function hai, zara si tabdeeli par chhalaang.
  • Naya point beech mein girta hai, jahan model par koi shart nahi lagi thi.

Ilaaj: k barhayein (ya soft-NN with β), features standardize karein, k ko validation se chunein — training error se nahi, wo hamesha k = 1 chunega.

Quiz-1 (solution file)3 markssoft nearest neighbour

Combat drone — kaunsa predictor aur kaunsa parameter

A drone predicts threat level y from a feature vector x (radar cross-section, speed, emission frequencies), comparing it against a database of past encounters. It must predict using a weighted average of the known outcomes, where the influence of a past encounter drops off with feature-vector distance. Engineers need a tunable dial to control how rapidly this influence decays. Which predictor, and what parameter controls how strictly it relies on the most similar past encounters?

Hint — sawal ne taqreeban jawab likh diya hai

  • "weighted average" → 1-NN khaarij (ek point uthata hai), k-NN bhi khaarij (saada average, weighted nahi).
  • "drops off with distance" → exp(−dist²/β). "how rapidly decays" → yehi β hai.

English — likhein

Predictor: the soft nearest neighbour predictor.
Parameter: the bandwidth β > 0 — the characteristic decay length.

y-hat(x) = SUM_i w_i y_i exp( - ||x - x_i||^2 / beta ) w_i = ---------------------------------------- SUM_j exp( - ||x - x_j||^2 / beta )

Why it fits: the squared distance sits in a negative exponent, so distance 0 gives exp(0) = 1 (maximum influence) and far points decay exponentially toward 0. Dividing by the sum makes Σwi = 1, so ŷ is a genuine weighted average.

Effect of β: small β → weight concentrates on the closest encounter, so it reverts to 1-NN (strictest reliance, high variance, overfitting). Large β → all weights near-equal, so it approaches the plain average (a constant predictor, underfitting).

Practical: choose β by validation, and standardize the three features first — otherwise the largest-range feature dominates the distance.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Predictor: soft nearest neighbour predictor.
Parameter: β > 0 — bandwidth, yaani asar khatam hone ki raftaar.

y-hat(x) = SUM_i w_i y_i exp( - ||x - x_i||^2 / beta ) w_i = ---------------------------------------- SUM_j exp( - ||x - x_j||^2 / beta )

Kaise kaam karta hai: faasla negative exponent mein hai, to rishta ulat jaata hai — faasla 0 par exp(0) = 1 (poora asar), door hone par taqreeban 0. Jama se taqseem karne par saare weights jama kar ke 1 ban jaate hain, to jawab sahi average rehta hai.

β ka asar: chhota β → poora weight sab se qareeb point par, model 1-NN jaisa (sab se sakht bharosa, variance zyada, overfitting). Bara β → saare weights barabar, model saada average (constant predictor, underfitting).

Amali: β validation se chunein, aur teeno features pehle standardize karein — warna sab se badi range wala feature faasle par qabza kar lega.

Quiz-1 MS DS3 marksfeature mapping

Parameters mein linear, input mein non-linear

How does a model that is linear in its parameters, like g(x) = θTx, become a non-linear function of the original input u when using a feature mapping like x = φ(u) = (1, u, u², …)? Discuss the implications for model flexibility and how the choice of feature mapping relates to the trade-off between underfitting and overfitting.

Hint

  • Teen hisse poochhe gaye hain (mechanism / flexibility / trade-off) — teen chhote paragraph likhein, warna 1/3 marks.
  • Dot product khol dein — wo khud saboot hai. Aur "trade-off" ka matlab dono taraf ka nuqsan + validation.

English — likhein

1. Mechanism. Expanding the dot product:

y-hat = theta^T phi(u) = theta_1 + theta_2 u + theta_3 u^2 + theta_4 u^3 + ...

This is a polynomial in u — a curve — yet each θj still only multiplies a fixed number and is summed. So the model is non-linear in u but linear in θ. All non-linearity lives in φ, a fixed map applied before the parameters enter.

2. Flexibility. Enriching φ (higher degree, log, interactions, hinges) enlarges the hypothesis space at no algorithmic cost — and because θ enters linearly, the empirical risk stays convex, so there is one global minimum and least squares still applies. φ is the designer's lever; ERM merely picks the best element of the space φ defines.

3. Trade-off. The richness of φ is the complexity dial:

  • Too poor (φ = (1,u)): the true function is not in the space → underfitting, high bias, patterned residuals.
  • Too rich (degree 20): d approaches n, the model interpolates noise → overfitting, high variance, wild oscillation between points.

Choose by validation — training error always prefers the richest φ. In practice: rich φ + regularization, tuning λ by validation.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

1. Hota kaise hai. Dot product khol dein:

y-hat = theta_1 + theta_2 u + theta_3 u^2 + ...

Ye u mein polynomial hai, yaani curve. Magar har θj abhi bhi sirf ek number se multiply ho kar jama ho raha hai. To: u mein non-linear, θ mein linear. Saara tedha-pan φ mein hai, jo parameters se pehle lagta hai.

2. Lachak. φ rich karne se (degree, log, interactions, hinges) hypothesis space bara ho jaata hai aur algorithm bilkul nahi badalta. Aur kyunki θ linearly ghusa hai, problem convex rehti hai — ek hi minimum, aur least squares chalta rehta hai. φ designer ka hathiyar hai; ERM sirf us space mein se behtareen chun leta hai.

3. Trade-off. φ ki richness hi complexity ka knob hai:

  • Bohat saada ((1,u)): asal function space mein mojood hi nahi → underfit, high bias, residuals mein pattern.
  • Bohat rich (degree 20): d n ke qareeb, model noise bhi yaad kar leta hai → overfit, high variance, points ke beech curve pagal.

Validation se chunein — training error hamesha sab se rich φ chunega. Amali tareeqa: rich φ + regularization, aur λ validation se.

Quiz-2 MS AI3 marksfeature engineering

Ghar ki keemat — raw features kaafi nahi

A linear regression model predicts house prices from five raw features: area, bedrooms, bathrooms, age, distance to city center. The model performs poorly: it systematically underpredicts for large houses with many bedrooms and overpredicts for small houses. Also, the effect of age is not linear — very old and very new houses behave distinctly, but middle ages are similar. Raw features alone are not capturing the true patterns. What is the core problem, and how could it be solved?

Hint — do shikayatein, do alag bimaariyan

  • "systematically" — ba-qaida ghalti = random nahi = structure daalna bhool gaye = underfitting. (Overfitting ho hi nahi sakta: model training par bhi bura hai.)
  • "under for large, over for small" → interaction missing. "old and new distinct, middle similar" → non-monotonic → hinge/bin features.
  • "Raw features alone are not allowing…" — sawal khud keh raha hai: masla features ka hai, model ka nahi.

English — likhein

Core problem: underfitting caused by an inadequate feature map φ — not by the model form. Using the five raw features directly forces the model to be additive with one fixed slope per feature:

y-hat = theta_0 + theta_1(area) + theta_2(bed) + ... + theta_4(age) + ...

Such a function cannot represent either observed pattern, so the true function lies outside the hypothesis space. The systematic (non-random) residuals are the evidence:

  • Under for large / over for small → the value of extra area depends on bedroom count. One fixed θ1 cannot express a varying effect — an interaction is missing.
  • Age non-monotonic → one coefficient encodes exactly one slope, but the data needs down, then flat, then up.

Solution — enrich φ:

  • Interactions: area × bedrooms, area / bedrooms, bathrooms / bedrooms.
  • Transforms: log(area) or area², and model log(price) — prices scale multiplicatively.
  • Hinge features for age (knots at 15, 50): age, max(0, age−15), max(0, age−50) → three independent slopes, giving the fall–flat–rise shape. Same for distance.
  • Standardize everything; keep the constant feature for the offset.
  • Then ridge or lasso with λ by validation, since features have grown; lasso also shows which new ones earned their place.

Key point: the model stays linear in θ, so least squares applies unchanged.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Asal masla: na-kaafi feature map φ ki wajah se underfitting — model ki shakal kharab nahi, features kharab hain. Paanch raw features seedha daalne se model additive ban jaata hai, har feature ki ek pakki dhalaan:

y-hat = theta_0 + theta_1(area) + theta_2(bed) + ... + theta_4(age) + ...

Aisa function in dono patterns ko bayaan hi nahi kar sakta — asal function hypothesis space ke bahar hai. Saboot: residuals ba-qaida hain, random nahi:

  • Bade ghar kam, chhote zyada → area ka faida bedrooms par depend karta hai. Ek pakka θ1 badalta hua asar nahi bata sakta — interaction missing.
  • Age non-monotonic → ek coefficient sirf ek dhalaan deta hai, magar data teen chahta hai: girna, flat, phir barhna.

Hal — φ ko rich karein:

  • Interactions: area × bedrooms, area / bedrooms, bathrooms / bedrooms.
  • Transforms: log(area) ya area², aur target log(price) — keematein guna ke hisaab se barhti hain.
  • Age ke liye hinge features (knots 15, 50): age, max(0, age−15), max(0, age−50) → teen azaad dhalaanein, yaani "girna → flat → barhna". Wohi distance ke saath.
  • Standardize sab kuch; constant feature rakhein (offset).
  • Phir ridge ya lasso, λ validation se — features barh gaye hain. Lasso ye bhi bata dega kaun si nayi feature kaam ki thi.

Ahem baat: model θ mein linear hi rehta hai, to least squares bagair tabdeeli chalta rahega.

Hissa 2

OHT-1 Sample Paper — saare 10 sawal

6 marks har ek, kul 60. Har sawal ke neeche 4 line ki jagah hai — jawab utne hi chhote hain.

OHT Q-16 markslearning types

Grocery chain — supervised, unsupervised, ya association?

A grocery chain wants to analyze market-basket data, observes that (bread → milk) = 0.7, and wants to build a product recommendation system. State whether this is best framed as supervised, unsupervised, or association analysis.

Hint

  • (bread → milk) = 0.7 — ek conditional probability di gayi hai. Ye taqreeban hamesha association analysis ki nishani hai.
  • Khud se poochein: y kahan hai? Transactions ki list mein koi "sahi jawab" column nahi → supervised khaarij. Customers ko group nahi kar rahe → clustering bhi khaarij.

English — likhein

Answer: association analysis (association rule mining / pattern mining) — the fifth category, not supervised or unsupervised.

Not supervised: supervised learning needs labelled pairs (xi, yi) with a designated target. Market-basket data is just transactions (TID, items) with no target column.

Not clustering: clustering groups records (customers) by similarity; here we discover co-occurrence relationships between items.

The given number: 0.7 is the rule's confidence, i.e. P(milk | bread) = 0.7 — of all baskets with bread, 70% also have milk. Association analysis estimates exactly such P(Y|X), measured by support and confidence, and feeds the recommender directly.

Worth adding: if reframed as "will this customer buy milk", with per-customer labels, it would be supervised classification. The framing depends on whether a target label exists — here none does.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: association analysis (association rule mining) — paanchwi qism, na supervised na unsupervised.

Supervised nahi: us ke liye (xi, yi) ke jode aur ek target chahiye. Yahan sirf transactions hain (TID, items) — koi target column nahi.

Clustering nahi: clustering records (customers) ko groups mein baantti hai; yahan hum items ke darmiyan rishte dhoond rahe hain.

Jo number diya: 0.7 us rule ka confidence hai, yaani P(milk | bread) = 0.7 — jin baskets mein bread hai, un mein 70% mein doodh bhi. Association analysis ka kaam bilkul yehi hai (support aur confidence se naapna), aur recommender isi par chalta hai.

Extra mark: agar sawal badal kar "is customer doodh khareedega?" ho, aur per-customer labels mil jaayein, to wo supervised classification ban jaayega. Faisla is par hai ke target label hai ya nahi — yahan nahi hai.

OHT Q-26 marksembeddings

NLP startup — faithful embedding, aur one-hot kyun bura

An NLP startup wants to classify customer support tickets into categories, has raw text data, and wants to convert words into vectors. Explain what faithful embedding is? And why is one-hot embedding often unsuitable for a this large vocabulary-based task.

Hint

  • Do hisse hain — tareef, aur one-hot ki burai. Dono likhein.
  • "large vocabulary" — jawab mein numbers daalein (50,000 alfaaz, lakhon parameters, d ≫ n). Lafz "faithful" ka matlab hi jawab hai: wafaadar.

English — likhein

Faithful embedding. An embedding φ maps raw data u to a numerical vector x = φ(u). It is faithful when it preserves in the vector space the distinctions and relationships that matter in the raw data: (1) items that differ meaningfully map to different vectors, and (2) the geometry reflects real similarity — similar items land close, unrelated ones far. Requirement (2) is what lets the model generalize across related words.

Why one-hot fails on a large vocabulary — three reasons:

  1. Dimensionality → overfitting. d = V. With 50,000 words and 10 categories that is 50,000 × 10 = 500,000 parameters; with n ≈ 2,000 tickets, d ≫ n, so the model can memorise the training set. Plus heavy memory and compute.
  2. Not faithful — all distinct words are equidistant. Any two differ by one +1 and one −1, so
    || x_a - x_b ||_2 = sqrt(1 + 1) = sqrt(2) for EVERY pair
    "refund" is exactly as far from "reimbursement" as from "banana" — no similarity exists, so nothing transfers between synonyms.
  3. No handling of unseen or rare words. A new word has no coordinate at all (out-of-vocabulary); a word seen twice has a coefficient fitted to two examples.

Fix: dense learned embeddings (word2vec / GloVe / transformer, 50–300 dims) — approximately faithful, synonyms cluster. Alternatives: TF-IDF + SVD, feature hashing, sub-word tokens (BPE) to kill the OOV problem.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Faithful embedding. φ raw data u ko numbers ki list x = φ(u) mein badalti hai. Wo faithful (wafaadar) hai jab raw data ke asal farq aur asal rishte vector space mein bach jaayein: (1) jo cheezein maani mein alag hain wo alag vectors par jaayein, aur (2) geometry sach boley — milti julti cheezein qareeb, ghair-mutaalliq door. Doosri shart hi generalization deti hai.

One-hot bade vocabulary par kyun bura — teen wajuhat:

  1. Dimension phat jaata hai → overfitting. d = V. 50,000 alfaaz aur 10 categories = 5,00,000 parameters; agar n ≈ 2,000 tickets hain to d ≫ n, model saara training data yaad kar sakta hai. Saath memory aur computation ka bojh.
  2. Faithful nahi — saare alag alfaaz barabar door. Kisi bhi do ke farq mein ek +1 aur ek −1 hota hai, to
    || x_a - x_b ||_2 = sqrt(1 + 1) = sqrt(2) HAR jodi ke liye
    "refund" bilkul utna door hai "reimbursement" se jitna "banana" se — similarity ka tasawwur hi nahi, to hum-maani alfaaz par kuch transfer nahi hota.
  3. Naye / kam-aane wale alfaaz ka hal nahi. Naye lafz ka column hi nahi (out-of-vocabulary); do baar aaye lafz ka coefficient do misaalon se seekha gaya.

Ilaaj: dense learned embeddings (word2vec / GloVe / transformer, 50–300 dims) — taqreeban faithful, hum-maani alfaaz jhund banate hain. Doosre raste: TF-IDF + SVD, feature hashing, sub-word tokens (BPE) — OOV masla khatam.

OHT Q-36 marksi.i.d. / transfer

Self-driving car — dhoop mein train, dhund mein deploy

A self-driving car company trains a vision model on daylight images but must deploy it in foggy, low-light conditions, so the test distribution is different from the training distribution. What type of learning is required when the test distribution differs from training?

Hint

  • "test distribution is different" — Lecture-1 ka lafz ba lafz jumla. Jawab ek lafz: transfer learning.
  • Magar 6 marks hain — baqi lines mein: kaunsa i.i.d. hissa toota, khaas naam, aur ilaaj. "Collective classification" na likhein — wo independence tootne par hota hai.

English — likhein

Answer: transfer learning is required — specifically domain adaptation.

Which assumption fails: the i.i.d. assumption's "identically distributed" part. Training inputs come from a daylight distribution, test inputs from a foggy low-light one. (Independence is not the problem — had it failed, the remedy would be collective classification.)

Precise terms: since the task is unchanged (detect vehicles, pedestrians, lanes) and only the input distribution shifts, this is covariate shift — p(x) changes while p(y|x) is essentially the same. General term: dataset / distribution shift.

Why dangerous: error measured on a daylight hold-out no longer estimates deployment risk, so the model looks excellent in validation and fails on the road — safety-critical.

Fix: fine-tune on labelled fog images; augment with synthetic fog/glare/blur; unsupervised domain adaptation (domain-invariant features) if fog data is unlabelled; sensor fusion with radar/LiDAR; and validate on a fog-only hold-out, never a daylight split.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Jawab: transfer learning chahiye — khaas tor par domain adaptation.

Kaunsa assumption toota: i.i.d. ka "identically distributed" hissa. Training ki tasveerein dhoop ki distribution se, test ki dhund/andhere ki distribution se. (Independence ka masla nahi — wo tootta to ilaaj collective classification hota.)

Theek istilaahein: kyunki kaam wohi hai (gaadi, banda, lane pehchanna) aur sirf input ka mahaul badla, is ko covariate shift kehte hain — p(x) badal gaya, p(y|x) taqreeban wohi. Aam lafz: distribution shift.

Khatra: dhoop wale hold-out ka error ab deployment ka risk nahi bata raha — model validation mein shaandaar lagega aur sadak par fail hoga. Ye jaan ka masla hai.

Ilaaj: labelled dhund ki tasveeron par fine-tune; nakli dhund/glare/blur se augmentation; agar labels na hon to unsupervised domain adaptation (domain-invariant features); radar/LiDAR sensor fusion; aur validation sirf dhund wale hold-out par, dhoop ke split par hargiz nahi.

OHT Q-46 marksT,P,E + RL

Mars rover — T, P, E aur learning type

An AI team is building a Mars rover that must navigate autonomously, human expertise does not exist for this environment, and the rover must learn from rewards after each action. Define the learning task, performance metric, and experience, state whether this is supervised, unsupervised, or reinforcement learning.

Hint

  • "Define the task, metric, experience" → Mitchell ka <T,P,E>. Teen alag lines likhein: "T:", "P:", "E:" — paragraph mein mila kar na likhein.
  • "human expertise does not exist" — Lecture-1 ka exact example (navigating on Mars). Ye supervised ko khaarij karta hai. "rewards after each action" → RL ka dastkhat.
  • P ek ginne wala number ho — "acha navigate karna" P nahi hai.

English — likhein

T (task): navigate autonomously across unknown Martian terrain — at each state choose an action advancing toward the target without collision or immobilisation. Formally: learn a policy mapping states to actions.

P (metric): fraction of traverses completed without failure (or expected cumulative reward per traverse, or average distance travelled before human intervention).

E (experience): the rover's own interaction with the environment — sequences of (state, action, reward, next state) gathered by trial and error, with rewards often delayed.

Type: reinforcement learning. Three reasons:

  • No labels can exist — supervised learning needs yi giving the correct action, but the question says no human expertise exists, so there is no teacher.
  • Feedback is a reward, not an answer — the rover learns how good an outcome was, not what it should have done. Delayed reward creates the credit assignment problem.
  • Output is a policy, not a prediction — which excludes unsupervised learning, whose goal is a data model, not a control rule.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

T (task): Mars ki anjaan zameen par khud-mukhtaar navigate karna — har state par aisi harkat chunna jo target ke qareeb le jaaye, bagair takraane ya phansne ke. Technically: ek policy seekhna jo state se action ka naqsha de.

P (metric): kitne percent traverses bagair failure mukammal hue (ya har traverse par kul reward, ya insaan ki madad se pehle average faasla).

E (experience): rover ka khud ka mahaul ke saath taamul — (state, action, reward, agli state) ki sequences, trial-and-error se, aur reward aksar der se milta hai.

Qism: reinforcement learning. Teen wajuhat:

  • Labels mojood hi nahi ho sakte — supervised ke liye yi (sahi harkat) chahiye, magar sawal keh raha hai koi insaani expert nahi, to ustaad kahan se aayega.
  • Jawab nahi, reward milta hai — rover ko pata chalta hai natija kitna acha tha, ye nahi ke kya karna chahiye tha. Der se milne wala reward credit assignment problem paida karta hai.
  • Natija ek policy hai, prediction nahi — is liye unsupervised khaarij, kyunki wo data model banata hai, control rule nahi.
OHT Q-56 marksi.i.d. / validation

Retail sales — random split acha laga, agla mahina tabahi

A retail company splits its monthly sales data randomly into 80% training and 20% testing; the model performs very well on the test set but fails badly when deployed on next month's sales. State what assumption about the data is violated here, explain why this is a problem for generalization, and describe what validation strategy you would use instead and why.

Hint

  • Teen kaam poochhe gaye hain — assumption / kyun masla / kaunsi strategy. Teesra hissa aksar log bhool jaate hain, wo 2 marks hain.
  • "monthly" + "randomly" — yehi jodi trap hai. Aur asal lafz "overfitting" nahi, data leakage hai.

English — likhein

1. Violated assumption: i.i.d. — and both parts fail. Independence: sales are temporally autocorrelated (this week predicts next week), so records are not independent draws. Identical distribution: the series is non-stationary — trend, seasonality, promotions — so month t+1's distribution differs from month t's.

2. Why generalization breaks — data leakage. A random split interleaves test points among training points in time, so for any test point the model has already seen records from both before and after it. Predicting it is interpolation within a known period, not extrapolation into an unknown future — this is look-ahead bias / leakage. The test error is therefore a badly optimistic estimate of deployment risk, which needs next-month error from a shifted distribution. A high score on a leaky split is not evidence of generalization.

3. Strategy: temporal (forward-chaining / rolling-origin) validation. Every validation point must be strictly later than the training data used to predict it:

fold 1 : train months 1..6 -> validate month 7 fold 2 : train months 1..7 -> validate month 8 ... roll the origin forward, average the errors ...

Why: it simulates deployment exactly — at prediction time only the past is available — so it gives an honest estimate, and it also reveals whether performance degrades over time, which a random split hides completely. Compute feature statistics (means, std) from the training window only; retrain periodically.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

1. Toota assumption: i.i.d. — aur dono hisse tootay. Independence: sales waqt ke saath juri hain (is hafte se agla hafta pata chal jaata hai), to records azaad draws nahi. Identical distribution: series non-stationary hai — trend, seasonality, promotions — to mahina t+1 ki distribution mahina t jaisi nahi.

2. Generalization kyun tooti — data leakage. Random split test points ko training points ke beech beech bo deta hai, to har test point ke pehle aur baad — dono taraf ke records model ne dekh liye. Us ka andaza lagana jaane-pehchane arse ke andar interpolation hai, anjaan mustaqbil mein chhalaang nahi — yehi look-ahead bias / leakage hai. Natija: test error deployment ke risk ka bohat jhoota (umeed-afza) andaza hai, jabke chahiye tha agle mahine ka error, jiski distribution khisak chuki hai.

3. Strategy: temporal (forward-chaining / rolling-origin) validation. Har validation point us training data se baad ka ho jo uske andaze mein istemaal hua:

fold 1 : train mahine 1..6 -> validate mahina 7 fold 2 : train mahine 1..7 -> validate mahina 8 ... origin aage barhate jaayein, errors ka average ...

Kyun: ye deployment ki hubahu naqal karta hai — prediction ke waqt sirf guzra waqt mojood hota hai — to andaza imaandar milta hai. Aur ye ye bhi dikha deta hai ke performance waqt ke saath gir rahi hai ya nahi, jo random split bilkul chhupa deta hai. Feature statistics (mean, std) sirf training window se; waqtan fawaqtan dobara train karein.

OHT Q-66 marksrobust loss

Ride-sharing — squared loss outliers par kyun phat jaata hai

A ride-sharing company predicts trip duration from GPS and traffic data, and a few GPS readings are severe outliers due to sensor glitches; squared loss gives a model that fits these outliers poorly for normal trips. Why squared loss is sensitive to outliers, state which robust loss you would recommend? and how robust loss behaves differently from squared loss for large errors.

Hint

  • Teen hisse: kyun sensitive / kaunsa loss / bade errors par farq.
  • Sab se ahem: jawab mein derivative likhein, sirf value nahi. "p′(r) = 2r bay-had barhta hai" — yehi poore marks deta hai.

English — likhein

1. Why squared loss is sensitive. p(r) = r², so its derivative p′(r) = 2r grows without bound — and each point's pull on the ERM gradient is proportional to that derivative. A residual 10× larger gives 100× the loss and 10× the pull:

r = 0.5 -> p = 0.25 p' = 1 r = 10 -> p = 100 p' = 20 r = 50 -> p = 2500 p' = 100

So one glitched GPS reading can dominate the average loss; ERM then shifts θ toward it, which worsens the fit for all normal trips — exactly the stated symptom.

2. Recommendation: the Huber penalty.

/ r^2 if |r| <= alpha p_hub(r) = < \ alpha ( 2|r| - alpha ) if |r| > alpha

Quadratic on normal trips, affine on outliers. It is convex and smooth, so optimization stays reliable with a unique global minimum. Choose α by validation, at roughly the scale of genuine noise. (Log-Huber is more robust but not convex, so Huber first.)

3. Difference at large errors. Growth is linear, not quadratic, so the derivative saturates at 2α: with α = 1, at r = 10 the square pulls 20 but Huber pulls only 2. Each outlier's influence is therefore bounded however far off it is, so the model can leave a few points with large residuals and fit the bulk well — perhaps 98% of trips accurately.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

1. Squared loss kyun sensitive hai. p(r) = r², to uska derivative p′(r) = 2r bay-had barhta hai — aur gradient mein har point ka zor usi derivative ke mutabiq hota hai. 10 guna bara residual = 100 guna loss aur 10 guna zor:

r = 0.5 -> p = 0.25 p' = 1 r = 10 -> p = 100 p' = 20 r = 50 -> p = 2500 p' = 100

To ek kharab GPS reading poore average loss par qabza kar leti hai; ERM phir θ usi taraf khisaka deta hai, jis se saare normal trips ki prediction kharab ho jaati hai — bilkul wohi shikayat.

2. Sifarish: Huber penalty.

/ r^2 agar |r| <= alpha p_hub(r) = < \ alpha ( 2|r| - alpha ) agar |r| > alpha

Normal trips par square jaisa, outliers par seedhi line. Ye convex aur smooth hai, to optimization bharosay ke qabil aur global minimum ek. α validation se, taqreeban asli shor ke paimane par. (Log-Huber zyada robust magar convex nahi, is liye pehle Huber.)

3. Bade errors par farq. Barhna linear hai, quadratic nahi, to derivative 2α par ruk jaata hai: α = 1 par, r = 10 pe square 20 zor lagata hai magar Huber sirf 2. Yaani har outlier ka asar mehdood ho gaya, chahe wo kitna bhi door ho. Is liye model chand points ko bade residual ke saath chhod kar baqi data theek fit kar leta hai — maslan 98% trips par sahi.

OHT Q-76 markssparsity

E-commerce — ridge ke 200 coefficients non-zero kyun

An e-commerce company has 200 features for purchase prediction, but only a few are actually relevant; a data scientist uses ridge regression and finds that all 200 coefficients remain non-zero. Explain why ridge does not produce sparsity, state which regularizer should be tried instead.

Hint

  • "200 features, few relevant" — Lecture-5 ke tajurbe ka hubahu scenario. Slide ke numbers likh dein (ridge 200, lasso 35).
  • "Explain why" → derivative likhein: d(θ²)/dθ = 2θ → 0. Ye ek line poora dil hai.

English — likhein

1. Why ridge gives no sparsity. The penalty is Σθj2, whose per-coefficient derivative is 2θj — which itself tends to 0 as θj → 0. So the push toward zero vanishes exactly where it would be needed:

shrink 1.00 -> 0.99 : gain = 1.0000 - 0.9801 = 0.0199 shrink 0.01 -> 0.00 : gain = 0.0001 - 0.0000 = 0.0001 (negligible)

Setting a small coefficient exactly to zero buys almost nothing in penalty but always costs some empirical risk — an unfavourable trade. Hence every coefficient ends up small but non-zero; ridge shrinks, it does not select. Geometrically the ℓ2 ball is round, with no corners on the axes, so the contour touches it where no coordinate is zero. All 200 staying non-zero is expected, not a bug.

2. Use the lasso — the ℓ1 / sum-absolute regularizer:

r(theta) = || theta ||_1 = |theta_1| + ... + |theta_d|

Its derivative is ±1 — constant all the way to zero — so the incentive never weakens, and the kink at 0 pins the coefficient exactly there. ℓ1 is a sparsifying regularizer and performs automatic feature selection. The lecture's experiment on this exact setting (50 points, 200 features): lasso 35 non-zero, ridge all 200.

Practical: standardize first; do not regularize the offset; λ by validation. Lasso has no closed form but is convex, so it computes efficiently. If relevant features are correlated, use elastic net.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

1. Ridge sparsity kyun nahi deta. Penalty Σθj2 hai, aur har coefficient ke lehaz se uska derivative 2θj — jo θj → 0 hone par khud 0 ho jaata hai. Yaani zero ki taraf dhakka waheen khatam ho jaata hai jahan zaroorat thi:

1.00 -> 0.99 karein : faida = 1.0000 - 0.9801 = 0.0199 0.01 -> 0.00 karein : faida = 0.0001 - 0.0000 = 0.0001 (kuch nahi)

Chhote coefficient ko bilkul zero karne se penalty mein taqreeban koi faida nahi, magar empirical risk mein hamesha nuqsan — sauda ghaate ka. Is liye har coefficient chhota magar non-zero reh jaata hai; ridge dabaata hai, chunta nahi. Geometry: ℓ2 ka ilaaqa gol hai, axes par koi kon nahi, to contour aise point par chhuta hai jahan koi coordinate zero nahi. Saare 200 non-zero rehna tawaqqo ke mutabiq hai, kharabi nahi.

2. Lasso use karein — ℓ1 / sum-absolute regularizer:

r(theta) = || theta ||_1 = |theta_1| + ... + |theta_d|

Iska derivative ±1 hai — zero tak constant — to dhakka kamzor nahi hota, aur 0 par kink coefficient ko waheen pakad leta hai. ℓ1 sparsifying regularizer hai aur khud-kaar feature selection karta hai. Lecture ka tajurba isi soorat par (50 points, 200 features): lasso 35 non-zero, ridge saare 200.

Amali: pehle standardize karein; offset ko regularize na karein; λ validation se. Lasso ka closed form nahi magar convex hai, to efficiently hal ho jaata hai. Mutaalliq features correlated hon to elastic net.

OHT Q-86 marksconstant predictors

Food delivery — mean, median, aur late deliveries

A food delivery company wants to predict delivery time, first fits a constant predictor to the delivery times, and the manager says that late deliveries are much worse than early deliveries. State what constant predictor results from squared loss, absolute loss, and which loss you would use if late deliveries are to be penalized more.

Hint — teesra hissa hi asal imtihaan hai

  • Pehle do muft: square → mean, absolute → median.
  • Teesre mein nishaan check karein, andaza na lagayein: model ne kaha 20, khaana aaya 35 → r = 20 − 35 = −15 → late = under-estimate = r < 0 → τ > ½.
  • Aqal se tasdeeq: late mehnga karein to model lamba waqt batayega; ooncha τ = ooncha quantile = lamba waqt. ✓

English — likhein

1. Squared loss → the mean.

L'(theta) = (2/n) SUM (theta - y_i) = 0 => theta = (1/n) SUM y_i

At this optimum the MSE equals the variance of the delivery times.

2. Absolute loss → the median. ℒ(θ) is convex and piecewise linear with kinks at the data values; by the left/right derivative conditions θ is optimal iff n1/n ≤ ½ and n2/n ≤ ½ — i.e. iff θ is a median. (For even n it is not unique: any θ in [yn/2, yn/2+1] works.) The median is the robust choice.

3. If lateness must cost more: tilted absolute loss with τ > ½ (quantile regression).

Sign first: with r = ŷ − y, late means actual exceeded predicted, so y > ŷ and r < 0 — late = under-estimation. The tilted penalty charges τ|r| for r < 0 and (1−τ)|r| for r ≥ 0, so τ > ½ makes lateness the expensive error. With τ = 0.9 a 10-minute lateness costs 9 against 1 for earliness.

The optimal constant is then the τ-quantile — at τ = 0.9, the 90th percentile, i.e. a deliberately longer quoted time so only ~10% arrive late. τ becomes a business dial: the manager picks the acceptable lateness rate.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

1. Squared loss → MEAN.

L'(theta) = (2/n) SUM (theta - y_i) = 0 => theta = (1/n) SUM y_i

Us optimum par MSE, delivery times ka variance hota hai.

2. Absolute loss → MEDIAN. ℒ(θ) convex aur piecewise linear hai (kinks data values par); left/right derivative ki shart se θ optimal hai agar aur sirf agar n1/n ≤ ½ aur n2/n ≤ ½ — yaani θ median ho. (Juft n par unique nahi: [yn/2, yn/2+1] mein koi bhi.) Median robust chunaav hai.

3. Late mehnga karna hai to: tilted absolute loss with τ > ½ (quantile regression).

Pehle nishaan: r = ŷ − y ke saath, late ka matlab asal waqt andaze se zyada, yaani y > ŷ, yaani r < 0 — late = under-estimate. Tilted penalty r < 0 par τ|r| leta hai aur r ≥ 0 par (1−τ)|r|, to τ > ½ late hone ko mehnga bana deta hai. τ = 0.9 par 10 minute late ki qeemat 9, early ki sirf 1.

Behtareen constant phir τ-quantile hai — τ = 0.9 par 90th percentile, yaani jaan boojh kar lamba waqt bataya jaata hai taake sirf ~10% late hon. τ ek business knob ban jaata hai: manager tay kare kitna late hona qubool hai.

OHT Q-96 marksrobust penalties

Robotics — linear aur logarithmic growth kyun madad karti hai

A robotics team predicts joint angles from sensor readings, and some sensor readings are outliers due to electromagnetic interference, so squared loss produces a poor fit. Explain why robust penalty functions are useful here and why linear or logarithmic growth for large errors helps in the presence of outliers.

Hint — Q-6 jaisa lagta hai, magar poocha kuch aur gaya hai

  • Q-6 poochta tha "kaunsa loss". Q-9 poochta hai "kyun linear ya logarithmic growth madad karti hai" — yaani mechanism par marks hain, naam par nahi.
  • "linear OR logarithmic" — do cheezein. Dono alag samjhayein: linear = zor ruk jaata hai; logarithmic = zor ghatta hai. Lafz "growth" = derivative.
  • Extra mark: log-Huber convex nahi hai — ye bohat kam log likhte hain.

English — likhein

Why robust penalties are useful. The EMI readings were not produced by the physical process being modelled — they carry no information about the true joint-angle relationship. But under square loss each point's gradient pull is p′(r) = 2r, which is unbounded, so one interference spike dominates the average loss and ERM drags θ toward it, corrupting predictions for all the valid readings. A robust penalty bounds (or shrinks) the influence of a large residual, letting ERM give up on the few corrupted points and fit the bulk accurately.

Why linear growth helps (Huber). Beyond α the penalty is affine, so its derivative saturates at the constant 2α:

p'_sqr = 2r p'_hub = 2 alpha (alpha = 1) r = 1 2 2 r = 10 20 2 -> 10x weaker r = 50 100 2 -> 50x weaker

Each outlier exerts a bounded, equal pull — a reading wrong by 50° pulls no harder than one wrong by 1°. Influence is capped, so the many valid readings govern the fit. Huber is also convex and C1, so the global minimum is unique.

Why logarithmic growth helps even more (log-Huber). Its derivative is 2α²/r, which decreases toward 0:

r = 1 -> p'_dh = 2.00 r = 10 -> p'_dh = 0.20 r = 100 -> p'_dh = 0.02

This is the diminishing incremental penalty: the further out a point is, the less it matters, so gross outliers are effectively ignored — the right inference for an EMI spike, since a 50° error is certainly interference, not physics.

Trade-off: log-Huber is not convex (p″ < 0 beyond α), so ERM may have local minima and the answer can depend on initialisation. Use Huber first and, if needed, initialise log-Huber from its solution. Pick α by validation, near the scale of genuine sensor noise.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

Robust penalties kyun kaam ki hain. EMI wali readings us physical process se aayi hi nahi jisay model kar rahe hain — un mein asal rishte ki koi information nahi. Magar square loss ke saath har point ka zor p′(r) = 2r hai, jo bay-had hai, to ek spike poore average loss par qabza kar leta hai aur ERM θ usi taraf kheench leta hai — jis se saari sahi readings kharab ho jaati hain. Robust penalty bade residual ke asar ko mehdood kar deti hai, to ERM chand kharab points chhod kar baqi theek fit karta hai.

Linear growth kyun madad karti hai (Huber). α ke baad penalty seedhi line hai, to derivative 2α par ruk jaata hai:

p'_sqr = 2r p'_hub = 2 alpha (alpha = 1) r = 1 2 2 r = 10 20 2 -> 10 guna kam r = 50 100 2 -> 50 guna kam

Har outlier mehdood aur barabar zor lagata hai — 50° ghalti wali reading utna hi kheenchti hai jitna 1° wali. Asar capped ho gaya, to fit ka faisla bohat si sahi readings ke haath mein. Huber convex aur smooth bhi hai, to global minimum ek hi.

Logarithmic growth aur zyada kyun madad karti hai (log-Huber). Iska derivative 2α²/r hai, jo 0 ki taraf ghatta hai:

r = 1 -> p'_dh = 2.00 r = 10 -> p'_dh = 0.20 r = 100 -> p'_dh = 0.02

Yehi diminishing incremental penalty hai: point jitna door, utna kam ahem — to shadeed outliers taqreeban nazar-andaaz ho jaate hain. EMI spike ke liye ye theek natija hai, kyunki 50° ki ghalti yaqeenan interference hai, physics nahi.

Trade-off: log-Huber convex nahi hai (α ke baad p″ < 0), to local minima ho sakte hain aur jawab shuruati guess par depend kar sakta hai. Pehle Huber, aur zaroorat par usi jawab ko log-Huber ka starting point banayein. α validation se, asli sensor noise ke paimane par.

OHT Q-106 marksk-NN + scaling

Hospital — k = 1 kyun overfit karta hai

A hospital uses a 1-nearest neighbor classifier to predict disease from 10 symptoms; it performs perfectly on training data but poorly on new patients, and the features have very different scales, such as age in years and fever in Celsius. Why k = 1 may overfit?

Hint — sawal chhota, scenario mein 3 hints

  • Likha sawal chhota hai magar 6 marks hain — to scenario ki saari baatein jawab mein chahiye.
  • "perfectly on training" → zero training error khaali hai. "age in years and fever in Celsius" → standardization (hisaab likhein!). "10 symptoms" → curse of dimensionality.
  • Chamakdar nukta: Celsius ki jagah Fahrenheit se predictions badal jaati hain — unit par depend karne wala model kharab hai.

English — likhein

1. The perfect training score is vacuous. Each point is its own nearest neighbour (distance 0), so ŷ(xi) = yi and training error is 0 for any loss. This follows from the predictor's definition, not from learning — a lookup table is always perfect on its own entries — so it says nothing about generalization.

2. Why k = 1 overfits.

  • Maximum variance — each prediction rests on one point, so there is no averaging to suppress label noise; one misdiagnosed patient corrupts its whole Voronoi cell.
  • Maximum sensitivity — the predictor is a step function, so a tiny change in symptoms can flip the diagnosis. Sensitive predictors generalize poorly.
  • Curse of dimensionality — with 10 symptoms distances concentrate, so the "nearest" neighbour is not meaningfully near.

3. The unscaled features make it worse. 1-NN has no parameters to compensate — the distance is the model. Two patients:

A: age 25, fever 37 C B: age 45, fever 40 C squared distance = (45-25)^2 + (40-37)^2 = 400 + 9 = 409 age contributes 400/409 = 97.8 % fever contributes 9/409 = 2.2 %

So "nearest patient" means "closest in age", and a clinically decisive 3°C fever is almost invisible. Worse, it is unit-dependent: in Fahrenheit the same 3°C becomes 5.4°F, contributing 29.16/429.16 = 6.8% — the predictions change with the chosen unit, which alone shows the model is unsound.

4. Fix: standardize every feature (training-set statistics only, to avoid leakage); raise k or use soft-NN with bandwidth β; select k or β by validation, never by training error (which always picks k = 1); and use feature selection for the 10 dimensions.

Roman Urdu — samajhne ke liye

1. Training par perfect hona khaali hai. Har point apna hi nearest neighbour hai (faasla 0), to ŷ(xi) = yi aur training error kisi bhi loss ke saath 0. Ye model ki tareef se nikla, seekhne se nahi — lookup table apne hi khaano par hamesha 100% hoti hai — to is se generalization ka kuch pata nahi chalta.

2. k = 1 overfit kyun karta hai.

  • Variance maximum — har jawab ek point par tika hai, label ka shor dabane ka zariya nahi; ek ghalat diagnosis apne poore ilaaqe (Voronoi cell) ko kharab kar deta hai.
  • Sensitivity maximum — prediction step function hai, symptoms mein zara si tabdeeli par bimaari palat sakti hai. Sensitive predictors generalize nahi karte.
  • Curse of dimensionality — 10 symptoms ke saath faasle jama ho jaate hain, to "sab se qareeb" point waqai qareeb nahi hota.

3. Un-scaled features is ko bigad dete hain. 1-NN mein muaawza karne ke liye koi parameter nahi — faasla hi model hai. Do patients:

A: age 25, fever 37 C B: age 45, fever 40 C squared distance = (45-25)^2 + (40-37)^2 = 400 + 9 = 409 age ka hissa = 400/409 = 97.8 % fever ka hissa = 9/409 = 2.2 %

Yaani "sab se qareeb patient" ka matlab "hum-umar" ban gaya, aur 3°C bukhaar — jo medically faisla-kun hai — taqreeban nazar nahi aa raha. Aur ye unit par depend karta hai: Fahrenheit mein wohi 3°C = 5.4°F, hissa 29.16/429.16 = 6.8% — sirf unit badalne se predictions badal gayin, aur yehi saboot kaafi hai ke model theek nahi.

4. Ilaaj: har feature standardize karein (sirf training set ki statistics se, warna leakage); k barhayein ya soft-NN use karein β ke saath; k / β ka chunaav validation se, training error se hargiz nahi (wo hamesha k = 1 chunega); aur 10 dimensions ke liye feature selection.

Ye page kaise bana. OHT sample paper aur chaaron quiz ka pattern nikaala (scenario + ek technical sawal), phir wohi tareeqa lectures ke baqi topics par lagaya. Yaani: wo sawal jo abhi nahi aaye magar isi style mein aa sakte hain.

Kaise padhein: sawal English mein hai (asli paper bhi English mein aayega), aur uske neeche hare khaane mein Roman Urdu mein likha hai ke sawal kya poochh raha hai. Jawab + ke peeche hai — bayein English, dayein Roman Urdu. Pehle khud likhein, phir kholein. Aakhir mein poora mock paper hai.

Pehle ye

Kaun sa topic kitna aayega

Ginti 14 asli sawalon (10 OHT + 4 quiz) se nikali gayi hai. Andaza hai, zamanat nahi — magar padhne ki tarteeb tay karne ke liye kaafi.

Set 1 — 8 sawal

Learning type pehchanna

P-1.16 marks

A telecom operator has 18 months of call records for 2 million subscribers, with no indication of who later cancelled. Management wants natural groupings of usage behaviour to design tariff bundles. Identify the learning type. Would the answer change if a "cancelled" column were supplied?

Sawal ka matlab: Telecom company ke paas 20 lakh customers ka 18 mahine ka data hai, magar kisi par label nahi ke kaun baad mein chhod gaya. Unhein customers ke "qudrati groups" chahiye. Batayein kaunsa learning type hai — aur agar "cancelled" wala column bhi de diya jaaye to jawab badlega?

Jawab

English

Unsupervised learning — clustering. No target variable exists; only feature vectors, and the goal is to output hidden structure (the lecture's "market segmentation").

Yes, it would change. With a cancellation column, labelled pairs (xi, yi) exist, so it becomes supervised binary classification (churn prediction).

The rule: clustering invents the groups; classification is given them. Always ask: is there a y?

Roman Urdu

Unsupervised learning — clustering. Koi target (y) mojood nahi; sirf feature vectors hain, aur maqsad data ke andar chhupi structure nikalna hai (lecture ka "market segmentation").

Haan, jawab badal jaayega. "Cancelled" column aa jaane se (xi, yi) ke labelled jode ban jaate hain, to ye supervised binary classification (churn prediction) ho jaayega.

Qanoon: clustering groups khud banata hai; classification ko groups pehle se diye jaate hain. Hamesha poochein: y hai ya nahi?

P-1.26 marks

A robotic arm learns to stack irregular parcels. No human can specify the correct joint trajectory, and the arm gets a score only after the stack completes or collapses. Define <T,P,E>, name the learning type, and identify the difficulty created by the score arriving only at the end.

Sawal ka matlab: Ek robotic arm bay-tarteeb parcels ka dher lagana seekh raha hai. Koi insaan sahi harkat bata nahi sakta, aur score sirf aakhir mein milta hai (dher ban gaya ya gir gaya). <T,P,E> define karein, learning type batayein, aur ye batayein ke "score sirf aakhir mein milna" se kaunsi khaas mushkil paida hoti hai.

Jawab

English

T: choose at each state an arm action that builds a stable stack — learn a policy.
P: fraction of episodes completed without collapse (or parcels stacked per collapse).
E: the arm's own (state, action, reward, next state) sequences from trial and error.

Type: reinforcement learning — no labelled correct trajectories can exist, and feedback is a reward not an answer.

The difficulty: the credit assignment problem. The reward is delayed to the end of the episode, so when the stack collapses the agent cannot tell which of the many actions caused it.

Roman Urdu

T: har state par aisi harkat chunna jo mazboot dher banaye — yaani ek policy seekhna.
P: kitne percent koshishein bagair girne mukammal huin (ya ek girne par kitne parcels lage).
E: arm ka khud ka tajurba — (state, action, reward, agli state) ki sequences, trial-and-error se.

Qism: reinforcement learning — sahi harkat ke labels mojood ho hi nahi sakte, aur feedback ek reward hai, jawab nahi.

Mushkil: credit assignment problem. Reward der se (aakhir mein) milta hai, to jab dher girta hai to agent ko pata nahi chalta ke kaun si harkat ne nuqsan kiya — pehli ya aakhri.

P-1.36 marks

A pharmacy chain observes P(antacid | painkiller) = 0.62. A junior analyst proposes training a classifier with "bought antacid" as the label. Is the analyst wrong, and when is each framing correct?

Sawal ka matlab: Pharmacy ko mila ke P(antacid | painkiller) = 0.62. Ek junior analyst kehta hai "antacid khareeda" ko label bana kar classifier train karein. Kya wo ghalat hai? Aur kaunsi framing kab theek hoti hai?

Jawab

English

Not wrong — the two framings answer different questions.

Association analysis fits the stated observation: 0.62 is a rule's confidence, computed from transactions with no target column. It answers "which items co-occur?" and yields rules over all item pairs at once.

Supervised classification becomes correct if the question changes to "will this customer buy an antacid?", which needs per-customer labels and can use features rules cannot (demographics, time of day).

The test: association analysis is item-centric and label-free; classification is record-centric and personalises. A fixed 0.62 applies to everyone identically.

Roman Urdu

Ghalat nahi hai — dono framings alag alag sawal ka jawab deti hain.

Association analysis is observation par fit hoti hai: 0.62 ek rule ka confidence hai, jo transactions se nikla hai jahan koi target column nahi. Ye batata hai "kaun se items sath aate hain?" aur saari item jodiyon ke rules ek saath deta hai.

Supervised classification tab theek hogi jab sawal badal jaaye: "ye khaas customer antacid khareedega?" — us ke liye per-customer labels chahiye, aur phir aise features bhi use ho sakte hain jo rules nahi kar sakte (umar, waqt).

Pehchan: association analysis items par markoz hai aur usay label nahi chahiye; classification records par markoz hai aur har banday ke liye alag jawab deti hai. Ek pakka 0.62 sab par ek jaisa lagta hai.

P-1.46 marks

A startup has 200,000 chest X-rays but only 1,800 annotated by radiologists. Which paradigm applies, and why is discarding the unlabelled images a poor decision?

Sawal ka matlab: 2,00,000 chest X-ray hain magar sirf 1,800 par radiologist ne label lagaya hai. Kaunsa paradigm hai, aur baqi bagair-label tasveerein phenk dena kyun bura faisla hai?

Jawab

English

Semi-supervised learning — "training data + a few desired outputs". The most common real situation, because labelling is expensive.

Why not discard them:

  • 1,800 X-rays against very high image dimensionality means d ≫ n → severe overfitting.
  • The unlabelled images still carry the structure of p(x) — usable via self-supervised pre-training, pseudo-labelling or consistency regularization.
  • They reveal whether the 1,800 are representative; if from one scanner, the labelled subset is distribution-shifted relative to deployment.

Fix: self-supervised pre-training on all 200,000 + fine-tuning on the 1,800, plus active learning to pick what to annotate next.

Roman Urdu

Semi-supervised learning — "training data + thoray se labels". Haqeeqat mein ye sab se aam soorat hai, kyunki labelling mehngi hoti hai.

Kyun na phenkein:

  • 1,800 tasveerein aur image ka dimension bohat bara → d ≫ n → shadeed overfitting.
  • Bagair-label tasveeron mein bhi p(x) ki structure mojood hai — yaani "chest kaisi dikhti hai" — aur wo self-supervised pre-training, pseudo-labelling ya consistency regularization se istemaal ho sakti hai.
  • Wo ye bhi batati hain ke 1,800 waqai numainda (representative) hain ya nahi. Agar wo ek hi scanner se hain to labelled hissa deployment se khisak chuka hai.

Hal: saari 2,00,000 par self-supervised pre-training, phir 1,800 par fine-tuning; aur active learning se chunein ke agla kaunsa X-ray label karwana sab se faidemand hai.

P-1.56 marks

State the learning type and output form (point / probability / confidence band / generative) for: (a) flagging unusual card transactions; (b) probability of each of 10 diagnoses; (c) synthetic patient records for testing; (d) forecasting tomorrow's peak electricity demand.

Sawal ka matlab: Chaar kaamon ke liye learning type aur jawab ki shakal batayein: (a) ajeeb card transactions pakadna, (b) 10 bimaariyon ki probability, (c) nakli patient records (testing ke liye), (d) kal ki peak bijli ki talab ka andaza.

Jawab

English

(a) Unsupervised (anomaly detection); output a probability / score — the fraud team must rank alerts and set a threshold against staffing.

(b) Supervised multi-class; output a probability estimate — a single label discards the risk information a doctor needs.

(c) Unsupervised; output a generative model — the purpose is sampling.

(d) Supervised regression, specifically forecasting; output a confidence band — a grid operator sizes reserve against the upper end.

Extra: (d) is time-ordered, so no random split — use temporal validation; and an asymmetric (tilted) loss fits, since under-predicting demand causes blackouts.

Roman Urdu

(a) Unsupervised (anomaly detection); jawab probability / score ho — fraud team ko alerts tarteeb mein lagane hain aur apni afradi quwwat ke hisaab se had (threshold) tay karni hai.

(b) Supervised multi-class; jawab probability ho — ek akela label doctor ko risk ki wo information nahi deta jo usay chahiye.

(c) Unsupervised; jawab generative model — kyunki maqsad hi naya data banana hai.

(d) Supervised regression, khaas tor par forecasting; jawab confidence band ho — grid wale reserve capacity us range ke ooper wale sire se naapte hain.

Extra: (d) waqt ke saath juda hua hai, to random split nahi — temporal validation. Aur asymmetric (tilted) loss munasib hai, kyunki talab kam batana blackout ka sabab banta hai.

P-1.66 marks

A university predicts which students will fail. Students study in groups and group members' outcomes are strongly correlated, but each student is treated independently. Which assumption is violated, which technique do the lectures prescribe, and how does it differ from transfer learning?

Sawal ka matlab: University andaza lagana chahti hai kaun fail hoga. Students groups mein padhte hain aur ek group ke natije ek doosre se bohat jure hue hain, magar model har student ko alag samajh raha hai. Kaunsa assumption toota, lectures kya tareeqa batate hain, aur wo transfer learning se kaise mukhtalif hai?

Jawab

English

Violated: independence — the first "i" in i.i.d. Group members are not independent draws. (Identical distribution is fine — same cohort.)

Prescribed: collective classification — predict linked students jointly rather than in isolation.

The distinction: collective classification fixes broken independence (points are linked); transfer learning fixes broken identical distribution (test data from elsewhere). Trigger words: "outcomes correlated / same group" vs "trained on X, deployed in Y".

Also: split validation data by group, or correlated members leak across the split.

Roman Urdu

Toota: independence — i.i.d. ka pehla "i". Ek group ke members azaad draws nahi hain. (Identical distribution theek hai — sab ek hi batch ke hain.)

Lectures ka tareeqa: collective classification — jure hue students ka faisla mila kar karein, alag alag nahi.

Farq: collective classification toote hue independence ka ilaaj hai (points ek doosre se jure); transfer learning toote hue identical distribution ka (test data kahin aur se aaya). Trigger words: "outcomes correlated / same group" vs "trained on X, deployed in Y".

Aur: validation data group ke hisaab se baantein, warna ek group ke jure hue members split ke dono taraf chale jaayenge (leakage).

P-1.76 marks

A payroll department asks for an ML model to compute tax deductions. The rules are published in the tax code. Is ML appropriate, and what principle decides when learning is warranted?

Sawal ka matlab: Payroll department ML model maang raha hai jo tax deduction nikaale. Magar qawaid tax code mein likhe hue hain. Kya ML munasib hai? Aur wo usool kya hai jo tay karta hai ke seekhne ki zaroorat kab hai?

Jawab

English

No — the lecture's own counter-example: "there is no need to learn to calculate payroll." The rule is known, exact and published, so write a deterministic program.

Why ML would be worse: it only approximates an exact rule, errs on rare salary ranges, needs retraining whenever the law changes (instead of editing one line), and is unauditable — a legal requirement for tax.

The principle: ML is warranted when the rule is not known — human expertise does not exist (Mars), humans cannot explain it (speech), models must be personalised (medicine), or data is huge (genomics). Payroll meets none.

Nuance: detecting payroll fraud or data-entry errors would suit ML, since no exact rule exists there.

Roman Urdu

Nahi — ye lecture ka apna counter-example hai: "payroll calculate karne ke liye seekhne ki koi zaroorat nahi." Qanoon maaloom, pakka aur likha hua hai, to seedha program likhein.

ML kyun bura hoga: wo ek pakke qanoon ka sirf andaza lagayega, kam aane wali salary ranges par ghalti karega, qanoon badalne par dobara train karna padega (ek line badalne ke bajaye), aur uska hisaab (audit) nahi ho sakega — jo tax ke liye qanooni zaroorat hai.

Usool: ML wahan chahiye jahan qanoon maaloom na ho — insaani expertise mojood na ho (Mars), insaan samjha na sake (speech), har banday ke liye alag model chahiye (medicine), ya data pahaad jitna ho (genomics). Payroll in mein se kisi par poora nahi utarta.

Baareek baat: payroll mein fraud ya data-entry ki ghalti pakadna ML ke liye munasib hai, kyunki wahan koi pakka qanoon mojood nahi.

P-1.86 marks

Decompose into the three components every ML algorithm has: (a) a decision tree trained by information gain, judged by error rate; (b) lasso regression.

Sawal ka matlab: Do algorithms ko un teen hisson mein toudein jo har ML algorithm mein hote hain: (a) decision tree (information gain se train, error rate se naapa), (b) lasso regression.

Jawab

English

The three components are Representation, Optimization, Evaluation.

(a) Decision tree. Representation: a symbolic function — a flowchart of threshold tests ("is feature 2 greater than 3.5?"). Optimization: divide and conquer — greedy recursive tree induction. Evaluation: entropy / information gain internally, error rate externally.

(b) Lasso. Representation: a numerical function θTx over the feature map φ. Optimization: convex optimization of ℒ(θ) + λ‖θ‖1 — no closed form, but convex so computed efficiently. Evaluation: squared error + the ℓ1 penalty; out-of-sample MSE, with λ by validation.

Why it matters: tens of thousands of algorithms exist, but every one decomposes this way — so an unfamiliar algorithm can always be understood by asking these three questions.

Roman Urdu

Teen hisse hain: Representation, Optimization, Evaluation.

(a) Decision tree. Representation: ek symbolic function — sawalon ka flowchart ("kya feature 2, 3.5 se bara hai?"). Optimization: divide and conquer — tree ko qadam ba qadam (greedy) banana. Evaluation: andar entropy / information gain, bahar error rate.

(b) Lasso. Representation: ek numerical function θTx, feature map φ ke oopar. Optimization: ℒ(θ) + λ‖θ‖1 ki convex optimization — closed-form formula nahi, magar convex hai to efficiently hal ho jaata hai. Evaluation: squared error + ℓ1 penalty; aur bahar out-of-sample MSE, λ validation se.

Ye kyun ahem hai: hazaaron algorithms hain, magar har ek isi tarah tootta hai — to kisi bhi anjaan algorithm ko ye teen sawal poochh kar samjha ja sakta hai.

Set 2 — 6 sawal

i.i.d., validation aur data leakage

P-2.16 marks

A hospital predicts post-operative complications. The dataset has multiple admissions for the same patients. A random 80/20 split gives 94% accuracy; a pilot on new patients gives 71%. Explain the failure and state the correct splitting strategy.

Sawal ka matlab: Hospital operation ke baad ki pechidagiyon ka andaza lagata hai. Data mein ek hi patient ke kai admissions hain. Random 80/20 split par 94% accuracy, magar naye patients par sirf 71%. Ye kyun hua, aur split ka theek tareeqa kya hai?

Jawab

English

Mechanism: identity leakage (broken independence). A random split puts some admissions of patient X in training and others in test. The model need not learn clinical relationships — it can recognise individual patients and recall their outcomes. So 94% partly measures memorisation of patients.

At deployment the patient is genuinely new, that memorised information is gone, and accuracy falls to the true level of ~71%. Both numbers are correct; only 71% answers the deployment question.

Fix: grouped (patient-level) splitting — all admissions of any patient on one side only (GroupKFold with patient as group).

General rule: split at the level of the entity you must generalize to — new patients → by patient; new hospitals → by hospital.

Roman Urdu

Wajah: identity leakage (independence toot gaya). Random split patient X ke kuch admissions training mein aur kuch test mein daal deta hai. Ab model ko medical rishte seekhne ki zaroorat hi nahi — wo banday ko pehchan kar uska natija yaad se bata deta hai. To 94% mein bara hissa "patients yaad kar lena" hai.

Deployment par patient waqai naya hota hai, wo yaad kiya hua ilm kaam nahi aata, aur accuracy asal darje (~71%) par gir jaati hai. Dono numbers theek hain; magar deployment ke sawal ka jawab sirf 71% hai.

Hal: grouped (patient-level) splitting — kisi bhi patient ke saare admissions sirf ek taraf (GroupKFold, group = patient).

Aam qanoon: split us cheez ke darje par karein jis par generalize karna hai — naye patients → patient ke hisaab se; naye hospitals → hospital ke hisaab se.

P-2.26 marks

A data scientist standardizes using the mean and standard deviation of the entire dataset, then splits, then fits. Identify the error, why it inflates the score, and the correct procedure.

Sawal ka matlab: Ek banda poore data (train + test) ke mean aur std deviation se standardize karta hai, phir split karta hai, phir model fit karta hai. Ghalti kya hai, score kyun barh jaata hai, aur theek tareeqa kya hai?

Jawab

English

Error: preprocessing leakage. The scaling statistics were computed over all data including the test set, so a summary of the test set has entered the training pipeline.

Why it inflates: the test set is no longer unseen. The effect is mild with large n but substantial when n is small, when test outliers shift the global mean, or with stronger steps (target encoding, PCA, imputation, feature selection fitted on all data). Decisively: at deployment no statistics of future data can be known, so the procedure cannot be reproduced in production.

Correct: split first → fit preprocessing on training only → apply those stored statistics to validation/test → refit within each CV fold. Use a pipeline so it cannot be forgotten.

One-line rule: anything that learns from data is part of the model and belongs inside the training fold.

Roman Urdu

Ghalti: preprocessing leakage. Scaling ke numbers (mean, std) poore data se nikale gaye, test set bhi shamil — to test set ka khulasa training pipeline mein ghus gaya.

Score kyun barhta hai: test set ab "unseen" nahi raha. Bade n par asar halka hota hai, magar jab n chhota ho, ya test ke outliers global mean khisaka dein, ya zyada taqatwar steps hon (target encoding, PCA, imputation, feature selection poore data par) — to asar bohat bara hota hai. Faisla-kun baat: deployment par future data ke numbers maaloom ho hi nahi sakte, to ye tareeqa production mein dohraya ja hi nahi sakta.

Theek tareeqa: pehle split → preprocessing sirf training par fit → wohi mehfooz kiye hue numbers validation/test par lagayein → aur har CV fold ke andar dobara fit karein. Pipeline use karein taake bhoolna mumkin na ho.

Ek line ka qanoon: jo cheez bhi data se seekhti hai wo model ka hissa hai, aur usay training fold ke andar hona chahiye.

P-2.36 marks

An engineer tries 400 values of λ, picks the lowest held-out error, and reports that as expected performance. Why is the figure biased, and what is the correct protocol?

Sawal ka matlab: Ek engineer λ ki 400 values aazmata hai, jis par held-out error sab se kam aaya usay chun leta hai, aur wohi error report kar deta hai. Ye number kyun jhoota (biased) hai, aur theek protocol kya hai?

Jawab

English

Why biased: the held-out set was used for selection, so it is no longer unseen. Taking the minimum over 400 noisy estimates selects partly for genuine merit and partly for favourable noise on that set, so the minimum sits systematically below the true expected error. This is overfitting to the validation set, and it worsens with more trials.

Correct protocol — three-way split: training fits θ; validation chooses λ; test is touched once at the end and takes part in no decision. With limited data use nested cross-validation.

Also: 400 values is excessive — a coarse log grid reduces selection bias; and per the lectures take λ slightly larger than the minimiser.

Roman Urdu

Kyun jhoota hai: us held-out set se chunaav kiya gaya, to wo ab "unseen" nahi raha. 400 shor-aalood (noisy) andazon mein se sab se kam uthana — iska matlab aap ne wo λ chuna jo thodi asli khoobi aur thoda us set par acha ittefaq rakhta tha. Is liye wo minimum asal error se hamesha neeche baithta hai. Isi ko "validation set par overfitting" kehte hain, aur jitne zyada try karein ye utna bigadta hai.

Theek protocol — teen hissay: training se θ fit; validation se λ chunein; aur test ko sirf ek baar aakhir mein dekhein — wo kisi faisle mein shamil na ho. Data kam ho to nested cross-validation.

Aur: 400 values bohat zyada hain — mota (coarse) log grid kaafi hai aur bias kam karta hai; aur lectures ke mutabiq λ minimum se thoda bara lein.

P-2.46 marks

A credit model trained on 2019–2021 with a random split scored well but failed in 2023. Two explanations: (i) i.i.d. violated, (ii) overfitting. Evaluate both and say what evidence distinguishes them.

Sawal ka matlab: 2019–2021 ke data par bana credit model random split par acha chala, magar 2023 mein fail ho gaya. Do wajuhat batai gayi hain: (i) i.i.d. toota, (ii) overfitting. Dono par raye dein, aur batayein kaunse saboot se farq pata chalega.

Jawab

English

(i) Distribution shift — likely the main cause. Lending conditions changed (rates, employment, applicant mix), so "identically distributed" fails. If the same income now implies different risk, that is concept drift, worse than covariate shift. The random split also leaked time, so extrapolation was never tested.

(ii) Overfitting — possible but would show differently. Had it been the sole cause, the random-split test score would already have been poor, since those test points came from the same period.

Distinguishing evidence:

  • Training vs random-split test gap within 2019–2021 → a large gap means overfitting.
  • Re-validate temporally (train 2019–20, test 2021) → a sharp drop means shift.
  • Compare feature distributions 2019–21 vs 2023 → visible movement confirms covariate shift.
  • Error over time → overfitting gives constant elevated error; drift gives error that grows.

Most likely both: a somewhat overfitted model, masked by a leaky split, then exposed by genuine shift.

Roman Urdu

(i) Distribution shift — ghaaliban asal wajah. Qarz dene ke haalat badal gaye (interest rates, rozgaar, aane walon ki qism), to "identically distributed" toot gaya. Agar wohi income ab mukhtalif risk batati hai to ye concept drift hai, jo covariate shift se bhi bura hai. Random split ne waqt bhi leak kar diya, to mustaqbil mein chhalaang (extrapolation) kabhi test hi nahi hui.

(ii) Overfitting — mumkin hai, magar uski nishani alag hoti. Agar sirf yehi wajah hoti, to random-split ka test score bhi pehle se bura hota, kyunki wo test points usi arse ke the.

Farq batane wale saboot:

  • Training aur random-split test ka faasla 2019–2021 ke andar → bara faasla = overfitting.
  • Temporal validation dobara karein (train 2019–20, test 2021) → score bohat gir jaaye = shift.
  • Feature distributions ka moazna 2019–21 vs 2023 → saaf tabdeeli = covariate shift ki tasdeeq.
  • Waqt ke saath error → overfitting mein error pakka ooncha rehta hai; drift mein error barhta jaata hai.

Ghaaliban dono: thoda overfit model, jo leaky split ne chhupa diya, aur phir asli shift ne nanga kar diya.

P-2.56 marks

State the i.i.d. assumption, then for each say which part fails and what is prescribed: (a) predicting a user's next song; (b) digits trained on US postal codes, tested on Pakistani ones; (c) flood risk for houses on the same street.

Sawal ka matlab: i.i.d. assumption theek theek likhein, phir teeno ke liye batayein kaunsa hissa tootta hai aur ilaaj kya hai: (a) user ka agla gaana, (b) digits — US postal codes par train, Pakistani par test, (c) ek hi gali ke gharon ka flood risk.

Jawab

English

Assumption: training and test examples are drawn independently from the same distribution — two separate claims.

(a) Next song → independence fails: consecutive plays are sequentially dependent (and that dependence is the signal); tastes also drift. Prescription: sequence modelling + temporal validation, never random.

(b) US → Pakistan digits → identical distribution fails: handwriting conventions, pen, paper, scanner differ. Task unchanged → covariate shift. Prescription: transfer learning — fine-tune on Pakistani samples, augment; validate on Pakistani data only.

(c) Same street → independence fails: flood risk is spatially autocorrelated (shared elevation, drainage). Prescription: collective classification, and split by street/catchment, not by house.

Roman Urdu

Assumption: training aur test ke points azaadana (independently) aur ek hi distribution se uthaye gaye hain — ye do alag daawe hain.

(a) Agla gaana → independence tootta hai: lagatar gaane ek doosre se jure hue hain (aur yehi juraav asal signal hai); pasand bhi waqt ke saath badalti hai. Ilaaj: sequence modelling + temporal validation, random hargiz nahi.

(b) US → Pakistan digits → identical distribution tootta hai: likhne ka andaz, pen, kaaghaz, scanner — sab alag. Kaam wohi hai → covariate shift. Ilaaj: transfer learning — Pakistani samples par fine-tune, augmentation; aur validation sirf Pakistani data par.

(c) Ek hi gali → independence tootta hai: flood risk jagah ke lehaz se juda hua hai (ek jaisi bulandi, ek hi nikaas). Ilaaj: collective classification, aur split gali / ilaaqe ke hisaab se karein, ghar ke hisaab se nahi.

P-2.66 marks

Why does training error always fall as flexibility rises while test error does not? Why does this make training error useless for hyper-parameters? Give two hyper-parameters it would choose wrongly.

Sawal ka matlab: Model ki lachak barhane se training error hamesha kyun girta hai, magar test error nahi? Is se training error hyper-parameters chunne ke liye kyun bay-kaar ho jaata hai? Do aise hyper-parameters batayein jo wo ghalat chunega.

Jawab

English

Training error falls monotonically because more flexibility enlarges the hypothesis space, and the best element of a larger set can never be worse than the best element of a subset. In the limit it reaches 0.

Test error does not, because it has two opposing parts: as flexibility rises bias falls but variance rises (the fit tracks this sample's noise, which will not recur). Hence the U-shape with an interior minimum.

Why useless for selection: being monotone, optimising it always picks the most flexible setting — i.e. maximum overfitting. It contains no information about the variance term, which is the whole thing we must control.

Two examples: k in k-NN — minimised at k = 1 where it is exactly 0; λ in ridge/lasso — minimised at λ = 0, i.e. no regularization at all. (Same for polynomial degree and tree depth.) So: use held-out data.

Roman Urdu

Training error hamesha girta hai kyunki zyada lachak se hypothesis space bara ho jaata hai, aur bade set ka behtareen member chhote set ke behtareen member se bura nahi ho sakta. Aakhir mein wo 0 tak pohonch jaata hai.

Test error nahi girta rehta, kyunki is mein do ulti cheezein hain: lachak barhne se bias girta hai magar variance barhta hai (model isi sample ka shor pakad leta hai, jo naye data mein nahi hoga). Isi liye U ki shakal banti hai, jiska minimum beech mein hota hai.

Chunaav ke liye bay-kaar kyun: kyunki wo hamesha girta hai, usay minimize karne se hamesha sab se lachakdar setting chuni jaati hai — yaani maximum overfitting. Us mein variance ke baare mein koi information hi nahi, jabke qaabu usi par karna hai.

Do misaalein: k-NN ka k — k = 1 par kam se kam, aur wahan wo exactly 0 hota hai; ridge/lasso ka λ — λ = 0 par kam se kam, yaani regularization bilkul nahi. (Wohi haal polynomial degree aur tree depth ka.) To: held-out data use karein.

Set 3 — 7 sawal

Features aur feature engineering

P-3.16 marks

A "city" feature is encoded Karachi = 1, Lahore = 2, Islamabad = 3, Peshawar = 4. What false claim does this impose, what is the correct encoding, and when would integer encoding be legitimate?

Sawal ka matlab: "City" feature ko aise likha gaya: Karachi = 1, Lahore = 2, Islamabad = 3, Peshawar = 4. Is se model par kaunsa jhoota daawa laad diya gaya, theek encoding kya hai, aur integer encoding kab jaayez hoti hai?

Jawab

English

False claim: it imposes an order and a metric on an unordered category. The term is θcity × code, so the model is forced to assert the cities are ordered, that the spacing is equal and additive, and that Peshawar's effect is exactly four times Karachi's. The whole city effect is compressed into one coefficient along an ordering the analyst invented — and relabelling the cities would change the predictions, which proves the encoding is wrong.

Correct: one-hot — one binary indicator per city, so each gets its own free coefficient. (Drop one level if an offset is present.) With only 4 categories this is fine; the objections in B2 apply to large vocabularies.

When integers are legitimate: when categories are genuinely ordinal with roughly equal spacing — education level, a 1–5 rating. Also for trees, which only ask "is code ≤ 2?".

Roman Urdu

Jhoota daawa: bagair tarteeb wali cheez par tarteeb aur faasla laad diya. Model mein term hai θcity × code, to model majboor hai ye kehne par ke sheher tarteeb mein hain, un ka faasla barabar hai, aur Peshawar ka asar Karachi se bilkul chaar guna hai. Poora sheher ka asar ek coefficient mein daba diya gaya, us tarteeb par jo analyst ne khud banáyi — aur sheheron ke numbers badal dein to predictions badal jaayengi, jo saboot hai ke encoding ghalat hai.

Theek: one-hot — har sheher ka apna 0/1 column, to har ek ko apna azaad coefficient mil jaata hai. (Offset mojood ho to ek level chhod dein.) Sirf 4 categories par ye bilkul theek hai; B2 wale aitraaz bade vocabulary par lagte hain.

Integer kab jaayez: jab categories waqai ordinal hon aur faasla taqreeban barabar ho — jaise taleem ka darja, ya 1–5 ki rating. Aur decision trees ke liye bhi, kyunki wo sirf poochte hain "kya code ≤ 2 hai?".

P-3.26 marks

Crop yield rises with rainfall up to about 800 mm then falls from waterlogging. Construct a feature map letting a linear model capture this, show it produces the required shape, and state what property is preserved.

Sawal ka matlab: Fasal ki paidawar baarish ke saath 800 mm tak barhti hai, phir paani jama hone se girne lagti hai. Aisa feature map banayein jo linear model ko ye shakal pakadne de, numbers se dikhayein ke shakal sahi banti hai, aur batayein kaunsi khoobi barqaraar rehti hai.

Jawab

English

The relationship is non-monotonic, which a single coefficient cannot express (one coefficient = one slope).

Hinge feature with a knot at 800:

phi(u) = ( 1 , u , max(0, u - 800) )

With θ2 = +0.05 and θ3 = −0.09:

u < 800 : slope = theta_2 = +0.05 (rising) u > 800 : slope = theta_2 + theta_3 = -0.04 (falling)

Peak at 800 mm, then decline — exactly as described. (A quadratic (1,u,u²) also works but forces a symmetric decline and places the peak wherever the fit likes.)

Property preserved: linear in θ — all non-linearity is inside the fixed map φ, so the risk stays convex and least squares applies unchanged.

Roman Urdu

Rishta non-monotonic hai (pehle barhta, phir girta), aur ek akela coefficient ye bayaan nahi kar sakta (ek coefficient = ek dhalaan).

Hinge feature, knot 800 par:

phi(u) = ( 1 , u , max(0, u - 800) )

θ2 = +0.05 aur θ3 = −0.09 ke saath:

u < 800 : dhalaan = theta_2 = +0.05 (barhti) u > 800 : dhalaan = theta_2 + theta_3 = -0.04 (girti)

800 mm par choti, phir utaar — bilkul wohi shakal jo maangi gayi. (Quadratic (1,u,u²) bhi chal jaata hai magar wo utaar ko symmetric hone par majboor karta hai aur choti kahin bhi rakh deta hai.)

Barqaraar khoobi: θ mein linear — saara tedha-pan pakke map φ ke andar hai, to risk convex rehta hai aur least squares bagair tabdeeli chalta hai.

P-3.36 marks

A says "the model is poor, so we need a neural network." B says "we need better features." The residuals show a clear systematic pattern against one input. Who is right, and why?

Sawal ka matlab: A kehta hai "model bura hai, neural network chahiye". B kehta hai "behtar features chahiye". Residuals mein ek input ke khilaaf saaf pattern dikh raha hai. Kaun theek hai aur kyun?

Jawab

English

B is right, on this evidence.

Why: for a well-specified model residuals should be random around zero. A systematic pattern against an input means the model cannot represent a relationship demonstrably present in the data — the true function is outside the hypothesis space. That is underfitting from an inadequate φ, not an inadequate model family. The residual plot has effectively told you which feature to add.

Why A is premature: a neural network is a non-convex, data-hungry, opaque way to discover what the residual plot already revealed; it adds local minima, hyper-parameters and variance, and discards interpretability for no gain.

When A would be right: if, after feature engineering, residuals stay structured in ways no hand-designed φ can capture — typically images, audio or raw text, with plenty of data.

Correct order: diagnose residuals → fix φ → regularize → only then a richer family.

Roman Urdu

Is saboot ki roshni mein B theek hai.

Wajah: theek bane hue model ke residuals 0 ke aas paas be-tarteeb hone chahiye.
