Kisi input ke khilaaf saaf pattern ka matlab hai ke model wo rishta bayaan hi nahi kar sakta jo data mein saaf mojood hai — yaani asal function hypothesis space ke bahar hai. Ye na-kaafi φ ki wajah se underfitting hai, model ke khandan ki kharabi nahi. Residual plot ne asal mein bata diya hai ke kaunsa feature daalna hai.

A ka mashwara jaldbaazi kyun hai: neural network ek non-convex, data-khor aur na-shaffaf tareeqa hai us cheez ko dhoondne ka jo residual plot ne pehle hi dikha di; wo local minima, naye hyper-parameters aur variance barha deta hai, aur interpretability bay-wajah khatam kar deta hai.

A kab theek hoga: jab feature engineering ke baad bhi residuals mein aisi structure baaqi rahe jo haath se bane φ se pakdi na ja sake — aam tor par images, audio ya raw text par, aur jab data bohat ho.

Theek tarteeb: residuals dekho → φ theek karo → regularize karo → phir kahin ja kar bada model.

P-3.46 marks

A sentiment classifier uses one-hot bag-of-words over 30,000 words with 8 classes, trained on 1,500 reviews. Compute the parameter count and the ratio to data size, state what will happen, and give two remedies.

Sawal ka matlab: 30,000 alfaaz ka one-hot bag-of-words, 8 classes, aur sirf 1,500 reviews par training. Parameters ginein aur data se nisbat nikaalein, batayein kya hoga, aur do ilaaj dein.

Jawab

English

parameters = 30,000 x 8 = 240,000 ratio = 240,000 / 1,500 = 160 per example

What happens: severe overfitting. With d ≫ n the model can drive training error to zero by memorising which rare words appeared in which review. Many words occur once or twice, so their coefficients are fitted to pure noise. Training accuracy looks excellent, test accuracy is poor.

Remedy 1 — reduce d. Use a dense pre-trained embedding (50–300 dims), cutting parameters by ~2 orders of magnitude. It is also approximately faithful, so evidence is shared across synonyms instead of learned separately for each.

Remedy 2 — regularize heavily. Lasso is especially apt: most of the 30,000 words are irrelevant, so a sparsifying penalty zeroes them, giving feature selection and an interpretable word list.

Roman Urdu

parameters = 30,000 x 8 = 2,40,000 nisbat = 2,40,000 / 1,500 = 160 per example

Kya hoga: shadeed overfitting. d ≫ n ke saath model training error 0 kar sakta hai, sirf ye yaad kar ke ke kaun sa kam-aane wala lafz kis review mein tha. Bohat se alfaaz ek ya do baar aate hain, to un ke coefficients mehez shor par fit hote hain. Training accuracy shaandaar lagegi, test accuracy buri.

Ilaaj 1 — d kam karein. Dense pre-trained embedding (50–300 dims) use karein — parameters taqreeban 100 guna kam. Aur wo taqreeban faithful bhi hai, to hum-maani alfaaz ka saboot mushtarka ho jaata hai, har lafz ke liye alag seekhna nahi padta.

Ilaaj 2 — sakht regularization. Lasso yahan khaas tor par munasib hai: 30,000 mein se zyadatar alfaaz bay-kaar hain, to sparsifying penalty unko zero kar deti hai — feature selection ho jaata hai aur ek samajh mein aane wali lafzon ki list mil jaati hai.

P-3.56 marks

Define faithful embedding, then judge whether each encoding of {small, medium, large} is faithful: (a) one-hot; (b) 1, 2, 3; (c) every category mapped to 1.

Sawal ka matlab: Faithful embedding ki tareef dein, phir {small, medium, large} ke teen encodings par faisla dein: (a) one-hot; (b) 1, 2, 3; (c) teeno ko 1 bana dena.

Jawab

English

Definition. φ is faithful if it preserves the raw data's meaningful distinctions and relationships: (1) meaningfully different items → different vectors, and (2) the geometry reflects real similarity.

(a) One-hot — faithful enough, the safe default. Satisfies (1) fully. Fails (2) in principle (all pairs √2 apart, so it does not encode that medium lies between), but each category gets a free coefficient and with 3 categories the dimensionality objection does not arise.

(b) 1, 2, 3 — faithful here, because these categories are genuinely ordinal. The geometry actually helps: medium sits between small and large, matching reality, using one parameter instead of three. Caveat: it also asserts equal spacing and a monotone effect. (Contrast city names, where no order exists.)

(c) All → 1 — definitely not faithful. Violates (1) outright: three distinguishable inputs collapse to one vector, so information is irrecoverably destroyed and the model can never use size at all.

Lesson: faithfulness depends on the encoding together with the structure of the raw data.

Roman Urdu

Tareef. φ faithful hai agar wo raw data ke asal farq aur rishte bacha le: (1) maani mein alag cheezein → alag vectors, aur (2) geometry asal similarity ko aks kare.

(a) One-hot — kaafi had tak faithful, aur mehfooz chunaav. Shart (1) poori karta hai. Shart (2) usoolan tordta hai (har jodi √2 door, to ye nahi batata ke medium beech mein hai), magar har category ko azaad coefficient mil jaata hai aur 3 categories par dimension ka aitraaz nahi banta.

(b) 1, 2, 3 — yahan faithful hai, kyunki ye categories waqai ordinal hain. Yahan geometry faida de rahi hai: medium waqai small aur large ke beech baithta hai, aur teen ke bajaye ek parameter kaafi hai. Magar dhyan: ye barabar faasla aur ek hi taraf chalne wala asar bhi maan leta hai. (City names se moazna karein, jahan koi tarteeb nahi thi.)

(c) Teeno ko 1 — bilkul faithful nahi. Shart (1) seedha tootti hai: teen alag cheezein ek hi vector par jama ho gayin, to information hamesha ke liye zaya ho gayi aur model size ko kabhi istemaal hi nahi kar sakega.

Sabaq: faithfulness encoding ki akeli khoobi nahi — wo encoding aur raw data ki structure, dono par depend karti hai.

P-3.66 marks

Why is standardization essential for k-NN and for ridge, but irrelevant to the predictions of unregularized least squares? Illustrate the ridge case.

Sawal ka matlab: Standardization k-NN aur ridge ke liye kyun lazmi hai, magar bagair-regularization wale least squares ki predictions par kyun koi asar nahi daalti? Ridge wala case numbers se samjhayein.

Jawab

English

k-NN — essential. There are no parameters; everything is decided by distance, so the widest-range feature dominates and nothing inside the model can compensate. Predictions also become unit-dependent.

Ridge — essential, because the penalty hits coefficients, whose size depends on the feature's units:

Same real effect, +3000 rupees per m^2 : in m^2 : theta = 3000 -> penalty 3000^2 = 9,000,000 in ft^2 : theta = 278.7 -> penalty 278.7^2 = 77,674 ratio = 115.9

The identical relationship is penalised 116× more heavily just because of the unit, so the fit depends on an arbitrary choice. Standardizing makes λ penalise all features comparably.

Plain least squares — no effect. Rescaling feature j by c just rescales θj by 1/c, leaving every product θjxj — and so every prediction — unchanged.

One line: scaling matters wherever features are compared — in a distance or in a penalty.

Roman Urdu

k-NN — lazmi. Yahan koi parameter nahi; sab kuch faasla tay karta hai, to sab se badi range wala feature ghalib aa jaata hai aur model ke andar koi cheez usay theek nahi kar sakti. Aur predictions unit par depend karne lagti hain.

Ridge — lazmi, kyunki penalty coefficients par lagti hai, aur coefficient ka size feature ke unit par depend karta hai:

Wohi asal asar, +3000 rupaye per m^2 : m^2 mein : theta = 3000 -> penalty 3000^2 = 90,00,000 ft^2 mein : theta = 278.7 -> penalty 278.7^2 = 77,674 nisbat = 115.9

Bilkul wohi rishta sirf unit badalne par 116 guna zyada saza paa raha hai, to fit ek bay-maani chunaav par depend karne lag jaata hai. Standardize karne se λ har feature ko barabari se saza deta hai.

Saada least squares — koi asar nahi. Feature j ko c se guna karein to fitted θj khud 1/c ho jaata hai, aur har zarb θjxj — yaani har prediction — wohi reh jaati hai.

Ek line: scaling wahan ahem hai jahan features ka ek doosre se moazna hota hai — yaani kisi faasle mein ya kisi penalty mein.

P-3.76 marks

An airfare model uses days_before_departure and is_weekend as separate additive features. Booking late is far more severe for weekend flights. What is missing, write the corrected map, and show numerically that it fixes it.

Sawal ka matlab: Airfare model mein days_before_departure aur is_weekend alag alag jama ho rahe hain. Magar weekend ki flights par late booking ka nuqsan bohat zyada hai. Kya cheez missing hai, theek feature map likhein, aur numbers se dikhayein ke masla hal ho gaya.

Jawab

English

Missing: an interaction feature. An additive model gives θ2 as the day-effect whatever the weekend flag, and θ3 merely shifts the line by a constant. It cannot express "the day-effect is stronger at weekends", which needs the slope in days to depend on the flag.

phi(u) = ( 1 , days , weekend , days x weekend )

With θ = (200, −2, 50, −3):

slope in days, weekday = theta_2 = -2 per day slope in days, weekend = theta_2 + theta_4 = -5 per day booking 25 days later: weekday +50 weekend +125 (2.5x worse)

The interaction supplies a second, independent slope. Without it one θ2 is fitted to a compromise, systematically under-predicting weekend late bookings — a systematic residual pattern, the signature of underfitting.

Note: still linear in θ. If many interactions are plausible, generate them and let lasso select.

Roman Urdu

Missing: interaction feature. Additive model mein din ka asar θ2 hai — chahe weekend ho ya na ho — aur θ3 sirf poori line ko oopar/neeche khisaka deta hai. Aisa model ye keh hi nahi sakta ke "weekend par din ka asar zyada hai", kyunki is ke liye din ki dhalaan ko weekend par depend karna padega.

phi(u) = ( 1 , days , weekend , days x weekend )

θ = (200, −2, 50, −3) ke saath:

din ki dhalaan, weekday = theta_2 = -2 per din din ki dhalaan, weekend = theta_2 + theta_4 = -5 per din 25 din baad booking: weekday +50 weekend +125 (2.5 guna bura)

Interaction term ek doosri, azaad dhalaan de deta hai. Us ke bagair ek hi θ2 dono ka beech ka samjhauta ban jaata hai, aur weekend ki late bookings ko ba-qaida kam batata hai — yaani systematic residual pattern, jo underfitting ki pehchan hai.

Dhyan: model phir bhi θ mein linear hai. Agar kai interactions mumkin hon to sab bana dein aur lasso ko chunne dein.

Set 4 — 5 sawal

Nearest neighbour predictors

P-4.16 marks

A soft-NN predictor has three stored points at squared distances 0, 2 and 8 from a query, with outcomes y = (10, 20, 90). Compute the prediction for β = 2 and β = 50, and interpret.

Sawal ka matlab: Soft-NN ke paas teen purane points hain, jin ke naye point se squared faasle 0, 2 aur 8 hain, aur un ke jawab y = (10, 20, 90). β = 2 aur β = 50 ke liye prediction nikaalein aur natije ka matlab batayein.

Jawab

English

beta = 2 : scores 1.000 0.3679 0.0183 sum = 1.3862 weights 0.7214 0.2654 0.0132 y-hat = 0.7214(10) + 0.2654(20) + 0.0132(90) = 13.71 beta = 50 : scores 1.000 0.9608 0.8521 sum = 2.8129 weights 0.3555 0.3416 0.3029 y-hat = 0.3555(10) + 0.3416(20) + 0.3029(90) = 37.65 reference : 1-NN = 10 plain average = 40

Interpretation. At β = 2 the far point (y = 90) gets weight 0.013 and is almost ignored, so the answer sits near the 1-NN value of 10 — strict reliance on the closest case, high variance, overfitting risk.

At β = 50 the weights are nearly equal, the far point now contributes more than the other two combined, and the answer sits near the plain average of 40 — similarity has stopped mattering, underfitting risk.

So β interpolates between 1-NN and the constant mean. Note how much it changes one prediction (13.71 vs 37.65 on identical data) — which is why it must be chosen by validation.

Roman Urdu

beta = 2 : scores 1.000 0.3679 0.0183 jama = 1.3862 weights 0.7214 0.2654 0.0132 y-hat = 0.7214(10) + 0.2654(20) + 0.0132(90) = 13.71 beta = 50 : scores 1.000 0.9608 0.8521 jama = 2.8129 weights 0.3555 0.3416 0.3029 y-hat = 0.3555(10) + 0.3416(20) + 0.3029(90) = 37.65 moazne ke liye : 1-NN = 10 saada average = 40

Matlab. β = 2 par door wale point (y = 90) ka weight sirf 0.013 hai, yaani wo taqreeban nazar-andaaz ho gaya — is liye jawab 1-NN wale 10 ke qareeb aa gaya. Sab se qareeb case par sakht bharosa, variance zyada, overfitting ka khatra.

β = 50 par teeno weights taqreeban barabar hain, aur door wala point ab baqi dono se zyada hissa daal raha hai — jawab saada average 40 ke qareeb. Similarity ka faida khatam, underfitting ka khatra.

To β 1-NN aur constant mean ke darmiyan safar karata hai. Dekhein ek hi data par jawab 13.71 se 37.65 ho gaya — isi liye β ko validation se chunna lazmi hai.

P-4.26 marks

Show that soft-NN approaches 1-NN as β → 0 and a constant predictor as β → ∞. Which extreme is overfitting, and name another hyper-parameter that behaves the same way.

Sawal ka matlab: Saabit karein ke β → 0 par soft-NN 1-NN ban jaata hai aur β → ∞ par constant predictor. Kaunsa sira overfitting hai, aur ek aur hyper-parameter batayein jo isi tarah behave karta hai.

Jawab

English

β → 0. Factor out the smallest distance dmin; the common factor cancels, so each weight depends on exp(−(di − dmin)/β). For the nearest point the exponent is 0 → 1; for all others di − dmin > 0, so the factor → 0. Hence wmin → 1 and ŷ → ymin — the 1-NN predictor.

β → ∞. Every exponent → 0, so every score → 1, the denominator → n, and every weight → 1/n. So ŷ → (1/n)Σyi — the mean, independent of x.

Which is which: β → 0 (1-NN) is overfitting — max flexibility, zero training error, max variance. β → ∞ is underfitting — high bias, ignores x.

Others: k in k-NN (direction reversed: k=1 overfits, k=n gives the mean), λ in ridge/lasso (λ=0 overfits, λ→∞ leaves only the offset), and polynomial degree. All chosen by validation.

Roman Urdu

β → 0. Sab se chhote faasle dmin ko bahar nikaal dein; wo mushtarka hissa numerator aur denominator se kat jaata hai, to har weight exp(−(di − dmin)/β) par reh jaata hai. Sab se qareeb point ke liye exponent 0 hai → 1; baqi sab ke liye di − dmin > 0, to β → 0 par wo hissa → 0. Yaani wmin → 1 aur ŷ → ymin — 1-NN predictor.

β → ∞. Har exponent → 0, to har score → 1, denominator → n, aur har weight → 1/n. To ŷ → (1/n)Σyi — yaani mean, jo x par depend hi nahi karta.

Kaunsa sira kya: β → 0 (1-NN) = overfitting — lachak maximum, training error zero, variance maximum. β → ∞ = underfitting — bias zyada, x ko dekhta hi nahi.

Isi tarah ke doosre: k-NN ka k (magar ulti taraf: k=1 overfit, k=n mean), ridge/lasso ka λ (λ=0 overfit, λ→∞ sirf offset chhodta hai), aur polynomial degree. Sab validation se chunte hain.

P-4.36 marks

A fraud team uses 1-NN where 99.8% of transactions are legitimate, and reports 99.8% test accuracy. Why is this misleading, and what metric and predictor change do you recommend?

Sawal ka matlab: Fraud team 1-NN use karti hai jahan 99.8% transactions theek hain, aur wo 99.8% test accuracy bata rahi hai. Ye number kyun dhoka hai, aur aap kaunsa metric aur predictor ki kaunsi tabdeeli tajweez karenge?

Jawab

English

Why misleading: the baseline is already 99.8%. A predictor that ignores its input and always says "legitimate" scores 99.8% — that is the constant predictor, and it catches zero fraud. So the model has shown no skill; the number comes entirely from class imbalance. Accuracy also weights both error types equally, though a missed fraud costs far more.

Metrics: recall on the fraud class (the business objective), precision (the investigation workload), the precision–recall curve, or best, an explicit cost/utility metric.

Predictor changes: output a score, not a label — use soft-NN, whose weighted average gives a continuous fraud score so the threshold can be tuned to capacity. Raise k (but use the weighted proportion of fraudulent neighbours, since majority voting always says "legitimate" here). Standardize features. Consider reframing as anomaly detection.

Principle: always compare against the trivial constant-predictor baseline.

Roman Urdu

Dhoka kyun: baseline hi 99.8% hai. Ek aisa predictor jo input dekhta hi nahi aur hamesha "theek hai" kehta rahe, wo bhi 99.8% score karega — wo constant predictor hai, aur wo ek bhi fraud nahi pakadta. Yaani model ne koi hunar nahi dikhaya; ye number poora class imbalance se aaya hai. Aur accuracy dono qism ki ghalti ko barabar wazan deti hai, jabke fraud chhoot jaana bohat zyada mehnga hai.

Metrics: fraud class par recall (business ka asal maqsad), precision (investigation ka bojh), precision–recall curve, ya sab se behtar ek saaf cost/utility metric.

Predictor mein tabdeeli: jawab score ho, label nahi — soft-NN use karein, jiska weighted average ek musalsal fraud score deta hai, to threshold team ki quwwat ke hisaab se set ho sakta hai. k barhayein, magar fraud parosiyon ka weighted hissa lein, kyunki yahan majority vote hamesha "theek hai" hi kahega. Features standardize karein. Aur anomaly detection ki framing par bhi ghaur karein.

Usool: model ko hamesha saade constant-predictor baseline se mila kar dekhein.

P-4.46 marks

Compare 1-NN, k-NN and soft-NN on how the prediction is formed, hyper-parameters, and sensitivity. Give one situation where soft-NN is clearly preferable to k-NN.

Sawal ka matlab: 1-NN, k-NN aur soft-NN ka moazna karein — jawab kaise banta hai, hyper-parameters, aur sensitivity. Aur ek aisi soorat batayein jahan soft-NN saaf tor par k-NN se behtar hai.

Jawab

English

1-NNk-NNSoft NN
PredictionCopy the closest yMean/vote of k closest; others weight exactly 0Σwiyi over all points
Hyper-paramNonek — discreteβ — continuous
SensitivityMax — step functionLower but still jumpsLowest & continuous

Where soft-NN clearly wins: when a smooth output is required. For a robot joint angle or a steering setpoint, k-NN gives a piecewise-constant output that holds still and then jumps the moment the k-neighbour set changes membership. In a control system those jumps appear as jerks and can excite oscillation. Soft-NN's weights vary continuously, so the output is smooth.

Two more: with non-uniform density, a fixed k means a tiny neighbourhood where data is dense and a huge one where sparse, while β fixes a consistent length scale; and β tunes finely where k jumps coarsely.

Where k-NN wins: prediction cost — only k neighbours via a spatial index, versus all n exponentials per query.

Roman Urdu

1-NNk-NNSoft NN
JawabSab se qareeb ka y copyk qareebi ka average/vote; baqi ka weight exactly 0Σwiyi — saare points
Hyper-paramKoi nahik — tukdon meinβ — musalsal
SensitivityMaximum — step functionKam, magar phir bhi chhalaangSab se kam, aur narm

Soft-NN kab saaf behtar hai: jab jawab narm (smooth) chahiye. Robot ke joint angle ya steering ke setpoint ke liye k-NN ka jawab tukdon mein pakka hota hai — wo ruka rehta hai aur jis lamhe k parosiyon ki fehrist badalti hai, chhalaang laga deta hai. Control system mein ye chhalaangein jhatke ban jaati hain aur machine hilne lagti hai. Soft-NN ke weights musalsal badalte hain, to jawab narm rehta hai.

Do aur wajuhat: jab data ki ghanaawat (density) barabar na ho, to pakka k ghane ilaaqe mein bohat chhota aur khaali ilaaqe mein bohat bara halqa bana deta hai, jabke β ek pakki lambai tay karta hai; aur β baareeki se tune hota hai, k motay tukdon mein.

k-NN kahan behtar: prediction ka kharch — sirf k parosi (spatial index se), jabke soft-NN har sawal par saare n exponentials nikaalta hai.

P-4.56 marks

Why is nearest-neighbour called "instance-based" in the lectures' taxonomy, and what distinguishes it from a linear predictor in what is learned, what is stored, and when computation happens?

Sawal ka matlab: Lectures ki taxonomy mein nearest-neighbour ko "instance-based" kyun kehte hain, aur linear predictor se uska farq kya hai — kya seekha jaata hai, kya mehfooz hota hai, aur hisaab kab hota hai?

Jawab

English

It is instance-based because the model is the stored data — predictions come from direct comparison against retained instances, not from evaluating a fitted functional form.

Nearest neighbourLinear predictor
LearnedNothing fitted — no θ, no ERM; only k/β selectedθ fitted by (regularized) ERM
StoredAll n data pairsOnly d numbers — data can be discarded
ComputationAt prediction — O(nd) per query. "Lazy"At training — one dot product per query. "Eager"

Two consequences. (1) Since nothing is fitted there is nothing to regularize — no θ whose size could be penalised — so flexibility must be controlled through the distance function and neighbourhood size. That is why standardization is mandatory for NN: the distance is the model.

(2) NN adapts to any function shape without specifying φ, but degrades badly in high dimensions as distances concentrate. The trade is that NN moves the effort from choosing φ to choosing a metric.

Roman Urdu

Isay instance-based kehte hain kyunki model asal mein wohi mehfooz data hai — jawab kisi fit kiye hue formula se nahi, balke mehfooz misaalon se seedha moazna kar ke milta hai.

Nearest neighbourLinear predictor
Seekha kyaKuch fit nahi hota — na θ, na ERM; sirf k/β chuna jaata haiθ (regularized) ERM se fit hota hai
Mehfooz kyaSaare n data jodeSirf d numbers — data phenka ja sakta hai
Hisaab kabPrediction ke waqt — har sawal par O(nd). "Lazy"Training ke waqt — phir har sawal par ek dot product. "Eager"

Do nataij. (1) Kyunki kuch fit hota hi nahi, is liye regularize karne ko kuch nahi — koi θ hi nahi jiske size par jurmana lage — to lachak ko sirf faasle ke formula aur halqe ke size se qaabu karna padta hai. Isi liye NN mein standardization lazmi hai: faasla hi model hai.

(2) NN kisi bhi shakal ke function ke saath dhal jaata hai bagair φ batane ke, magar zyada dimensions mein bohat bigad jaata hai kyunki faasle jama ho jaate hain. Yaani NN mehnat φ chunne se hata kar metric chunne par le aata hai.

Set 5 — 8 sawal

Losses, constant predictors aur drills

Kuch sawal hisaab maangte hain — haath se practise karein, calculator ke bagair.

P-5.16 marks

For y = (3, 7, 7, 9, 24) compute the optimal constant and the empirical risk under (a) square loss, (b) absolute loss. (c) Verify each beats the other under its own loss. (d) Replace 24 by 10 and comment.

Sawal ka matlab: y = (3, 7, 7, 9, 24) ke liye behtareen constant aur uska empirical risk nikaalein — (a) square loss, (b) absolute loss. (c) Tasdeeq karein ke har ek apne loss par doosre se behtar hai. (d) 24 ki jagah 10 rakh kar natija batayein.

Jawab

English

(a) SQUARE -> MEAN = 50/5 = 10 MSE = (49+9+9+1+196)/5 = 264/5 = 52.8 (= variance) (b) ABSOLUTE -> MEDIAN. n=5 odd, median = y_3 = 7 check n_1 = #{y<7} = 1 -> 0.2 <= 0.5 ok n_2 = #{y>7} = 2 -> 0.4 <= 0.5 ok MAE = (4+0+0+2+17)/5 = 23/5 = 4.6 (c) CROSS-CHECK MAE at the mean (10) = (7+3+3+1+14)/5 = 28/5 = 5.6 > 4.6 ok MSE at the median (7) = (16+0+0+4+289)/5 = 309/5 = 61.8 > 52.8 ok (d) 24 -> 10 : y = (3,7,7,9,10) new mean = 36/5 = 7.2 (was 10.0) moved 2.8 new median = 7 (was 7.0) moved 0.0

Comment on (d): changing one value by 14 moved the mean by 2.8 (exactly 14/5, since every value enters with weight 1/n) and the median not at all. That is robustness quantified.

Why: the derivative of MAE is n1/n − n2/n — it depends only on how many points lie each side, never how far. So the outlier's magnitude is irrelevant to the median; the mean sums values directly and feels every one.

Roman Urdu

(a) SQUARE -> MEAN = 50/5 = 10 MSE = (49+9+9+1+196)/5 = 264/5 = 52.8 (= variance) (b) ABSOLUTE -> MEDIAN. n=5 taaq, median = y_3 = 7 check n_1 = #{y<7} = 1 -> 0.2 <= 0.5 theek n_2 = #{y>7} = 2 -> 0.4 <= 0.5 theek MAE = (4+0+0+2+17)/5 = 23/5 = 4.6 (c) CROSS-CHECK MEAN (10) par MAE = (7+3+3+1+14)/5 = 28/5 = 5.6 > 4.6 theek MEDIAN (7) par MSE = (16+0+0+4+289)/5 = 309/5 = 61.8 > 52.8 theek (d) 24 -> 10 : y = (3,7,7,9,10) naya mean = 36/5 = 7.2 (pehle 10.0) 2.8 khisak gaya naya median = 7 (pehle 7.0) 0.0 bilkul nahi hila

(d) par baat: ek value 14 badalne se mean 2.8 khisak gaya (bilkul 14/5, kyunki har value 1/n weight se jama hoti hai) aur median bilkul nahi hila. Yehi robustness ka numbers wala saboot hai.

Wajah: MAE ka derivative n1/n − n2/n hai — ye sirf is par depend karta hai ke kitne points kis taraf hain, ye nahi ke kitne door. To outlier ki value (24 ho ya 2400) median ko farq nahi deti; mean ke formula mein har value seedha jama hoti hai, to wo har ek ko mehsoos karta hai.

P-5.26 marks

For y = (3, 7, 7, 9, 24) find the 0.75- and 0.25-quantiles using the optimality condition. Which τ gives each, and describe a delivery-time scenario for each.

Sawal ka matlab: Isi data ke liye 0.75- aur 0.25-quantile optimality condition se nikaalein. Kaunsa τ kaunsa jawab deta hai, aur har ek ke liye delivery-time ki ek soorat batayein.

Jawab

English

Condition: θ is a τ-quantile iff n1/n ≤ τ ≤ 1 − n2/n.

0.75-QUANTILE. theta = 9: n_1 = #{y<9} = 3 -> 0.6 n_2 = #{y>9} = 1 -> 0.2 range = [0.6 , 0.8] 0.75 is inside ok => answer 9 0.25-QUANTILE. theta = 7: n_1 = #{y<7} = 1 -> 0.2 n_2 = #{y>7} = 2 -> 0.4 range = [0.2 , 0.6] 0.25 is inside ok => answer 7 Note: theta = 7 covers tau from 0.2 to 0.6, so it is simultaneously the 0.25-quantile AND the median. With only 5 points quantiles are coarse.

τ = 0.75 → quote 9 minutes. τ > ½ penalises under-estimation (lateness) more, so this is the customer promise: a cautious quote, only ~25% arriving later than promised.

τ = 0.25 → quote 7 minutes. τ < ½ penalises over-estimation more — an internal use: telling a rider when the order is ready, where over-estimating wastes paid idle time.

Key point: τ is a business decision, not estimated from data. Two teams can fit different τ to the same dataset.

Roman Urdu

Shart: θ τ-quantile hai agar n1/n ≤ τ ≤ 1 − n2/n.

0.75-QUANTILE. theta = 9: n_1 = #{y<9} = 3 -> 0.6 n_2 = #{y>9} = 1 -> 0.2 range = [0.6 , 0.8] 0.75 andar hai theek => jawab 9 0.25-QUANTILE. theta = 7: n_1 = #{y<7} = 1 -> 0.2 n_2 = #{y>7} = 2 -> 0.4 range = [0.2 , 0.6] 0.25 andar hai theek => jawab 7 Dhyan: theta = 7 ki range 0.2 se 0.6 hai, to wo ek hi waqt mein 0.25-quantile AUR median dono hai. Sirf 5 points par quantiles motay hote hain.

τ = 0.75 → 9 minute batayein. τ > ½ kam batane (late hone) par zyada saza deta hai, to ye customer se wada hai: ehtiyaat wala waqt, taake sirf ~25% deliveries wade se der se aayein.

τ = 0.25 → 7 minute batayein. τ < ½ zyada batane par zyada saza deta hai — ye andar ka istemaal hai: rider ko batana ke order kab tayyar hoga, jahan zyada batane se rider ka tankhwah wala waqt zaya hota hai.

Ahem baat: τ ek business faisla hai, data se nikalne wali cheez nahi. Ek hi data par do teams jaayez tor par mukhtalif τ rakh sakti hain.

P-5.36 marks

Define a convex function. Prove that f(w) = |w| is minimised at w = 0 using the optimality conditions for non-differentiable convex functions, and explain why f′(w) = 0 cannot be used.

Sawal ka matlab: Convex function ki tareef dein. Phir saabit karein ke f(w) = |w| ka minimum w = 0 par hai — magar un conditions se jo non-differentiable convex functions ke liye hain. Aur batayein f′(w) = 0 kyun istemaal nahi ho sakta.

Jawab

English

Definition. f is convex if for all w, z and α ∈ [0,1]:

f( alpha w + (1-alpha) z ) <= alpha f(w) + (1-alpha) f(z)

i.e. every chord lies on or above the graph; equivalently f″ ≥ 0 where it exists.

Why f′ = 0 cannot be used: |w| has a kink at 0 — slope −1 on the left, +1 on the right — so the derivative does not exist there, and it vanishes nowhere else either. The smooth condition would wrongly conclude no minimum exists.

CORRECT CONDITIONS (one-sided derivatives always exist if f is convex): w minimises f <=> f'_-(w) <= 0 AND f'_+(w) >= 0 For f(w) = |w| at w = 0, f(0) = 0 : f'_+(0) = lim [ |t| - 0 ] / t = lim t/t = +1 (t > 0) f'_-(0) = lim [ |t| - 0 ] / t = lim -t/t = -1 (t < 0) f'_-(0) = -1 <= 0 ok f'_+(0) = +1 >= 0 ok Both hold => w = 0 minimises |w|. QED

Why it matters: this is the exact tool that proves the median minimises MAE and the τ-quantile minimises tilted loss, and it explains why the kink in |θj| holds lasso coefficients at exactly zero.

Roman Urdu

Tareef. f convex hai agar har w, z aur har α ∈ [0,1] ke liye:

f( alpha w + (1-alpha) z ) <= alpha f(w) + (1-alpha) f(z)

Yaani koi bhi chord (taar) curve ke oopar ya us par rehta hai; aur jahan derivative mojood ho wahan f″ ≥ 0.

f′ = 0 kyun kaam nahi karta: |w| mein 0 par nukeela mod (kink) hai — bayein dhalaan −1, dayein +1 — to wahan derivative mojood hi nahi, aur kisi aur jagah wo zero bhi nahi hota. Smooth shart ghalat natija nikaalegi ke "koi minimum hi nahi".

THEEK SHARTEIN (convex function ke one-sided derivatives hamesha mojood hain): w minimum hai <=> f'_-(w) <= 0 AUR f'_+(w) >= 0 f(w) = |w| ke liye, w = 0 par, f(0) = 0 : f'_+(0) = lim [ |t| - 0 ] / t = lim t/t = +1 (t > 0) f'_-(0) = lim [ |t| - 0 ] / t = lim -t/t = -1 (t < 0) f'_-(0) = -1 <= 0 theek f'_+(0) = +1 >= 0 theek Dono shartein poori => w = 0 minimum hai. SABIT

Ye kyun ahem hai: bilkul yehi hathiyar saabit karta hai ke median MAE ko minimize karta hai aur τ-quantile tilted loss ko — aur yehi batata hai ke |θj| ka kink lasso ke coefficients ko exactly zero par pakad leta hai.

P-5.46 marks

Derive the optimal constant predictor under square loss, show the resulting risk equals the data variance, and state the practical use of this result.

Sawal ka matlab: Square loss ke saath behtareen constant predictor derive karein, dikhayein ke us par risk data ka variance banta hai, aur batayein is nateeje ka amali faida kya hai.

Jawab

English

L(theta) = (1/n) SUM (theta - y_i)^2 L'(theta) = (2/n) SUM (theta - y_i) = 0 => n theta = SUM y_i => theta = (1/n) SUM y_i = the MEAN At that optimum: L(mean) = (1/n) SUM (mean - y_i)^2 = var(y) (by definition)

Practical use: it is the universal baseline for regression. The best predictor that ignores the features entirely achieves MSE equal to the data variance. Therefore:

  • Any model whose test MSE is not comfortably below the target variance has learned nothing useful.
  • It defines R² = 1 − MSE/var: R² = 0 means "as good as the constant"; R² < 0 on test data means worse — an overfitting warning.
  • It makes an absolute error figure meaningful: "MSE = 240" is worthless if the variance is 250, excellent if it is 10,000.

Generalises: under absolute loss the baseline is the median; in classification it is the majority-class predictor.

Roman Urdu

L(theta) = (1/n) SUM (theta - y_i)^2 L'(theta) = (2/n) SUM (theta - y_i) = 0 => n theta = SUM y_i => theta = (1/n) SUM y_i = MEAN Us optimum par: L(mean) = (1/n) SUM (mean - y_i)^2 = var(y) (tareef ke mutabiq)

Amali faida: ye regression ka aalmi baseline hai. Wo behtareen predictor jo features ko bilkul dekhta hi nahi, uska MSE data ke variance ke barabar hota hai. Is liye:

  • Jis model ka test MSE target ke variance se saaf neeche nahi, us ne kuch faidemand nahi seekha.
  • Isi se R² = 1 − MSE/var banta hai: R² = 0 ka matlab "constant jitna hi acha"; aur test par R² < 0 ka matlab constant se bhi bura — overfitting ki saaf tanbeeh.
  • Isi se akela error number maani-khez banta hai: "MSE = 240" bay-kaar hai agar variance 250 ho, aur shaandaar hai agar variance 10,000 ho.

Aur aage: absolute loss par baseline median hota hai; classification mein majority-class predictor.

P-5.56 marks

An energy retailer faces billing disputes over percentage errors: 20 kWh wrong matters far more for a small household. Which loss do the lectures provide, what is its optimal constant for y = (1, 4), and what familiar quantity is it?

Sawal ka matlab: Bijli company ke jhagde percentage ghalti par hote hain — 20 kWh ki ghalti chhote ghar ke liye bohat zyada maani rakhti hai. Lectures kaunsa loss dete hain, y = (1, 4) ke liye uska behtareen constant kya hai, aur wo kis jaani-pehchani cheez ke barabar nikalta hai?

Jawab

English

Loss: fractional loss (for ŷ, y > 0):

ell(y-hat, y) = max( y-hat/y - 1 , y/y-hat - 1 ) = exp|log y-hat - log y| - 1

Why it fits: square and absolute loss penalise the difference, so 20 kWh wrong costs the same on a 25 kWh household (80% wrong) as on a 2,500 kWh one (0.8% wrong). Fractional loss penalises the ratio.

theta = ( SUM_(i<=k) y_i / SUM_(i>k) 1/y_i )^(1/2) n = 2, k = 1 : theta = ( 1 / 0.25 )^(1/2) = 4^(1/2) = 2 check 1 <= 2 <= 4 ok

The familiar quantity: the geometric mean — √(1×4) = 2. (Arithmetic mean would be 2.5.) Not a coincidence: fractional loss measures distance on a logarithmic scale, and averaging in log-space then transforming back is the geometric mean.

Practical equivalent: fit ordinary squared loss to log(y) — it penalises proportional errors similarly and keeps the least-squares machinery.

Roman Urdu

Loss: fractional loss (jab ŷ, y > 0):

ell(y-hat, y) = max( y-hat/y - 1 , y/y-hat - 1 ) = exp|log y-hat - log y| - 1

Kyun munasib: square aur absolute loss farq par jurmana lagate hain, to 20 kWh ki ghalti 25 kWh wale ghar (80% ghalat) aur 2,500 kWh wale ghar (0.8% ghalat) — dono par barabar mehngi padti hai. Fractional loss nisbat (ratio) par jurmana lagata hai.

theta = ( SUM_(i<=k) y_i / SUM_(i>k) 1/y_i )^(1/2) n = 2, k = 1 : theta = ( 1 / 0.25 )^(1/2) = 4^(1/2) = 2 check 1 <= 2 <= 4 theek

Jaani-pehchani cheez: geometric mean — √(1×4) = 2. (Aam average 2.5 hota.) Ye ittefaq nahi: fractional loss faasla logarithm ke paimane par naapta hai, aur log ki duniya mein average lena phir wapas aana — yehi geometric mean hai.

Amali metbaadil: log(y) par aam squared loss fit kar dein — wo bhi percentage ghalti par isi tarah jurmana lagata hai aur least squares ki saari sahulat barqaraar rehti hai.

P-5.66 marks

Why is ℒ(θ) convex whenever the loss is convex, why does it matter, and what would change otherwise? Name a non-convex loss from the lectures.

Sawal ka matlab: Agar loss convex ho to ℒ(θ) bhi kyun convex hota hai, ye ahem kyun hai, aur agar convex na hota to kya badal jaata? Lectures se ek non-convex loss ka naam dein.

Jawab

English

Three-step argument: (1) the standard losses are convex in ŷ; (2) ŷi = θTxi is affine in θ, and a convex function of an affine map stays convex; (3) an average of convex functions is convex, and ℒ is such an average.

Why it matters: every local minimum is global; the optimality conditions are sufficient as well as necessary (which licenses the clean mean/median/quantile derivations); results are reproducible regardless of initialisation; and the problem is solvable efficiently — hence the lectures' remark that the lasso has no formula "but we can efficiently compute it anyway (since it's convex)".

Without convexity: multiple local minima and saddle points → the answer depends on initialisation, there is no certificate of optimality, and results are not reproducible.

Non-convex example: log-Huber. Beyond α its derivative 2α²/r decreases, so p″ < 0. Its extra robustness is bought by sacrificing convexity. (Similarly ℓ0.5 is non-convex, so computing it is only heuristic.)

Roman Urdu

Teen qadam ki daleel: (1) aam losses ŷ ke lehaz se convex hain; (2) ŷi = θTxi θ mein affine (seedha) hai, aur convex function ke andar affine map daalne se wo convex hi rehta hai; (3) convex functions ka average bhi convex hota hai, aur ℒ to average hi hai.

Ye kyun ahem hai: jo bhi local minimum mila wo global hai; optimality conditions sirf zaroori nahi, kaafi bhi hain (isi liye mean/median/quantile ke saaf saboot mumkin hue); jawab shuruati guess par depend nahi karta, yaani reproducible hai; aur masla efficiently hal ho jaata hai — isi liye lectures kehte hain ke lasso ka formula nahi hai "magar hum phir bhi usay efficiently nikaal lete hain (kyunki wo convex hai)".

Convexity ke bagair: kai local minima aur saddle points → jawab shuruati guess par depend karta hai, optimality ka koi saboot nahi milta, aur nataij dohraye nahi ja sakte.

Non-convex misaal: log-Huber. α ke baad uska derivative 2α²/r ghatta hai, to p″ < 0. Uski zyada robustness convexity ki qurbani de kar kharidi gayi hai. (Isi tarah ℓ0.5 bhi non-convex hai, is liye usay nikalna sirf heuristic hai.)

P-5.76 marks

A pharmacy model predicts drug dose. Overdosing is life-threatening; under-dosing merely reduces efficacy. Which loss, and what range for its parameter? Justify the direction with an explicit sign calculation.

Sawal ka matlab: Dawa ki khuraak ka model. Zyada khuraak jaan-leva hai; kam khuraak sirf asar kam karti hai. Kaunsa loss, aur uske parameter ki kaunsi range? Nishaan (sign) ka hisaab kar ke wajah dein.

Jawab

English

Loss: tilted absolute loss with τ < ½.

SIGN CHECK. r = y-hat - y correct dose y = 40 mg, model predicts y-hat = 55 mg r = 55 - 40 = +15 (POSITIVE) -> too much -> OVERDOSE => OVERDOSE = over-estimation = r > 0 <- dangerous side WHICH tau PENALISES r > 0 MORE? r >= 0 costs (1-tau)|r| ; r < 0 costs tau|r| need (1 - tau) > tau => 1 > 2 tau => tau < 1/2 tau = 0.1 : 10 mg overdose -> 0.9(10) = 9 10 mg under-dose -> 0.1(10) = 1 -> 9 : 1

The optimal constant is the τ-quantile — at τ = 0.1, the 10th percentile, a deliberately conservative dose so only ~10% of patients are over-dosed.

Sanity check: penalising overdose should make the model prescribe less; a small τ gives a low quantile. ✓ (Mirror image of OHT Q-8, where lateness was costly and τ > ½ was needed.)

Caveats: τ encodes a clinical judgement, so set it with clinicians; and a quantile loss gives asymmetry but not a hard limit — for a true ceiling add an explicit constraint.

Roman Urdu

Loss: tilted absolute loss, τ < ½ ke saath.

NISHAAN CHECK. r = y-hat - y sahi khuraak y = 40 mg, model kehta hai y-hat = 55 mg r = 55 - 40 = +15 (POSITIVE) -> zyada mil gayi -> OVERDOSE => OVERDOSE = zyada batana = r > 0 <- khatarnak taraf KAUNSA tau r > 0 PAR ZYADA SAZA DETA HAI? r >= 0 ki qeemat (1-tau)|r| ; r < 0 ki qeemat tau|r| chahiye (1 - tau) > tau => 1 > 2 tau => tau < 1/2 tau = 0.1 : 10 mg zyada -> 0.9(10) = 9 10 mg kam -> 0.1(10) = 1 -> 9 : 1

Behtareen constant τ-quantile hota hai — τ = 0.1 par 10th percentile, yaani jaan boojh kar ehtiyaat wali kam khuraak, taake sirf ~10% patients ko zyada khuraak mile.

Aqal se tasdeeq: overdose par saza lagane se model kam khuraak likhega; aur chhota τ neecha quantile deta hai. ✓ (OHT Q-8 ka ulta case, jahan late hona mehnga tha aur τ > ½ chahiye tha.)

Ehtiyaat: τ ek tibbi faisla hai, to usay doctors ke saath tay karein; aur quantile loss sirf na-barabari deta hai, pakki had nahi — asli safety ceiling ke liye alag se constraint lagayein.

P-5.86 marks

Three teams fit a constant predictor to the same data and report 42, 38 and 51, with no arithmetic errors. How is this possible, and what single piece of information decides which is correct?

Sawal ka matlab: Teen teams ek hi data par constant predictor fit karti hain aur 42, 38, 51 batati hain — aur kisi ne hisaab ki ghalti nahi ki. Ye kaise mumkin hai, aur kaunsi ek cheez bata degi ke kaun theek hai?

Jawab

English

They used different loss functions. "What is the best single number?" has no answer until the loss is specified, because the loss defines "best" — the lectures' point that "different losses lead to different predictors".

  • 42 = the mean → square loss.
  • 38 = the median → absolute loss.
  • 51 = an upper τ-quantile (τ > ½) → tilted loss.

The ordering is informative: median (38) < mean (42) means the data is right-skewed — a few large values pulling the mean up — typical of durations, prices and incomes.

The information needed: the loss function — i.e. how the organisation values the direction and magnitude of error. Clean data, large errors disproportionately damaging → 42. Outliers present → 38. Under-estimation much worse → 51.

Lesson: the loss is a modelling decision encoding business values, not a default to inherit.

Roman Urdu

Un teeno ne mukhtalif loss functions use kiye. "Behtareen akela number kya hai?" ka koi jawab nahi jab tak loss na bataya jaaye, kyunki loss hi "behtareen" ki tareef karta hai — lectures ka nukta: "mukhtalif losses se mukhtalif predictors nikalte hain".

  • 42 = mean → square loss.
  • 38 = median → absolute loss.
  • 51 = ooncha τ-quantile (τ > ½) → tilted loss.

In ki tarteeb khud ek ishara hai: median (38) < mean (42) ka matlab data dayein ko jhuka hua (right-skewed) hai — yaani kuch badi values mean ko oopar kheench rahi hain — jo waqt, keematon aur income mein aam hai.

Jo cheez chahiye: loss function — yaani company ghalti ki taraf aur jasaamat ko kitna bura samajhti hai. Saaf data aur badi ghalti bohat nuqsan-deh → 42. Outliers mojood → 38. Kam batana bohat zyada bura → 51.

Sabaq: loss ek faisla hai jo business ki qadrein bayaan karta hai, koi default nahi jo bagair soche utha liya jaaye.

Set 6 — 5 sawal

Robust fitting

P-6.16 marks

With α = 2, tabulate the square and Huber penalties and their derivatives at r = 1, 2, 5, 20. Explain in one sentence why Huber is robust, and verify the two branches agree at r = α.

Sawal ka matlab: α = 2 ke saath square aur Huber penalty aur un ke derivatives ka table banayein (r = 1, 2, 5, 20 par). Ek jumle mein batayein Huber robust kyun hai, aur tasdeeq karein ke r = α par dono tukde mil jaate hain.

Jawab

English

r p_sqr p_hub p'_sqr = 2r p'_hub ---- ------- --------- ------------ -------- 1 1 1 2 2 2 4 4 4 4 5 25 16 10 4 (2.5x weaker) 20 400 76 40 4 (10x weaker) BRANCHES AGREE AT r = alpha = 2: value: r^2 = 4 vs alpha(2r - alpha) = 2(4-2) = 4 ok (continuous) slope: 2r = 4 vs 2 alpha = 4 ok (smooth, C1)

One sentence: beyond |r| = α the Huber derivative saturates at the constant 2α = 4, so every outlier exerts the same bounded pull however extreme it is — whereas 2r grows without bound, letting one far-off point dominate the gradient.

Why the smoothness matters: Huber is both convex and C1, so optimization is well behaved with a unique global minimum — unlike absolute loss (kinked at 0) and log-Huber (smooth but non-convex).

Roman Urdu

r p_sqr p_hub p'_sqr = 2r p'_hub ---- ------- --------- ------------ -------- 1 1 1 2 2 2 4 4 4 4 5 25 16 10 4 (2.5 guna kam) 20 400 76 40 4 (10 guna kam) r = alpha = 2 PAR DONO TUKDE MILTE HAIN: value: r^2 = 4 vs alpha(2r - alpha) = 2(4-2) = 4 theek (continuous) slope: 2r = 4 vs 2 alpha = 4 theek (smooth, C1)

Ek jumla: |r| = α ke baad Huber ka derivative 2α = 4 par ruk jaata hai, to har outlier barabar aur mehdood zor lagata hai — chahe wo kitna bhi door ho — jabke 2r bay-had barhta hai aur ek door ka point poore gradient par qabza kar leta hai.

Smoothness kyun ahem hai: Huber convex aur C1 dono hai, to optimization mein koi masla nahi aur global minimum ek hi hota hai — absolute loss (0 par kink) aur log-Huber (smooth magar non-convex) ke barkhilaaf.

P-6.26 marks

One colleague sets α = 10,000 where typical residuals are ~2 and reports that robustness "made no difference". Another sets α = 0.001. What has each actually fitted, and how should α be chosen?

Sawal ka matlab: Ek banda α = 10,000 rakhta hai (jabke residuals taqreeban 2 hain) aur kehta hai "robustness se koi farq nahi pada". Doosra α = 0.001 rakhta hai. Dono ne asal mein kya fit kiya, aur α kaise chunna chahiye?

Jawab

English

α = 10,000 → they fitted ordinary least squares. Every residual satisfies |r| < α, so every point sits on the quadratic branch and phub = r² throughout. The affine branch is never reached, so robustness is never activated — their observation is exactly correct. As α → ∞, Huber → square loss.

α = 0.001 → they fitted (scaled) absolute loss. Almost every residual exceeds α, so phub ≈ 2α|r| — a constant times |r|, and scaling does not move the minimiser. As α → 0, Huber → absolute loss.

How to choose: α is the boundary between ordinary noise and outlier, so set it near the scale of genuine noise. Primary method: validation on a log grid. Good starting point: 1–2× the median absolute deviation of the residuals — use MAD not the standard deviation, since the standard deviation is itself inflated by the outliers you are guarding against. Here, α ≈ 2–4.

Roman Urdu

α = 10,000 → us ne asal mein aam least squares fit kiya. Har residual |r| < α hai, to har point quadratic tukde par baithta hai aur phub = r² hi reh jaata hai. Seedhi line wala tukda kabhi aata hi nahi, to robustness chalu hi nahi hui — uski baat bilkul theek hai. α → ∞ par Huber square loss ban jaata hai.

α = 0.001 → us ne (scaled) absolute loss fit kiya. Taqreeban har residual α se bara hai, to phub ≈ 2α|r| — yaani ek constant guna |r|, aur constant se guna karne par minimum ki jagah nahi badalti. α → 0 par Huber absolute loss ban jaata hai.

Kaise chunein: α wo sarhad hai jahan "aam shor" khatam aur "outlier" shuru hota hai, to usay asli shor ke paimane par rakhein. Asal tareeqa: log grid par validation. Acha shuruati andaza: residuals ke median absolute deviation (MAD) ka 1–2 guna — MAD lein, standard deviation nahi, kyunki standard deviation khud unhi outliers se phool jaati hai jin se bachna hai. Yahan α ≈ 2–4.

P-6.36 marks

State the lectures' iterative outlier-removal procedure, give two reasons a robust penalty is generally preferable, and one situation where deletion is right.

Sawal ka matlab: Lectures ka wo tareeqa likhein jis mein outliers baar baar hata kar dobara fit kiya jaata hai. Phir do wajuhat dein ke robust penalty aam tor par behtar kyun hai, aur ek soorat batayein jahan points hatana hi theek hai.

Jawab

English

The procedure: (1) fit a predictor; (2) flag points with large prediction errors as outliers; (3) remove them and repeat.

Reason 1 — the deletion criterion comes from the fit the outliers already corrupted. On the first pass the model is fitted including the outliers, so it has already been dragged toward them — and that displaced fit then decides which points look "large". A strong outlier can pull the line far enough that nearby valid points appear anomalous while the outlier looks acceptable (masking and swamping). Iterating can progressively delete good data.

Reason 2 — deletion is a hard, irreversible decision needing an arbitrary threshold. A point just over it is discarded entirely; just under it keeps full quadratic influence. There is no principled cut, and iterating may strip a genuine tail — which biases the model, since real extreme values are legitimate data. Huber instead caps influence smoothly and keeps all the data.

When deletion is right: when points are provably invalid, not merely extreme — physically impossible values (negative age, 200°C body temperature), a sensor logged as faulty, or clear decimal-point corruption.

Roman Urdu

Tareeqa: (1) predictor fit karein; (2) jin points ki prediction error bohat badi hai unko outlier ka nishaan lagayein; (3) unko nikaal dein aur dobara shuru karein.

Wajah 1 — jis fit ne faisla karna hai, usay khud outliers ne bigada hua hai. Pehle chakkar mein model outliers ke saath fit hota hai, to wo pehle hi un ki taraf kheencha ja chuka hai — aur wohi khisaka hua fit ab tay karta hai ke kaun "bada" lag raha hai. Ek taqatwar outlier line ko itna kheench sakta hai ke paas ke sahi points ajeeb lagne lagein aur outlier khud theek lage (masking aur swamping). Baar baar dohrane se acha data bhi kat sakta hai.

Wajah 2 — hatana ek sakht, wapas na hone wala faisla hai, aur usay ek bay-maani had chahiye. Had se zara oopar wala point poora phenk diya jaata hai, zara neeche wala poora quadratic asar rakhta hai. Us cut ki koi usooli jagah nahi, aur dohrate rehne se data ki asli dum (tail) kat sakti hai — jo model ko jhuka deta hai, kyunki asli badi values bhi jaayez data hoti hain. Huber is ke bajaye asar par narmi se had lagata hai aur saara data rakhta hai.

Hatana kab theek hai: jab points sabit shuda ghalat hon, sirf bade na hon — jismani tor par namumkin values (negative umar, 200°C jism ka darja), ya wo sensor jiski kharabi record mein darj hai, ya saaf decimal point ki ghalti.

P-6.46 marks

Choose between absolute, Huber and log-Huber loss for data with a few extremely corrupted readings. Compare on robustness, convexity and smoothness, and give a recommended procedure.

Sawal ka matlab: Jis data mein chand bohat hi kharab readings hain, us ke liye absolute, Huber aur log-Huber mein se chunein. Robustness, convexity aur smoothness par moazna karein, aur ek tajweez-shuda tareeqa dein.

Jawab

English

AbsoluteHuberLog-Huber
Large-r derivative±1±2α (capped)2α²/r — decays to 0
RobustnessGoodGoodBest
Convex?YesYesNo
Smooth?No — kink at 0Yes (C1)Yes
Small errorsPenalty relatively largeQuadraticQuadratic

Recommended procedure:

  1. Start with Huber — the only one robust and convex and smooth. α near 1–2× the MAD, by validation.
  2. Check the residual histogram and held-out error. Since corruption is extreme, Huber's capped-but-nonzero pull of 2α per outlier may still bias the fit.
  3. If so, escalate to log-Huber — but initialise from the Huber solution. Solving the convex problem first makes the non-convex one far likelier to land in the right basin.
  4. Compare candidates on held-out error, as the lectures prescribe.
  5. Separately remove provably invalid records.

Why not plain absolute loss? Robust, convex and parameter-free, but its penalty is relatively large for small residuals, so it fits the clean majority less tightly, and its kink at 0 complicates gradient methods.

Roman Urdu

AbsoluteHuberLog-Huber
Bade r par derivative±1±2α (had lag gayi)2α²/r — 0 tak ghatta hai
RobustnessAchiAchiSab se behtar
Convex?HaanHaanNahi
Smooth?Nahi — 0 par kinkHaan (C1)Haan
Chhoti ghalti parJurmana nisbatan zyadaQuadraticQuadratic

Tajweez-shuda tareeqa:

  1. Huber se shuru karein — sirf yehi robust aur convex aur smooth hai. α MAD ka 1–2 guna, validation se.
  2. Residual histogram aur held-out error dekhein. Kyunki kharabi shadeed hai, Huber ka 2α wala mehdood magar sifar-se-zyada zor phir bhi fit ko jhuka sakta hai.
  3. Zaroorat par log-Huber par jaayein — magar Huber ke jawab se shuru karein. Pehle convex masla hal karne se non-convex masle mein sahi jagah girne ka imkaan bohat barh jaata hai.
  4. Candidates ka moazna held-out error par karein, jaisa lectures kehte hain.
  5. Alag se wo records nikaal dein jo sabit shuda ghalat hain.

Saada absolute loss kyun nahi? Wo robust, convex aur parameter se azaad hai, magar chhote residuals par uska jurmana nisbatan zyada hai, to wo saaf data ko utna kas kar fit nahi karta; aur 0 par uska kink gradient methods ko mushkil bana deta hai.

P-6.56 marks

How does the penalty choice shape the residual histogram, and what would you conclude from: (a) symmetric with two long tails; (b) most residuals slightly negative; (c) residuals growing with one feature; (d) a tall spike at zero with a few very large values?

Sawal ka matlab: Penalty ka chunaav residuals ke histogram ki shakal kaise badalta hai? Aur in chaar histograms se kya natija nikaalenge: (a) 0 ke around symmetric magar dono taraf lambi dumein; (b) zyadatar residuals thoday negative; (c) residuals ek feature ke saath barhte hue; (d) 0 par ooncha spike aur kuch bohat badi values?

Jawab

English

How: ERM minimises the average penalty, so the penalty is a statement about which distribution of errors is acceptable. Square loss punishes large residuals hard and small ones barely, spreading error symmetrically; robust penalties concentrate mass near zero and tolerate a few large values; tilted penalties deliberately shift the histogram to one side.

(a) Symmetric, two long tails → healthy centring but outliers. The signature of square loss on contaminated data. Action: switch to Huber.

(b) Mostly slightly negative → r < 0 means systematic under-prediction. If a tilted loss with τ < ½ was used, this is intended. If a symmetric loss was used it is a bias — most likely a missing or wrongly regularized offset.

(c) Growing with one feature → underfitting / misspecification. Residuals should be random against every input. Action: enrich the feature map — a robust loss would not help.

(d) Tall spike at zero + a few large values → the fingerprint of a successful robust fit, and a good result: the bulk fitted tightly, a few outliers deliberately abandoned.

Rule: shifted → bias/asymmetric loss. Patterned against a feature → fix the features. Heavy-tailed → fix the loss.

Roman Urdu

Kaise: ERM average penalty minimize karta hai, to penalty asal mein ye elaan hai ke ghaltiyon ki kaunsi taqseem qubool hai. Square loss badi ghalti par sakhti aur chhoti par narmi karta hai, to error ko symmetric tor par phaila deta hai; robust penalties 0 ke paas jhund bana deti hain aur chand badi values bardasht kar leti hain; tilted penalties jaan boojh kar poora histogram ek taraf khisaka deti hain.

(a) Symmetric, dono taraf lambi dumein → markaz theek hai magar outliers mojood hain. Ye kharab data par square loss ki pehchan hai. Amal: Huber par aa jaayein.

(b) Zyadatar thoday negative → r < 0 ka matlab model ba-qaida kam bata raha hai. Agar tilted loss τ < ½ ke saath istemaal hua tha to ye irada tha. Agar symmetric loss tha to ye bias hai — ghaaliban offset missing hai ya usay ghalti se regularize kar diya gaya.

(c) Ek feature ke saath barhte hue → underfitting / misspecification. Residuals har input ke khilaaf be-tarteeb hone chahiye. Amal: feature map rich karein — robust loss yahan kaam nahi aayega.

(d) 0 par ooncha spike + kuch badi values → ye kaamyaab robust fit ki pehchan hai, aur acha natija hai: bulk kas kar fit ho gaya, chand outliers jaan boojh kar chhod diye gaye.

Qanoon: khisak gaya → bias ya asymmetric loss. Feature ke khilaaf pattern → features theek karein. Lambi dumein → loss theek karein.

Set 7 — 7 sawal

Regularization aur sparsity

P-7.16 marks

For θ = (1, −2, 2) and ρ = 0.5, compute the worst-case sensitivity under (a) an ℓ2 and (b) an ℓ∞ perturbation set. Exhibit the worst-case δ in each, and state which regularizer each justifies.

Sawal ka matlab: θ = (1, −2, 2) aur ρ = 0.5 ke liye worst-case sensitivity nikaalein — (a) ℓ2 perturbation set par, (b) ℓ∞ par. Dono mein wo sab se bura δ bhi dikhayein, aur batayein kaunsa regularizer kis se nikalta hai.

Jawab

English

NORMS: ||theta||_2 = sqrt(1+4+4) = 3 ||theta||_1 = 1+2+2 = 5 (a) L2 SET { ||delta||_2 <= 0.5 } worst case = rho * ||theta||_2 = 0.5 * 3 = 1.5 worst delta = rho * theta / ||theta||_2 = (0.1667, -0.3333, 0.3333) check ||delta||_2 = sqrt(1/36+4/36+4/36) = sqrt(0.25) = 0.5 ok check theta^T delta = 0.1667 + 0.6667 + 0.6667 = 1.50 ok (b) L-INFINITY SET { |delta_j| <= 0.5 for all j } worst case = rho * ||theta||_1 = 0.5 * 5 = 2.5 worst delta = rho * sign(theta) = (0.5, -0.5, 0.5) check ||delta||_inf = 0.5 ok check theta^T delta = 0.5 + 1.0 + 1.0 = 2.50 ok L2 -> justifies RIDGE (sum square) Linf -> justifies LASSO (sum absolute)

Interpretation. Same θ, same ρ, but the worst case is 1.5 vs 2.5 — 67% larger. Geometrically the ℓ2 ball is inscribed in the ℓ∞ cube, so the cube contains extra corner directions (every component simultaneously at its limit) lying outside the ball. In general ‖θ‖1 ≥ ‖θ‖2.

What it means: the choice of regularizer is a statement about how you believe features can be corrupted. Total error bounded → ridge. Each feature with its own sensor, all able to be at their worst at once — usually more realistic → lasso.

Roman Urdu

NORMS: ||theta||_2 = sqrt(1+4+4) = 3 ||theta||_1 = 1+2+2 = 5 (a) L2 SET { ||delta||_2 <= 0.5 } worst case = rho * ||theta||_2 = 0.5 * 3 = 1.5 sab se bura delta = rho * theta / ||theta||_2 = (0.1667, -0.3333, 0.3333) check ||delta||_2 = sqrt(1/36+4/36+4/36) = sqrt(0.25) = 0.5 theek check theta^T delta = 0.1667 + 0.6667 + 0.6667 = 1.50 theek (b) L-INFINITY SET { har j ke liye |delta_j| <= 0.5 } worst case = rho * ||theta||_1 = 0.5 * 5 = 2.5 sab se bura delta = rho * sign(theta) = (0.5, -0.5, 0.5) check ||delta||_inf = 0.5 theek check theta^T delta = 0.5 + 1.0 + 1.0 = 2.50 theek L2 -> is se nikalta hai RIDGE (sum square) Linf -> is se nikalta hai LASSO (sum absolute)

Matlab. Wohi θ, wohi ρ, magar worst case 1.5 vs 2.5 — 67% zyada. Geometry: ℓ2 ki gol gaind ℓ∞ ke chaukor dabbe ke andar fit hoti hai, to dabbe mein wo kone bhi hain (jahan har component bar-e-waqt apni had par hai) jo gaind se bahar nikle hue hain. Aam tor par ‖θ‖1 ≥ ‖θ‖2.

Is ka matlab: regularizer ka chunaav asal mein ye elaan hai ke aap features kaise kharab hone ka yaqeen rakhte hain. Kul ghalti mehdood ho → ridge. Har feature ka apna sensor ho aur sab ek saath apni had par ja sakein — aur ye aksar zyada haqeeqi hai → lasso.

P-7.26 marks

Prove that the worst-case sensitivity of a linear predictor under an ℓ2 perturbation set equals ρ‖θ‖2. State where Cauchy-Schwarz is used and why the bound is attained.

Sawal ka matlab: Saabit karein ke ℓ2 perturbation set ke saath linear predictor ki worst-case sensitivity ρ‖θ‖2 hoti hai. Batayein Cauchy-Schwarz kahan lagaya, aur ye had waqai haasil kyun ho jaati hai.

Jawab

English

SETUP. The change in prediction when x -> x + delta is theta^T(x + delta) - theta^T x = theta^T delta (x cancels) S = max over { ||delta||_2 <= rho } of | theta^T delta | STEP 1 - UPPER BOUND (Cauchy-Schwarz is used HERE): | theta^T delta | <= ||theta||_2 ||delta||_2 <= ||theta||_2 * rho so S <= rho ||theta||_2 STEP 2 - THE BOUND IS ATTAINED. Take delta* = rho * theta / ||theta||_2 : admissible? ||delta*||_2 = rho ||theta||_2 / ||theta||_2 = rho ok value? theta^T delta* = rho (theta^T theta)/||theta||_2 = rho ||theta||_2 ok => S = rho * ||theta||_2 QED

Why it is attained: Cauchy-Schwarz holds with equality exactly when the vectors are parallel, and δ* is built parallel to θ. Intuitively the adversary's best move is to spend the whole budget pushing along the direction the predictor is most responsive to; any component orthogonal to θ contributes nothing while still consuming budget.

Purpose: ρ is a property of the world, not under our control, so the only way to reduce sensitivity is to make ‖θ‖2 small — this derives the sum-square regularizer rather than assuming it.

Roman Urdu

SETUP. x -> x + delta hone par prediction mein tabdeeli: theta^T(x + delta) - theta^T x = theta^T delta (x kat jaata hai) S = { ||delta||_2 <= rho } mein | theta^T delta | ka maximum QADAM 1 - UPPER BOUND (Cauchy-Schwarz YAHAN lagta hai): | theta^T delta | <= ||theta||_2 ||delta||_2 <= ||theta||_2 * rho to S <= rho ||theta||_2 QADAM 2 - HAD WAQAI HAASIL HOTI HAI. delta* = rho * theta / ||theta||_2 lein: qanooni hai? ||delta*||_2 = rho ||theta||_2 / ||theta||_2 = rho theek value? theta^T delta* = rho (theta^T theta)/||theta||_2 = rho ||theta||_2 theek => S = rho * ||theta||_2 SABIT

Had kyun haasil hoti hai: Cauchy-Schwarz mein barabari theek us waqt hoti hai jab dono vectors ek hi direction mein hon, aur hum ne δ* jaan boojh kar θ ke saath saath banaya. Aqal ki baat: dushman ka behtareen daao ye hai ke poora budget usi taraf lagaye jahan predictor sab se zyada hilta hai; θ ke aamood (orthogonal) hissa kuch nahi deta magar budget khaa jaata hai.

Maqsad: ρ duniya ki cheez hai, hamare haath mein nahi — to sensitivity kam karne ka ek hi rasta hai: ‖θ‖2 chhota karo. Yaani sum-square regularizer yahan se nikla hai, bagair wajah maana nahi gaya.

P-7.36 marks

A ridge model includes the offset in the penalty. House prices average ~8,000,000 rupees, and predictions come out systematically far too low. Explain the mechanism and the correct treatment of the offset.

Sawal ka matlab: Ridge model mein offset bhi penalty ke andar daal diya gaya. Gharon ki keemat ka average taqreeban 80 lakh hai, aur predictions ba-qaida bohat kam aa rahi hain. Ye kyun hua, aur offset ka theek sulook kya hai?

Jawab

English

Mechanism: regularizing the offset pulls all predictions toward zero. The offset θ1 must be ~8,000,000 to place predictions in range, so the penalty contains

lambda * theta_1^2 ~ lambda * (8,000,000)^2 = lambda * 6.4 x 10^13

This single term dwarfs everything else, so RERM buys an enormous penalty reduction by shrinking θ1 drastically, accepting a large rise in squared error as a bargain. The offset collapses toward 0, so every prediction is dragged toward 0 — the stated symptom.

Correct: do not regularize θ1 — start the penalty sum at j = 2.

Justification: the constant feature never changes, so δ1 = 0 always, and in θTδ = θ1·0 + θ2δ2 + … the offset contributes nothing to sensitivity, however large. Penalising it serves none of regularization's purpose and only adds bias.

Also: centre the target and standardize the features; then the required offset is near zero anyway.

Roman Urdu

Wajah: offset ko regularize karna saari predictions ko zero ki taraf kheench leta hai. Offset θ1 ko taqreeban 80,00,000 hona padta hai taake predictions sahi range mein aayein, to penalty mein ye term aa jaata hai:

lambda * theta_1^2 ~ lambda * (80,00,000)^2 = lambda * 6.4 x 10^13

Ye akela term baqi sab par bhaari pad jaata hai, to RERM θ1 ko bohat chhota kar ke penalty mein zabardast faida uthata hai aur badle mein squared error ka bara nuqsan qubool kar leta hai. Offset 0 ki taraf gir jaata hai, to har prediction 0 ki taraf kheench jaati hai — bilkul wohi shikayat.

Theek: θ1 ko regularize na karein — penalty ka jama j = 2 se shuru karein.

Daleel: constant feature kabhi badalta nahi, to δ1 = 0 hamesha; aur θTδ = θ1·0 + θ2δ2 + … mein offset sensitivity mein kuch hissa daalta hi nahi, chahe wo kitna bara ho. Us par jurmana regularization ka koi maqsad poora nahi karta, sirf bias barhata hai.

Aur: target ko centre kar dein aur features standardize karein; phir zaroori offset khud-ba-khud 0 ke qareeb aa jaata hai.

P-7.46 marks

A model of lifespan from healthy-behaviour scores gives a negative coefficient to daily exercise. The sample is small. Which regularizer do the lectures provide, what does it impose, and why does clipping negative coefficients to zero work poorly?

Sawal ka matlab: Sehat ke scores se zindagi ki tawaqqo ka model, aur usne rozana exercise ko negative coefficient de diya (yaani "exercise se umar kam hoti hai"). Sample chhota hai. Lectures kaunsa regularizer dete hain, wo kya pabandi lagata hai, aur negative coefficients ko zero kar dene wala aasaan tareeqa kyun bura kaam karta hai?

Jawab

English

The nonnegative regularizer: φ(z) = 0 if z ≥ 0, ∞ if z < 0. With square loss this is nonnegative least squares (NNLS).

What it imposes: not really a penalty but a hard constraint written as one — "when xj increases, so must our prediction", i.e. the model is forced to be monotonically non-decreasing. It cannot assert that exercise shortens life.

Why the negative sign appeared: with a small sample and correlated behaviour scores the fit is under-determined, so noise easily produces a sign reversal. This is the prior information role of a regularizer.

NNLS loss = 0.59 LS loss = 0.30 heuristic (LS then clip) = 15.05 heuristic is 25x worse than NNLS and 50x worse than LS

Why clipping fails: least squares chooses all coefficients jointly, and with correlated features a negative coefficient is often offsetting a large positive one. Zeroing it unilaterally destroys that balance while every other coefficient stays at a now-wrong value — nothing re-optimizes. The fix is to put the constraint inside the optimization, so all coefficients are fitted simultaneously subject to nonnegativity.

Roman Urdu

Nonnegative regularizer: φ(z) = 0 agar z ≥ 0, aur ∞ agar z < 0. Square loss ke saath isay nonnegative least squares (NNLS) kehte hain.

Kya pabandi lagata hai: ye asal mein jurmana nahi, sakht pabandi hai jo jurmane ki shakal mein likhi gayi — "jab xj barhe to prediction bhi barhni chahiye", yaani model sirf ek taraf (non-decreasing) chalne par majboor hai. Wo ye keh hi nahi sakta ke exercise umar kam karti hai.

Negative nishaan kyun aaya: chhote sample aur ek doosre se jure hue behaviour scores ke saath fit poori tarah tay nahi hota, to shor se nishaan ulat sakta hai. Yehi regularizer ka prior information wala kirdaar hai — hum data se pehle hi jaante hain ke asar negative nahi ho sakta.

NNLS loss = 0.59 LS loss = 0.30 heuristic (LS phir clip) = 15.05 heuristic NNLS se 25 guna bura aur LS se 50 guna bura hai

Clipping kyun fail hoti hai: least squares saare coefficients mila kar chunta hai, aur jure hue features ke saath ek negative coefficient aksar kisi bade positive ka tawaazun (balance) kar raha hota hai. Usay akele zero kar dene se wo tawaazun toot jaata hai, jabke baqi saare coefficients apni purani (ab ghalat) values par baithe rehte hain — kuch dobara optimize nahi hota. Theek tareeqa ye hai ke pabandi ko optimization ke andar rakha jaaye, taake saare coefficients ek saath, pabandi ko maante hue fit hon.

P-7.56 marks

For ridge, lasso and the ℓ0.5 regularizer, compute the derivative of the per-coefficient penalty at θj = 1, 0.25, 0.01. Rank them by sparsifying strength and state the price paid by the strongest.

Sawal ka matlab: Ridge, lasso aur ℓ0.5 ke liye penalty ka derivative θj = 1, 0.25, 0.01 par nikaalein. Phir unko sparsity ki taqat ke hisaab se tarteeb dein, aur batayein sab se taqatwar wala kis qeemat par aata hai.

Jawab

English

derivatives: ridge 2z lasso 1 L_0.5 0.5/sqrt(z) theta_j ridge lasso L_0.5 ------- -------- ------- -------- 1.00 2.00 1.00 0.50 0.25 0.50 1.00 1.00 0.01 0.02 1.00 5.00 trend: DECREASES CONSTANT INCREASES to zero without bound

Reading it: the derivative is the force pushing the coefficient to zero, and what matters is its behaviour as θj → 0.

  • Ridge: the force evaporates (2.00 → 0.02) → no sparsity.
  • Lasso: the force is constant right down to zero, and the kink pins the coefficient there → sparsity.
  • ℓ0.5: the force grows (0.50 → 5.00, and → ∞) → strong sparsity.

Ranking: ℓ0.5 > lasso > ridge (which gives none).

Price paid by ℓ0.5: it is not convex — the very growing derivative that sparsifies means the penalty curves the wrong way. So ERM may have multiple local minima, the result depends on initialisation, and there is no optimality certificate (the lectures call it heuristic). Also (Σ|θj|0.5)² is not even a norm.

Why lasso is the practical choice: ℓ1 is the smallest power still convex and the largest power still sparsifying.

Roman Urdu

derivatives: ridge 2z lasso 1 L_0.5 0.5/sqrt(z) theta_j ridge lasso L_0.5 ------- -------- ------- -------- 1.00 2.00 1.00 0.50 0.25 0.50 1.00 1.00 0.01 0.02 1.00 5.00 rujhaan: GHATTA HAI CONSTANT BARHTA HAI zero tak bay-had

Table padhne ka tareeqa: derivative wo zor hai jo coefficient ko zero ki taraf dhakel raha hai, aur asal cheez ye hai ke θj → 0 hote hue us zor ka kya haal hota hai.

  • Ridge: zor khatam ho jaata hai (2.00 → 0.02) → koi sparsity nahi.
  • Lasso: zor zero tak constant rehta hai, aur kink coefficient ko waheen pakad leta hai → sparsity.
  • ℓ0.5: zor barhta jaata hai (0.50 → 5.00, aur → ∞) → bohat zyada sparsity.

Tarteeb: ℓ0.5 > lasso > ridge (jo bilkul nahi deta).

ℓ0.5 ki qeemat: wo convex nahi hai — wohi barhta hua derivative jo sparsity deta hai, wohi penalty ko ulti taraf moud deta hai. To ERM mein kai local minima ho sakte hain, jawab shuruati guess par depend karta hai, aur optimality ka koi saboot nahi (lectures isay heuristic kehte hain). Aur (Σ|θj|0.5)² norm bhi nahi hai.

Lasso kyun amali chunaav hai: ℓ1 wo sab se chhoti power hai jo phir bhi convex hai, aur wo sab se badi power hai jo phir bhi sparsity deti hai.

P-7.66 marks

60 features, ~50 strongly correlated with one another and all genuinely relevant. Lasso selects an apparently arbitrary 12, and the selection changes on resampling. Explain, and state what to use instead.

Sawal ka matlab: 60 features hain, un mein se ~50 ek doosre se bohat jure hue hain aur sab waqai kaam ke hain. Lasso un mein se bay-tarteeb 12 chun leta hai, aur data dobara sample karne par chunaav badal jaata hai. Wajah batayein, aur iski jagah kya use karein.

Jawab

English

Why lasso does this: when features carry nearly the same information, the empirical risk barely cares which one gets the weight — but the penalties differ:

Total weight 1.0 across two near-identical features A and B: L1 : (1.0, 0.0) -> 1.0 (0.5, 0.5) -> 1.0 EQUAL L2 : (1.0, 0.0) -> 1.0 (0.5, 0.5) -> 0.50 SPLITTING CHEAPER

So ℓ1 is indifferent between concentrating and splitting while ℓ2 strictly prefers splitting. Lasso therefore picks one representative and zeroes the rest — and which one is decided by noise-level differences, so the selection is unstable.

Two clarifications: predictive accuracy may be fine, since the selected feature proxies for the group — the failure is one of interpretation. And since all 50 are genuinely relevant, sparsity is undesirable here — the opposite of OHT Q-7.

Use instead: ridge if all 50 are truly relevant — it distributes weight across correlated features, which is stabler. Elastic net (ℓ1 + ℓ2) if some sparsity is still wanted: the ℓ1 term drops irrelevant features while the ℓ2 term keeps correlated relevant ones together (the grouping effect).

Rule: many irrelevant features → lasso. Many relevant, correlated → ridge. Both → elastic net.

Roman Urdu

Lasso aisa kyun karta hai: jab features taqreeban ek hi information rakhte hon, to empirical risk ko farq hi nahi padta ke weight kis ko mile — magar penalties mein farq hai:

Do taqreeban ek jaise features A aur B par kul weight 1.0 baantein: L1 : (1.0, 0.0) -> 1.0 (0.5, 0.5) -> 1.0 BARABAR L2 : (1.0, 0.0) -> 1.0 (0.5, 0.5) -> 0.50 BAANTNA SASTA

Yaani ℓ1 ko ek jagah jama karne aur baantne mein koi farq nahi, jabke ℓ2 baantne ko saaf tarjeeh deta hai. Is liye lasso ek numainda chun kar baqi zero kar deta hai — aur kaunsa chunta hai, ye shor ke barabar ke mamooli faraq tay karte hain, is liye chunaav be-aitbaar (unstable) rehta hai.

Do baatein saaf karein: prediction ki accuracy theek bhi ho sakti hai, kyunki chuna hua feature poore group ki numaindagi kar deta hai — kharabi samajhne/bayaan karne mein hai. Aur kyunki saare 50 waqai kaam ke hain, yahan sparsity mat-loob nahi — ye OHT Q-7 ka ulta case hai.

Iski jagah: agar saare 50 waqai kaam ke hain to ridge — wo jure hue features par weight baant deta hai, jo zyada stable hai. Aur agar thodi sparsity bhi chahiye to elastic net (ℓ1 + ℓ2): ℓ1 bay-kaar features nikaal deta hai aur ℓ2 jure hue kaam ke features ko sath rakhta hai (grouping effect).

Qanoon: bohat se bay-kaar features → lasso. Bohat se kaam ke, jure hue → ridge. Dono → elastic net.

P-7.76 marks

The lectures advise choosing λ to minimise test error "and maybe a little larger…". Explain the three distinct reasons for taking λ larger than the apparent minimiser.

Sawal ka matlab: Lectures kehte hain λ wo chunein jo test error kam kare "aur shayad thoda bara…". Wo teen alag alag wajuhat batayein ke λ ko minimum se bara kyun lena chahiye.

Jawab

English

1. The validation curve is itself noisy. Each point is estimated from a finite sample, so taking the arg min over many candidates selects partly for favourable noise at that λ. Near the bottom the curve is nearly flat, so a whole range is statistically indistinguishable. Choosing on the larger side makes it robust. (Formalised as the one standard error rule.)

2. Larger λ gives a less sensitive predictor. λ controls ‖θ‖, which controls worst-case sensitivity — and "insensitive predictors often generalize well". The validation set measures today's distribution, but deployment brings feature noise and mild drift it never exhibited.

3. Larger λ gives a simpler, sparser model. With lasso it zeroes more coefficients. If λ can be raised with no meaningful accuracy loss you gain: fewer features to collect and maintain, a model that can be explained and audited, and lower variance.

Caution: this is bounded, not licence to over-regularize — push far beyond and θ → 0, leaving only the offset (underfitting).

Roman Urdu

1. Validation curve khud shor-aalood hai. Us ka har point mehdood sample se naapa gaya hai, to kai candidates mein se arg min uthane ka matlab hai ke aap ne thoda us λ par acha ittefaq bhi chun liya. Curve ke neeche wala hissa taqreeban flat hota hai, to ek poori range statistically ek jaisi hoti hai. Bari taraf se chunne par faisla mazboot ho jaata hai. (Isi ko one standard error rule kehte hain.)

2. Bara λ kam sensitive predictor deta hai. λ ‖θ‖ ko qaabu karta hai, aur ‖θ‖ worst-case sensitivity ko — aur "insensitive predictors aksar acha generalize karte hain". Validation set aaj ki distribution naapta hai, magar deployment mein feature ka shor aur halki drift aati hai jo us set mein mojood hi nahi thi.

3. Bara λ saada aur zyada sparse model deta hai. Lasso ke saath wo zyada coefficients zero kar deta hai. Agar accuracy mein koi maani-khez kami ke bagair λ barh sakta hai to faida: kam features jama karne aur sambhalne padte hain, model samjhaya aur audit kiya ja sakta hai, aur variance kam hota hai.

Ehtiyaat: ye ek mehdood tabdeeli hai, over-regularize karne ki ijaazat nahi — bohat aage barh jaayein to θ → 0 ho jaata hai aur sirf offset bachta hai (underfitting).

Aakhri imtihaan

Mock OHT-1 — asli format mein

60 minute ki ghadi lagayein, kitaab band karein. 10 sawal, 6 marks har ek, kul 60. Jawab chaar line mein.

M-16 marks

A music service has 40 million unlabelled listening logs and wants to group users by taste. A second team wants to predict, per user, cancellation next month, using 3 years of labelled records. State the learning type for each, and explain why a random train/test split is acceptable for one but not the other.

Sawal ka matlab: Music service ke paas 4 crore bagair-label logs hain aur wo users ko pasand ke hisaab se groups mein baantna chahti hai. Doosri team 3 saal ke labelled records se andaza lagana chahti hai ke kaun agle mahine subscription chhod dega. Dono ka learning type batayein, aur samjhayein ke random split ek ke liye theek kyun hai aur doosre ke liye nahi.

Jawab

English

Task 1: unsupervised (clustering) — no labels, output hidden structure. Task 2: supervised binary classification, specifically forecasting, since a future value is predicted from past ones.

Random split: unacceptable for Task 2. The deployment question is "what happens next month", but a random split interleaves test months among training months, so the model sees data from after each test point — look-ahead bias / leakage. Use temporal (forward-chaining) validation.

For Task 1 there is no target to predict, so the framing is less central — but if cluster stability is evaluated, split by user, since multiple logs per user would otherwise leak (identity leakage, broken independence).

Roman Urdu

Kaam 1: unsupervised (clustering) — koi label nahi, maqsad chhupi structure nikalna. Kaam 2: supervised binary classification, aur khaas tor par forecasting, kyunki guzre waqt se aane wale waqt ka andaza lagaya ja raha hai.

Random split: Kaam 2 ke liye bilkul na-munasib. Deployment ka sawal hai "agle mahine kya hoga", magar random split test ke mahinon ko training ke mahinon ke beech bo deta hai, to model har test point ke baad ka data bhi dekh leta hai — look-ahead bias / leakage. Temporal (forward-chaining) validation use karein.

Kaam 1 mein koi target hi nahi, to split ka sawal itna markazi nahi — magar agar clusters ki stability naapni ho to split user ke hisaab se karein, warna ek user ke kai logs split ke dono taraf chale jaayenge (identity leakage, independence toot gaya).

M-26 marks

A warehouse predicts item pick-time from 4 raw features. It systematically under-predicts for heavy items on high shelves, but predicts well for heavy items on low shelves and light items on high shelves. Identify the problem, write the corrected feature map, and state what property is preserved.

Sawal ka matlab: Warehouse 4 raw features se item uthane ka waqt predict karta hai. Jo cheezein bhaari hain aur oonchi shelf par hain, un ke liye waqt ba-qaida kam batata hai — magar bhaari+neechi aur halki+oonchi, dono theek. Masla pehchanein, theek feature map likhein, aur batayein kaunsi khoobi barqaraar rehti hai.

Jawab

English

Problem: a missing interaction — underfitting from an inadequate feature map. The model is additive, so weight and height each contribute a fixed independent amount. But only the combination is mispredicted, which means the effect of weight depends on height — something an additive function cannot represent. The systematic residual confirms underfitting.

phi(u) = ( 1 , weight , height , weight x height )

The interaction gives the weight-effect a second slope that applies only at height: slope in weight becomes θ2 low down and θ2 + θ4·height higher up.

Property preserved: linear in θ — the product is just another column, so the risk stays convex and least squares applies unchanged.

Roman Urdu

Masla: interaction missing hai — na-kaafi feature map ki wajah se underfitting. Model additive hai, to wazan aur bulandi dono apna apna pakka hissa daalte hain. Magar ghalti sirf un ke milaap par ho rahi hai, jiska matlab wazan ka asar bulandi par depend karta hai — aur additive function ye bayaan nahi kar sakta. Ba-qaida residual underfitting ki tasdeeq karta hai.

phi(u) = ( 1 , weight , height , weight x height )

Interaction term wazan ke asar ko ek doosri dhalaan de deta hai jo sirf bulandi par lagti hai: wazan ki dhalaan neeche θ2 aur oopar θ2 + θ4·height ban jaati hai.

Barqaraar khoobi: θ mein linear — zarb (product) sirf ek naya column hai, to risk convex rehta hai aur least squares bagair tabdeeli chalta hai.

M-36 marks

Given y = (5, 8, 8, 11, 38) minutes, compute the optimal constant under square loss and absolute loss. Which would you quote to a customer, and why?

Sawal ka matlab: y = (5, 8, 8, 11, 38) minute ke liye square loss aur absolute loss ka behtareen constant nikaalein. Customer ko kaunsa waqt batayenge, aur kyun?

Jawab

English

SQUARE -> MEAN = (5+8+8+11+38)/5 = 70/5 = 14 ABSOLUTE -> MEDIAN: n=5 odd, median = y_3 = 8 check n_1 = #{y<8} = 1 -> 0.2 <= 0.5 ok ; n_2 = #{y>8} = 2 -> 0.4 <= 0.5 ok

Observation: the mean (14) lies above four of the five points, dragged up by the single 38; the median (8) sits inside the cluster. Moving one value by 24 shifts the mean by 24/5 = 4.8 and the median not at all.

Which to quote: of the two, the median (8) better describes typical performance, being robust. But for a customer promise neither is right — quoting 8 means about half the deliveries are late. The correct tool is tilted absolute loss with τ > ½, whose optimum is the τ-quantile: here the 0.75-quantile is 11 minutes (0.6 ≤ 0.75 ≤ 0.8 ✓). Quote 11.

Roman Urdu

SQUARE -> MEAN = (5+8+8+11+38)/5 = 70/5 = 14 ABSOLUTE -> MEDIAN: n=5 taaq, median = y_3 = 8 check n_1 = #{y<8} = 1 -> 0.2 <= 0.5 theek ; n_2 = #{y>8} = 2 -> 0.4 <= 0.5 theek

Ghaur karein: mean (14) paanch mein se chaar points se oopar hai — usay akele 38 ne oopar kheench liya; median (8) jhund ke andar baitha hai. Ek value 24 badalne par mean 24/5 = 4.8 khisakta hai aur median bilkul nahi.

Customer ko kya batayein: in do mein se median (8) aam performance ko behtar bayaan karta hai kyunki wo robust hai. Magar customer se wade ke liye dono hi theek nahi — 8 batane ka matlab taqreeban aadhi deliveries late. Theek hathiyar tilted absolute loss, τ > ½ hai, jiska jawab τ-quantile hota hai: yahan 0.75-quantile 11 minute hai (0.6 ≤ 0.75 ≤ 0.8 ✓). To 11 batayein.

M-46 marks

A vibration monitor predicts bearing wear. A loose cable produced 7 wildly wrong readings out of 900, and the team used squared loss. Explain the poor fit using the penalty derivative, and recommend a loss, justifying it over the alternatives.

Sawal ka matlab: Vibration monitor bearing ki ghisaawat predict karta hai. Ek dheeli cable ki wajah se 900 mein se 7 readings bilkul ghalat aa gayin, aur team ne squared loss use kiya. Derivative ke zariye samjhayein ke fit kyun bura hua, aur ek loss tajweez karein — baqi options par usay tarjeeh dene ki wajah ke saath.

Jawab

English

Mechanism: under square loss each point's gradient pull is p′(r) = 2r, which grows without bound. A reading wrong by a factor of 100 contributes 10,000× the loss and 100× the pull, so although the 7 bad readings are under 0.8% of the data they can dominate the average, and ERM shifts θ toward them — degrading all 893 valid readings.

Recommend Huber: r² for |r| ≤ α, α(2|r| − α) beyond. The derivative saturates at 2α, so each corrupted reading exerts a bounded pull however extreme, while the 893 good readings keep the efficient quadratic treatment. α by validation, ~1–2× the MAD.
